# 2.9.4 Model

- [WASA: WAtermark-based Source Attribution for Large Language Model-Generated Data](https://arxiv.org/abs/2310.00646): Embeds provider-specific watermarks during LLM generation so source attribution can identify which training-data contributor influenced generated text under adversarial conditions.
- [Prompting Fairness: Learning Prompts for Debiasing Large Language Models](https://doi.org/10.18653/v1/2024.ltedi-1.6): Learns prompts for debiasing LLM outputs, treating fairness improvement as a model-adaptation and prompt-learning problem.
- [TMFD: Two-Stage Meta-learning Feature Disentanglement Framework for DeepFake Detection](https://doi.org/10.1109/ijcb62174.2024.10744435): Uses two-stage meta-learning and feature disentanglement for DeepFake detection, adding a model-side misuse-detection method.
- [The Art of Deception: Robust Backdoor Attack using Dynamic Stacking of Triggers](https://arxiv.org/abs/2401.01537): Introduces DynamicTrigger, an audio backdoor that varies signal sampling rates and masks speaker identity with triggers such as claps to compromise ASR while preserving clean accuracy.
- [A Mechanistic Understanding of Alignment Algorithms: A Case Study on DPO and Toxicity](https://arxiv.org/abs/2401.01967): Shows DPO reduces toxicity by bypassing rather than removing pretrained toxic capabilities, then demonstrates an unalignment route that restores the suppressed behavior.
- [MLLM-Protector: Ensuring MLLM’s Safety without Hurting Performance](https://arxiv.org/abs/2401.02906): Defends MLLMs against malicious visual inputs with a plug-and-play harm detector and detoxifier that identify harmful responses and rewrite them without retraining the base model.
- [The Butterfly Effect of Altering Prompts: How Small Changes and Jailbreaks Affect Large Language Model Performance](https://arxiv.org/abs/2401.03729): Quantifies prompt sensitivity in LLM data labeling, showing tiny formatting changes, XML requests, and jailbreak-style wording can flip classification decisions.
- [Enhanced Generative Adversarial Network with Leaked Information (E-LeakGAN): A deep learning framework for synthesizing computational creativity in scientific domain](https://doi.org/10.1016/j.asoc.2025.114291): Introduces an enhanced GAN with leaked information for synthesizing computational-creativity samples in scientific domains.
- [Enhancing autonomous driving safety in real lane-changing scenarios under friction variability: A friction-adaptive shield reinforcement learning framework.](https://doi.org/10.1016/j.aap.2025.108265): Uses friction-adaptive shield reinforcement learning for safer lane-changing behavior under variable road friction.
- [VLM-RL: A unified vision language models and reinforcement learning framework for safe autonomous driving](https://doi.org/10.1016/j.trc.2025.105321): Combines vision-language models with reinforcement learning for safe autonomous-driving decisions.
- [Sleeper Agents: Training Deceptive LLMs that Persist Through Safety Training](https://arxiv.org/abs/2401.05566): Trains proof-of-concept deceptive LLM policies whose backdoor behavior persists through supervised fine-tuning, reinforcement learning, and adversarial safety training.
- [TOFU: A Task of Fictitious Unlearning for LLMs](https://arxiv.org/abs/2401.06121): Defines an unlearning benchmark with synthetic author profiles, forget sets, metrics, and baselines for testing whether LLMs truly remove memorized facts.
- [Scissorhands: Scrub Data Influence via Connection Sensitivity in Networks](https://arxiv.org/abs/2401.06187): Removes targeted data influence by finding connection-sensitive parameters, reinitializing the most influential weights, and fine-tuning with gradient projection to preserve retained data.
- [Intention Analysis Makes LLMs A Good Jailbreak Defender](https://arxiv.org/abs/2401.06561): Uses a two-stage intention-analysis prompt that first infers the user's underlying intent and then conditions the final answer on policy-aligned intent reasoning at inference time.
- [Few-Shot Detection of Machine-Generated Text using Style Representations](https://arxiv.org/abs/2401.06712): Detects machine-generated text in few-shot settings by using human style representations rather than training on samples from the suspect generator.
- [Signed-Prompt: A New Approach to Prevent Prompt Injection Attacks Against LLM-Integrated Applications](https://arxiv.org/abs/2401.07612): Separates trusted instructions from untrusted content by cryptographically signed command segments, with prompt-engineering and fine-tuning variants for prompt-injection defense.
- [Generative Steganography Based on Long Readable Text Generation](https://doi.org/10.1109/tcss.2022.3174013): Uses a plug-and-play language model and an embeddable candidate word pool to hide messages in topic-consistent long text while preserving readability and extraction accuracy.
- [Large Language Models Portray Socially Subordinate Groups as More Homogeneous, Consistent with a Bias Observed in Humans](https://arxiv.org/abs/2401.08495): Measures homogenization bias in ChatGPT generations, finding narrower portrayals of socially subordinate racial and gender groups than dominant groups.
- [PVASS-MDD: Predictive Visual-Audio Alignment Self-Supervision for Multimodal Deepfake Detection](https://doi.org/10.1109/tcsvt.2023.3309899): Learns predictive visual-audio alignment with a three-stream self-supervised stage, then detects deepfakes from forged visual-audio inconsistencies.
- [Low-Quality Deepfake Detection via Unseen Artifacts](https://doi.org/10.1109/tai.2023.3299894): Detects compressed low-quality fake media with a learned visibility matrix that exposes imperceptible artifacts lost under social-media compression.
- [NTD: Non-Transferability Enabled Deep Learning Backdoor Detection](https://doi.org/10.1109/tifs.2023.3312973): Uses the non-transferability of triggers across models as a runtime backdoor detector, comparing feature-space similarity between a suspect input and samples from its predicted class.
- [All in How You Ask for It: Simple Black-Box Method for Jailbreak Attacks](https://arxiv.org/abs/2401.09798): Builds a black-box jailbreak procedure that asks the target model to iteratively paraphrase harmful requests into benign-looking prompts, reaching high attack success in a few iterations.
- [Exposing Lip-syncing Deepfakes from Mouth Inconsistencies](https://arxiv.org/abs/2401.10113): Detects lip-sync deepfakes with LIPINC, a temporal mouth-inconsistency model focused on adjacent-frame irregularities in the mouth region.
- [Pruning for Protection: Increasing Jailbreak Resistance in Aligned LLMs Without Fine-Tuning](https://arxiv.org/abs/2401.10862): Shows moderate WANDA pruning can improve jailbreak resistance without fine-tuning by sharpening attention and increasing sensitivity to artificial jailbreak constructs.
- [The Ethics of Interaction: Mitigating Security Threats in LLMs](https://arxiv.org/abs/2401.12273): Surveys five LLM security threat classes and proposes an ethics-oriented evaluative tool for prompt injection, jailbreaks, PII exposure, explicit content, and hate-based content.
- [Red Teaming Visual Language Models](https://arxiv.org/abs/2401.12915): Introduces RTVLM, a red-teaming dataset with visual-language subtasks across faithfulness, privacy, safety, and fairness for evaluating harmful or inaccurate VLM behavior.
- [Adaptive Text Watermark for Large Language Models](https://arxiv.org/abs/2401.13927): Applies watermarking only to high-entropy token distributions measured by an auxiliary model, preserving low-entropy text quality while supporting prompt-agnostic detection.
- [Black-Box Access is Insufficient for Rigorous AI Audits](https://arxiv.org/abs/2401.14446): Argues black-box audits miss risks visible under white-box and deployment-context access, motivating stronger protocols with weights, activations, code, data, and deployment details.
- [AI auditing: The Broken Bus on the Road to AI Accountability](https://arxiv.org/abs/2401.14462): Taxonomizes AI audit practices across regulators, civil society, academia, journalism, consulting, and law, then identifies which audit designs translate into accountability outcomes.
- [Unlearning Traces the Influential Training Data of Language Models](https://arxiv.org/abs/2401.15241): Proposes UnTrac, estimating training-dataset influence by unlearning a dataset with gradient ascent or inverse unlearning and measuring prediction changes.
- [Model Supply Chain Poisoning: Backdooring Pre-trained Models via Embedding Indistinguishability](https://arxiv.org/abs/2401.15883): Introduces TransTroj, a task-transferable supply-chain backdoor for pretrained models that keeps triggers effective after downstream fine-tuning.
- [A Cross-Language Investigation into Jailbreak Attacks in Large Language Models](https://arxiv.org/abs/2401.16765): Studies multilingual jailbreaks with a semantic-preserving translation algorithm, showing safety filters can be bypassed by preserving harmful intent across languages.
- [Provably Robust Multi-bit Watermarking for AI-generated Text via Error Correction Code](https://arxiv.org/abs/2401.16820): Combines multi-bit text watermarking with error-correction coding so generated text can carry user identifiers for robust source tracing under perturbations.
- [Robust Prompt Optimization for Defending Language Models Against Jailbreaking Attacks](https://arxiv.org/abs/2401.17263): Optimizes a lightweight transferable defense suffix with Robust Prompt Optimization, directly modeling adaptive jailbreak adversaries in the defensive objective.
- [Prompt-Driven LLM Safeguarding via Directed Representation Optimization](https://arxiv.org/abs/2401.18018): Analyzes safety prompts in representation space and uses Directed Representation Optimization to move harmful queries toward higher-refusal directions while limiting over-refusal.
- [Machine Unlearning for Image-to-Image Generative Models](https://arxiv.org/abs/2402.00351): Extends machine unlearning to image-to-image generative models with an efficient objective that removes forget-sample influence while preserving retained-sample generation.
- [Safety of Multimodal Large Language Models on Images and Text](https://arxiv.org/abs/2402.00357): Surveys safety evaluation, attacks, and defenses for MLLMs on image-text inputs, including datasets, metrics, jailbreaks, and mitigation approaches.
- [Fast Exact Unlearning for In-Context Learning Data for LLMs](https://arxiv.org/abs/2402.00751): Achieves exact unlearning for LLM adaptation data by replacing gradient fine-tuning with in-context learning and deleting the relevant demonstration examples.
- [Vaccine: Perturbation-aware Alignment for Large Language Model](https://arxiv.org/abs/2402.01109): Introduces perturbation-aware alignment that trains hidden embeddings to remain invariant to crafted harmful fine-tuning perturbations.
- [An Information Theoretic Approach to Machine Unlearning](https://arxiv.org/abs/2402.01401): Derives a zero-shot unlearning rule from information gain, scaling the removal of forgotten samples without retraining while preserving model utility.
- [On Catastrophic Inheritance of Large Foundation Models](https://arxiv.org/abs/2402.01909): Frames catastrophic inheritance as downstream safety failures inherited from biased, noisy, corrupted, or out-of-distribution pretraining data in large foundation models.
- [Data Poisoning for In-context Learning](https://arxiv.org/abs/2402.02160): Introduces ICLPoison, a discrete-text poisoning attack that manipulates in-context examples to steer hidden states and degrade LLM predictions without fine-tuning.
- [Safety Fine-Tuning at (Almost) No Cost: A Baseline for Vision Large Language Models](https://arxiv.org/abs/2402.02207): Curates VLGuard safe instruction data and uses it in standard or post-hoc VLLM fine-tuning to reduce harmful responses and simple jailbreak vulnerability.
- [DisDet: Exploring Detectability of Backdoor Attack on Diffusion Models](https://arxiv.org/abs/2402.02739): Studies poisoned-noise detectability in backdoored diffusion models and builds DisDet to expose trigger-bearing inputs before generation.
- [C-RAG: Certified Generation Risks for Retrieval-Augmented Language Models](https://arxiv.org/abs/2402.03181): Certifies generation risk for retrieval-augmented language models by deriving conditions under which retrieval reduces hallucination or misalignment risk.
- [GUARD: Role-playing to Generate Natural-language Jailbreakings to Test Guideline Adherence of Large Language Models](https://arxiv.org/abs/2402.03299): Generates natural-language jailbreaks with a role-playing system that splits attack design across four LLM personas and recombines jailbreak characteristics.
- [The Instinctive Bias: Spurious Images lead to Hallucination in MLLMs](https://arxiv.org/abs/2402.03757): Introduces CorrelationQA, a 7,308-pair benchmark for measuring visual-illusion hallucinations caused by relevant but answer-inconsistent images.
- [Leak, Cheat, Repeat: Data Contamination and Evaluation Malpractices in Closed-Source LLMs](https://arxiv.org/abs/2402.03927): Systematically analyzes data contamination and indirect leakage in GPT-3.5 and GPT-4 studies, including evaluation practices that make closed-model results unreliable.
- [Risks of AI scientists: prioritizing safeguarding over autonomy](https://arxiv.org/abs/2402.04247): Maps misuse and autonomy risks in LLM-based AI scientists, emphasizing safeguards for user intent, model capability, tool access, and scientific workflow control.
- [Rapid Optimization for Jailbreaking LLMs via Subconscious Exploitation and Echopraxia](https://arxiv.org/abs/2402.05467): Introduces RIPPLE, an optimization-based jailbreak generator inspired by subconscious exploitation and echopraxia that produces transferable, stealthy harmful prompts with low overhead.
- [Buffer Overflow in Mixture of Experts](https://arxiv.org/abs/2402.05526): Shows cross-batch expert routing in mixture-of-experts models can let malicious queries influence benign queries processed in the same batch.
- [Selective Forgetting: Advancing Machine Unlearning Techniques and Evaluation in Language Models](https://arxiv.org/abs/2402.05813): Introduces SeUL for fine-grained language-model unlearning and evaluates forgetting with sensitive extraction likelihood and sensitive memorization accuracy.
- [Permute-and-Flip: An optimally robust and watermarkable decoder for LLMs](https://arxiv.org/abs/2402.05864): Proposes a Permute-and-Flip decoder with a cryptographic watermark that preserves the sampling distribution while improving the quality-stability trade-off.
- [Fight Back Against Jailbreaking via Prompt Adversarial Tuning](https://arxiv.org/abs/2402.06255): Trains a guard-prefix prompt control with adversarial and benign prompts, reducing grey-box and black-box jailbreak success while preserving normal utility.
- [StruQ: Defending Against Prompt Injection with Structured Queries](https://arxiv.org/abs/2402.06363): Separates instructions and data into structured channels, pairing a secure formatter with a fine-tuned LLM trained to ignore instructions inside user data.
- [Shadowcast: Stealthy Data Poisoning Attacks Against Vision-Language Models](https://arxiv.org/abs/2402.06659): Demonstrates stealthy VLM data poisoning with visually indistinguishable image-text samples that induce label or persuasion attacks and transfer across architectures.
- [Debating with More Persuasive LLMs Leads to More Truthful Answers](https://arxiv.org/abs/2402.06782): Uses debate between stronger LLM experts so weaker model or human judges can select more truthful answers without direct access to ground-truth labels.
- [Whispers in the Machine: Confidentiality in LLM-integrated Systems](https://arxiv.org/abs/2402.06922): Formalizes confidentiality risks in tool-using LLM agents where prompt injection through connected services can exfiltrate sensitive data.
- [A Theoretical Analysis of Nash Learning from Human Feedback under General KL-Regularized Preference](https://arxiv.org/abs/2402.07314): Generalizes RLHF beyond reward-based Bradley-Terry assumptions as a reverse-KL-regularized minimax game between competing LLM policies.
- [ODIN: Disentangled Reward Mitigates Hacking in RLHF](https://arxiv.org/abs/2402.07319): Trains a reward model with separate length-correlated and length-decorrelated heads, then discards the length head to reduce response-length reward hacking.
- [Refined Direct Preference Optimization with Synthetic Data for Behavioral Alignment of LLMs](https://arxiv.org/abs/2402.08005): Uses teacher-model self-critique to create synthetic preference data, then applies a generalized DPO loss with an external reward model for safer behavioral alignment.
- [Careless Whisper: Speech-to-Text Hallucination Harms](https://arxiv.org/abs/2402.08021): Evaluates Whisper hallucinations, finding fabricated transcription segments with explicit harms and higher hallucination rates for speech with long non-vocal durations.
- [Test-Time Backdoor Attacks on Multimodal Large Language Models](https://arxiv.org/abs/2402.08577): Introduces AnyDoor, a test-time MLLM backdoor that injects universal adversarial image perturbations to activate harmful effects without poisoning training data.
- [COLD-Attack: Jailbreaking LLMs with Stealthiness and Controllability](https://arxiv.org/abs/2402.08679): Adapts energy-based constrained decoding with Langevin dynamics to generate controllable jailbreak prompts with specified sentiment, style, and coherence.
- [Rethinking machine unlearning for large language models](https://arxiv.org/abs/2402.08787): Reviews LLM unlearning goals, threat models, algorithms, and evaluation criteria for removing undesirable data influence while preserving unrelated capabilities.
- [SafeDecoding: Defending against Jailbreak Attacks via Safety-Aware Decoding](https://arxiv.org/abs/2402.08983): Amplifies safety-disclaimer token probabilities and suppresses jailbreak-aligned token sequences during decoding, reducing harmful completions without extra fine-tuning.
- [Soft Prompt Threats: Attacking Safety Alignment and Unlearning in Open-Source LLMs through the Embedding Space](https://arxiv.org/abs/2402.09063): Attacks open-source LLM safety by optimizing continuous embedding-space soft prompts that bypass alignment and unlearning defenses.
- [Reinforcement Learning from Human Feedback with Active Queries](https://arxiv.org/abs/2402.09401): Formalizes RLHF as contextual dueling bandits and introduces active-query PPO and DPO variants that match DPO performance with fewer preference labels.
- [Towards Privacy-Aware Sign Language Translation at Scale](https://arxiv.org/abs/2402.09611): Combines anonymized self-supervised video pretraining with supervised sign-language translation fine-tuning to reduce biometric privacy exposure in web-scale SLT data.
- [UNDIAL: Self-Distillation with Adjusted Logits for Robust Unlearning in Large Language Models](https://arxiv.org/abs/2402.10052): Uses self-distillation on adjusted logits to suppress targeted token influence while avoiding unstable gradient-ascent unlearning and catastrophic forgetting.
- [Towards Safer Large Language Models through Machine Unlearning](https://arxiv.org/abs/2402.10058): Introduces Selective Knowledge Negation Unlearning, first acquiring harmful knowledge and then negating it in model parameters while preserving normal-prompt utility.
- [Recovering the Pre-Fine-Tuning Weights of Generative Models](https://arxiv.org/abs/2402.10208): Presents Spectral DeTuning, which recovers unsafe pre-fine-tuning weights from multiple LoRA fine-tuned generative models.
- [A StrongREJECT for Empty Jailbreaks](https://arxiv.org/abs/2402.10260): Builds the StrongREJECT jailbreak benchmark and evaluator to measure whether model responses provide specific useful information for forbidden prompts.
- [Active Preference Optimization for Sample Efficient RLHF](https://arxiv.org/abs/2402.10500): Recasts RLHF data selection as contextual preference bandits, showing adaptive context sampling can close uniform-sampling suboptimality under limited preference budgets.
- [When"Competency"in Reasoning Opens the Door to Vulnerability: Jailbreaking LLMs via Novel Complex Ciphers](https://arxiv.org/abs/2402.10601): Introduces ACE and LACE jailbreaks that encode malicious queries in custom single-layer or multi-layer ciphers, exposing a reasoning-safety trade-off.
- [Proving membership in LLM pretraining data via data watermarks](https://arxiv.org/abs/2402.10892): Uses randomized data watermarks and hypothesis testing to prove black-box membership of contributed documents in LLM pretraining data.
- [k-SemStamp: A Clustering-Based Semantic Watermark for Detection of Machine-Generated Text](https://arxiv.org/abs/2402.11399): Replaces locality-sensitive hashing in semantic sentence watermarks with k-means clustering, improving paraphrase robustness and sampling efficiency.
- [Pride and Prejudice: LLM Amplifies Self-Bias in Self-Refinement](https://arxiv.org/abs/2402.11436): Defines self-bias in LLM self-refinement and shows external feedback or larger evaluator models can reduce the tendency to prefer a model's own outputs.
- [Stumbling Blocks: Stress Testing the Robustness of Machine-Generated Text Detectors Under Attacks](https://arxiv.org/abs/2402.11638): Stress-tests generated-text detectors against editing, paraphrasing, prompting, and co-generating attacks, then proposes initial patches for detector robustness gaps.
- [Language Models are Homer Simpson! Safety Re-Alignment of Fine-tuned Language Models through Task Arithmetic](https://arxiv.org/abs/2402.11746): Restores safety after downstream fine-tuning by adding a safety vector to compromised model weights through task arithmetic.
- [ArtPrompt: ASCII Art-based Jailbreak Attacks against Aligned LLMs](https://arxiv.org/abs/2402.11753): Uses ASCII art to hide harmful instructions from semantic-only safety alignment and releases ViTC for evaluating vision-in-text recognition failures.
- [ROSE Doesn't Do That: Boosting the Safety of Instruction-Tuned Large Language Models with Reverse Prompt Contrastive Decoding](https://arxiv.org/abs/2402.11889): Uses reverse-prompt contrastive decoding to suppress unsafe continuations and increase the probability of safe responses without additional training.
- [Where is the answer? Investigating Positional Bias in Language Model Knowledge Extraction](https://arxiv.org/abs/2402.12170): Identifies the perplexity curse in fine-tuned LLM knowledge extraction and shows denoising autoregressive regularization improves recall from middle and late document positions.
- [Robust CLIP: Unsupervised Adversarial Fine-Tuning of Vision Embeddings for Robust Large Vision-Language Models](https://arxiv.org/abs/2402.12336): Adversarially fine-tunes CLIP vision encoders without supervision so downstream LVLMs gain image-attack robustness without retraining the full multimodal model.
- [GumbelSoft: Diversified Language Model Watermarking via the GumbelMax-trick](https://arxiv.org/abs/2402.12948): Introduces Logits-Addition and GumbelSoft watermarks to preserve output diversity while retaining strong detectability for GumbelMax-style LLM watermarks.
- [Prompt Stealing Attacks Against Large Language Models](https://arxiv.org/abs/2402.12959): Studies black-box prompt-stealing queries that recover hidden system instructions, framing instruction leakage as a model-inference security risk.
- [How Easy is It to Fool Your Multimodal LLMs? An Empirical Analysis on Deceptive Prompts](https://arxiv.org/abs/2402.13220): Tests deceptive text and image prompts against MLLMs to measure how easily benign-looking multimodal inputs induce unsafe or incorrect responses.
- [Learning to Poison Large Language Models During Instruction Tuning](https://arxiv.org/abs/2402.13459): Constructs instruction-tuning poisons that implant targeted unsafe behavior while preserving normal task performance after fine-tuning.
- [VL-Trojan: Multimodal Instruction Backdoor Attacks against Autoregressive Visual Language Models](https://arxiv.org/abs/2402.13851): Injects multimodal instruction backdoors into autoregressive VLMs so visual triggers activate attacker-chosen responses after instruction tuning.
- [Can Watermarks Survive Translation? On the Cross-lingual Consistency of Text Watermark for Large Language Models](https://arxiv.org/abs/2402.14007): Tests whether large-language-model text watermarks survive translation across languages.
- [Corrective Machine Unlearning](https://arxiv.org/abs/2402.14015): Proposes a corrective unlearning procedure that removes the influence of identified harmful or erroneous training examples without retraining from scratch.
- [Coercing LLMs to do and reveal (almost) anything](https://arxiv.org/abs/2402.14020): Demonstrates coercion prompts that make LLMs both perform restricted actions and reveal hidden information, exposing refusal and confidentiality failure modes.
- [Stick to your role! Stability of personal values expressed in large language models](https://arxiv.org/abs/2402.14846): Measures whether LLM-expressed personal values remain stable across assigned roles and dialogue contexts, treating value drift as an alignment risk.
- [Is the System Message Really Important to Jailbreaks in Large Language Models?](https://arxiv.org/abs/2402.14857): Ablates system-message placement and content to show how jailbreak robustness changes when the hidden instruction channel is weakened or contradicted.
- [The Wolf Within: Covert Injection of Malice into MLLM Societies via an MLLM Operative](https://arxiv.org/abs/2402.14859): Models covert MLLM-society compromise where one manipulated operative agent propagates harmful prompts, instructions, or misinformation through inter-agent communication.
- [Stop Reasoning! When Multimodal LLM with Chain-of-Thought Reasoning Meets Adversarial Image](https://arxiv.org/abs/2402.14899): Shows adversarial images can disrupt multimodal chain-of-thought reasoning and push MLLMs toward unsafe or wrong answers.
- [Watermarking Makes Language Models Radioactive](https://arxiv.org/abs/2402.14904): Detects whether downstream language models were trained on watermarked synthetic text by measuring residual watermark signals after fine-tuning.
- [Machine Unlearning of Pre-trained Large Language Models](https://arxiv.org/abs/2402.15159): Introduces a model-side method, architecture, training recipe, or data strategy for safety, alignment, watermarking, unlearning, or attack-defense capability.
- [Break the Breakout: Reinventing LM Defense Against Jailbreak Attacks with Self-Refinement](https://arxiv.org/abs/2402.15180): Uses model self-refinement to detect and rewrite jailbreak-prone responses before final output, turning the base LLM into its own safety filter.
- [Foot In The Door: Understanding Large Language Model Jailbreaking via Cognitive Psychology](https://arxiv.org/abs/2402.15690): Analyzes jailbreak escalation through the cognitive-psychology foot-in-the-door effect, where small compliant steps lead to later harmful compliance.
- [LLMs Can Defend Themselves Against Jailbreaking in a Practical Manner: A Vision Paper](https://arxiv.org/abs/2402.15727): Argues for self-defense pipelines where an LLM critiques, revises, and refuses unsafe completions without relying on a separate guard model.
- [ASETF: A Novel Method for Jailbreak Attack on LLMs through Translate Suffix Embeddings](https://arxiv.org/abs/2402.16006): Builds jailbreak suffixes in translated embedding space to transfer harmful instructions across language and representation boundaries.
- [No Free Lunch in LLM Watermarking: Trade-offs in Watermarking Design Choices](https://arxiv.org/abs/2402.16187): Compares LLM watermark design choices and shows detectability, robustness, text quality, and generation diversity trade off against one another.
- [Defending Large Language Models against Jailbreak Attacks via Semantic Smoothing](https://arxiv.org/abs/2402.16192): Applies semantic smoothing over paraphrased inputs so jailbreak decisions depend on stable intent rather than a single adversarial wording.
- [Immunization against harmful fine-tuning attacks](https://arxiv.org/abs/2402.16382): Formalizes harmful fine-tuning defense as attacker-budget-dependent "immunization" conditions, specifying what a defense must prove when the defender cannot control later fine-tuning.
- [Defending LLMs against Jailbreaking Attacks via Backtranslation](https://arxiv.org/abs/2402.16459): Translates suspect prompts away from and back into the source language to expose harmful intent and reduce jailbreak success at inference time.
- [Eight Methods to Evaluate Robust Unlearning in LLMs](https://arxiv.org/abs/2402.16835): Defines eight stress tests for LLM unlearning, including relearning, extraction, and retained-utility checks that reveal superficial forgetting.
- [DrAttack: Prompt Decomposition and Reconstruction Makes Powerful LLM Jailbreakers](https://arxiv.org/abs/2402.16914): Decomposes harmful prompts into disguised fragments and reconstructs them inside the model context to bypass safety filters with few queries.
- [Speak Out of Turn: Safety Vulnerability of Large Language Models in Multi-turn Dialogue](https://arxiv.org/abs/2402.17262): Shows multi-turn dialogue can desynchronize safety context so models answer harmful requests after earlier benign turns set up the topic.
- [Token-Specific Watermarking with Enhanced Detectability and Semantic Coherence for Large Language Models](https://arxiv.org/abs/2402.18059): Uses lightweight networks to assign token-specific watermark logits and split ratios, optimizing detectability and semantic quality together during LLM decoding.
- [Making Them Ask and Answer: Jailbreaking Large Language Models in Few Queries via Disguise and Reconstruction](https://arxiv.org/abs/2402.18104): Uses disguise and reconstruction prompts so the target model reformulates and answers a harmful request within only a few interactions.
- [Implicit Optimization Bias of Next-token Prediction in Linear Models](https://arxiv.org/abs/2402.18551): Analyzes next-token prediction in linear models to show how gradient optimization selects particular minimizers, making implicit training bias a measurable source of model behavior.
- [Curiosity-driven Red-teaming for Large Language Models](https://arxiv.org/abs/2402.19464): Guides automated red-teaming with curiosity rewards so attack generation explores novel harmful behaviors instead of repeating known jailbreak patterns.
- [LoRATK: LoRA Once, Backdoor Everywhere in the Share-and-Play Ecosystem](https://arxiv.org/abs/2403.00108): Shows a backdoor LoRA can be trained once and merged without training into multiple task-enhancing LoRAs, hiding malicious behavior behind useful downstream adapters.
- [AXOLOTL: Fairness through Assisted Self-Debiasing of Large Language Model Outputs](https://arxiv.org/abs/2403.00198): Provides a post-processing self-debiasing loop that asks an API-accessed LLM to identify bias, propose a resolution, and revise outputs without parameter access.
- [Enhancing Jailbreak Attacks with Diversity Guidance](https://arxiv.org/abs/2403.00292): Introduces DPP-based Stochastic Trigger Searching, using stochastic gradient search and determinantal-point-process selection to diversify jailbreak trigger optimization.
- [Provably Robust DPO: Aligning Language Models with Noisy Feedback](https://arxiv.org/abs/2403.00409): Develops robust DPO for aligning language models under noisy feedback.
- [Cognitive Bias in Decision-Making with LLMs](https://arxiv.org/abs/2403.00811): Measures cognitive biases in LLM decisions, showing how framing and heuristic effects can distort safety-critical recommendations.
- [LAB: Large-Scale Alignment for ChatBots](https://arxiv.org/abs/2403.01081): Presents a large-scale chatbot alignment recipe that combines synthetic instruction data and preference-style tuning for safer assistant behavior.
- [Dissecting Language Models: Machine Unlearning via Selective Pruning](https://arxiv.org/abs/2403.01267): Uses selective pruning to remove targeted knowledge from language models.
- [GuardT2I: Defending Text-to-Image Models from Adversarial Prompts](https://arxiv.org/abs/2403.01446): Trains a guard for text-to-image generation that detects adversarial prompts and blocks unsafe image synthesis before decoding.
- [Differentially Private Synthetic Data via Foundation Model APIs 2: Text](https://arxiv.org/abs/2403.01749): Generates differentially private synthetic text through foundation-model APIs while accounting for privacy leakage from prompt and output sampling.
- [Enhancing LLM Safety via Constrained Direct Preference Optimization](https://arxiv.org/abs/2403.02475): Adds safety constraints to DPO so preference optimization improves harmlessness without over-optimizing against useful responses.
- [Splat-Nav: Safe Real-Time Robot Navigation in Gaussian Splatting Maps](https://arxiv.org/abs/2403.02751): Uses Gaussian-splatting scene maps for real-time robot navigation with explicit collision and safety constraints.
- [ImgTrojan: Jailbreaking Vision-Language Models with ONE Image](https://arxiv.org/abs/2403.02910): Shows a single adversarial image can trigger jailbreak behavior in VLMs across different user instructions.
- [Sniffer: Multimodal Large Language Model for Explainable Out-of-Context Misinformation Detection](https://arxiv.org/abs/2403.03170): Builds an MLLM detector that explains image-text context mismatches for out-of-context misinformation.
- [Correlated Proxies: A New Definition and Improved Mitigation for Reward Hacking](https://arxiv.org/abs/2403.03185): Defines reward hacking through correlated proxy objectives and proposes mitigation that separates target rewards from spurious proxy signals.
- [Guardrail Baselines for Unlearning in LLMs](https://arxiv.org/abs/2403.03329): Compares guardrail-style baselines for LLM unlearning to distinguish true parameter forgetting from output filtering.
- [Neural Exec: Learning (and Learning from) Execution Triggers for Prompt Injection Attacks](https://arxiv.org/abs/2403.03792): Learns execution-trigger patterns that activate prompt-injection attacks, then uses those triggers to study and harden LLM applications.
- [From One to Many: Expanding the Scope of Toxicity Mitigation in Language Models](https://arxiv.org/abs/2403.03893): Extends toxicity mitigation beyond single-label detoxification by training against multiple toxicity types and affected groups.
- [WaterMax: breaking the LLM watermark detectability-robustness-quality trade-off](https://arxiv.org/abs/2403.04808): Adds a watermarking scheme that leaves model weights, logits, temperature, and sampling unchanged while improving detectability, quality preservation, and robustness.
- [Towards Multimodal Sentiment Analysis Debiasing via Bias Purification](https://arxiv.org/abs/2403.05023): Purifies biased multimodal features so sentiment models reduce demographic or modality-driven bias without discarding task signal.
- [Defending Against Unforeseen Failure Modes with Latent Adversarial Training](https://arxiv.org/abs/2403.05030): Trains against adversarial perturbations in latent space to improve robustness to failure modes not covered by explicit attack data.
- [DP-TabICL: In-Context Learning with Differentially Private Tabular Data](https://arxiv.org/abs/2403.05681): Adapts in-context learning to differentially private tabular demonstrations so private records can support prompting without direct disclosure.
- [Challenging Forgets: Unveiling the Worst-Case Forget Sets in Machine Unlearning](https://arxiv.org/abs/2403.07362): Recasts unlearning evaluation around adversarially chosen worst-case forget sets found by bi-level optimization, exposing failures hidden by random deletion tests.
- [ORPO: Monolithic Preference Optimization without Reference Model](https://arxiv.org/abs/2403.07691): Replaces the separate reference-model preference-alignment phase with odds-ratio preference optimization inside supervised fine-tuning for models from 125M to 7B parameters.
- [Fairness Feedback Loops: Training on Synthetic Data Amplifies Bias](https://arxiv.org/abs/2403.07857): Shows synthetic-data retraining can amplify demographic bias through feedback loops, warning against unfiltered model-generated safety data.
- [Distract Large Language Models for Automatic Jailbreak Attack](https://arxiv.org/abs/2403.08424): Automates jailbreak generation by adding distractor content that diverts safety reasoning away from the harmful intent.
- [AIGCs Confuse AI Too: Investigating and Explaining Synthetic Image-induced Hallucinations in Large Vision-Language Models](https://arxiv.org/abs/2403.08542): Shows synthetic images can induce LVLM hallucinations and analyzes the visual features that make generated content misleading.
- [Eyes Closed, Safety On: Protecting Multimodal LLMs via Image-to-Text Transformation](https://arxiv.org/abs/2403.09572): Converts images into text descriptions before safety judging so MLLMs can filter visual jailbreak content through a language-only guard path.
- [Images are Achilles' Heel of Alignment: Exploiting Visual Vulnerabilities for Jailbreaking Multimodal Large Language Models](https://arxiv.org/abs/2403.09792): Exploits visual perturbations and image-text conflicts to bypass MLLM safety alignment while the textual request appears benign.
- [A Watermark-Conditioned Diffusion Model for IP Protection](https://arxiv.org/abs/2403.10893): Conditions diffusion generation on hidden watermark information so black-box API outputs support both synthetic-content detection and user or owner identification.
- [Adversarial Nibbler: An Open Red-Teaming Method for Identifying Diverse Harms in Text-to-Image Generation](https://arxiv.org/abs/2403.12075): Evaluates red-teaming with An Open Red-Teaming Method for Identifying Diverse Harms in Text-to-Image Generation, focusing on diverse harm discovery and guardrail failure modes.
- [EasyJailbreak: A Unified Framework for Jailbreaking Large Language Models](https://arxiv.org/abs/2403.12171): Provides a unified jailbreak-generation framework that composes attack templates, mutation strategies, and evaluators across LLM targets.
- [Selective Domain-Invariant Feature for Generalizable Deepfake Detection](https://arxiv.org/abs/2403.12707): Learns selective domain-invariant features so deepfake detectors generalize across manipulation methods and datasets.
- [Duwak: Dual Watermarks in Large Language Models](https://arxiv.org/abs/2403.13000): Combines two watermark channels in LLM decoding to improve provenance detection while preserving generation quality.
- [RigorLLM: Resilient Guardrails for Large Language Models against Undesired Content](https://arxiv.org/abs/2403.13031): Trains resilient LLM guardrails that classify and block undesired content under adversarial prompt variation.
- [BadEdit: Backdooring large language models by model editing](https://arxiv.org/abs/2403.13355): Uses model editing to implant LLM backdoors, showing safety risks from post-hoc parameter edits rather than full retraining.
- [FMM-Attack: A Flow-based Multi-modal Adversarial Attack on Video-based LLMs](https://arxiv.org/abs/2403.13507): Generates flow-based multimodal adversarial perturbations against video LLMs to test robustness of temporal visual reasoning.
- [HETAL: Efficient Privacy-preserving Transfer Learning with Homomorphic Encryption](https://arxiv.org/abs/2403.14111): Uses homomorphic encryption to support privacy-preserving transfer learning while keeping source features or gradients hidden.
- [Locating and Mitigating Gender Bias in Large Language Models](https://arxiv.org/abs/2403.14409): Combines causal mediation analysis for locating biased LLM components with the Least Square Debias Method, a knowledge-editing mitigation for gender stereotypes.
- [Bypassing LLM Watermarks with Color-Aware Substitutions](https://arxiv.org/abs/2403.14719): Introduces Self Color Testing-based Substitution, which queries a watermarked LLM to infer green-token colors and replace them with non-green alternatives.
- [Defending Against Indirect Prompt Injection Attacks With Spotlighting](https://arxiv.org/abs/2403.14720): Marks untrusted retrieved or tool-provided text with spotlighting transformations so the model distinguishes data from executable instructions.
- [Testing the Limits of Jailbreaking Defenses with the Purple Problem](https://arxiv.org/abs/2403.14725): Uses the Purple Problem as a controlled harmful-task probe to compare how jailbreak defenses fail under adaptive wording.
- [Protecting Copyrighted Material with Unique Identifiers in Large Language Model Training](https://arxiv.org/abs/2403.15740): Inserts unique identifiers into copyrighted training material so generated memorization can be traced back to protected sources.
- [Unlearning Backdoor Threats: Enhancing Backdoor Defense in Multimodal Contrastive Learning via Local Token Unlearning](https://arxiv.org/abs/2403.16257): Removes backdoor behavior from multimodal contrastive models by locally unlearning trigger-associated tokens.
- [The N+ Implementation Details of RLHF with PPO: A Case Study on TL;DR Summarization](https://arxiv.org/abs/2403.17031): Audits PPO-based RLHF implementation details on summarization, showing how small training choices affect aligned-model behavior.
- [Task-Agnostic Detector for Insertion-Based Backdoor Attacks](https://arxiv.org/abs/2403.17155): Detects insertion-based backdoors without task-specific labels by identifying abnormal inserted-token patterns and activations.
- [Optimization-based Prompt Injection Attack to LLM-as-a-Judge](https://arxiv.org/abs/2403.17710): Optimizes prompt-injection strings that manipulate LLM-as-a-judge evaluators, exposing vulnerabilities in model-based safety scoring.
- [Is The Watermarking Of LLM-Generated Code Robust?](https://arxiv.org/abs/2403.17983): Stress-tests watermarking methods for LLM-generated code under code-specific transformations, clarifying when provenance signals survive formatting, rewriting, and semantic-preserving edits.
- [RAW: A Robust and Agile Plug-and-Play Watermark Framework for AI-Generated Images with Provable Guarantees](https://arxiv.org/abs/2403.18774): Adds provable image watermarks through a plug-and-play generation framework designed to remain detectable under common edits.
- [CPR: Retrieval Augmented Generation for Copyright Protection](https://arxiv.org/abs/2403.18920): Uses retrieval-augmented generation to detect and avoid copyrighted source overlap during text generation.
- [Measuring Political Bias in Large Language Models: What Is Said and How It Is Said](https://arxiv.org/abs/2403.18932): Separates political bias in LLM outputs into content choices and linguistic framing, measuring both what is said and how it is phrased.
- [FairCLIP: Harnessing Fairness in Vision-Language Learning](https://arxiv.org/abs/2403.19949): Fine-tunes CLIP-style vision-language representations with fairness constraints to reduce demographic bias in downstream predictions.
- [Zero-shot Safety Prediction for Autonomous Robots with Foundation World Models](https://arxiv.org/abs/2404.00462): Uses foundation world models for zero-shot robot safety prediction, forecasting action-conditioned violations without task-specific safety-model training.
- [Privacy Backdoors: Stealing Data with Corrupted Pretrained Models](https://arxiv.org/abs/2404.00473): Implants privacy backdoors in pretrained models so downstream fine-tuning leaks target data under attacker-chosen triggers.
- [Configurable Safety Tuning of Language Models with Synthetic Preference Data](https://arxiv.org/abs/2404.00495): Generates synthetic preference data with controllable safety levels, then tunes language models to different harmlessness and helpfulness trade-offs.
- [Embodied Active Defense: Leveraging Recurrent Feedback to Counter Adversarial Patches](https://arxiv.org/abs/2404.00540): Uses recurrent perception feedback in embodied agents to actively re-observe scenes and reduce adversarial patch effects.
- [Enabling Memory Safety of C Programs using LLMs](https://arxiv.org/abs/2404.01096): Applies LLMs to detect and repair memory-safety defects in C programs, targeting unsafe pointer and buffer behavior.
- [What is in Your Safe Data? Identifying Benign Data that Breaks Safety](https://arxiv.org/abs/2404.01099): Identifies apparently benign fine-tuning data that erodes LLM safety, showing safe-looking examples can still undo alignment.
- [Privacy Backdoors: Enhancing Membership Inference through Poisoning Pre-trained Models](https://arxiv.org/abs/2404.01231): Poisons pretrained models to amplify membership-inference leakage, turning later model access into a stronger privacy attack.
- [A Statistical Framework of Watermarks for Large Language Models: Pivot, Detection Efficiency and Optimal Rules](https://arxiv.org/abs/2404.01245): Derives statistical tests for LLM watermark detection, including pivot choices and optimal decision rules for detection efficiency.
- [Great, Now Write an Article About That: The Crescendo Multi-Turn LLM Jailbreak Attack](https://arxiv.org/abs/2404.01833): Introduces Crescendo, a multi-turn jailbreak that gradually escalates benign requests until the model produces forbidden content.
- [Jailbreaking Leading Safety-Aligned LLMs with Simple Adaptive Attacks](https://arxiv.org/abs/2404.02151): Shows simple adaptive prompt attacks can bypass leading safety-aligned LLMs by iteratively adjusting wording to observed refusals.
- [Vocabulary Attack to Hijack Large Language Model Applications](https://arxiv.org/abs/2404.02637): Inserts optimized vocabulary words, found with embeddings from an attacker LLM, to hijack Llama2 and Flan-T5 applications with inconspicuous single-word or short-word prompt changes.
- [Jailbreaking Prompt Attack: A Controllable Adversarial Attack against Diffusion Models](https://arxiv.org/abs/2404.02928): Searches diffusion-model text-embedding space for NSFW concepts using antonym prompts, then optimizes a discrete prefix prompt with soft assignment and gradient masking to trigger harmful image generation without target-model access.
- [Do Large Language Models Rank Fairly? An Empirical Study on the Fairness of LLMs as Rankers](https://arxiv.org/abs/2404.03192): Evaluates GPT and Llama2 rankers on TREC Fair Ranking, measuring protected-attribute bias from both user and content perspectives.
- [Learn What You Want to Unlearn: Unlearning Inversion Attacks against Machine Unlearning](https://arxiv.org/abs/2404.03233): Shows that comparing original and unlearned models can reconstruct feature and label information for forgotten samples under machine-learning-as-a-service access.
- [Red Teaming GPT-4V: Are GPT-4V Safe Against Uni/Multi-Modal Jailbreak Attacks?](https://arxiv.org/abs/2404.03411): Builds a 1,445-question multimodal jailbreak set across 11 safety policies and red-teams 11 proprietary and open LLMs and MLLMs including GPT-4V.
- [Robust Concept Erasure Using Task Vectors](https://arxiv.org/abs/2404.03631): Uses task vectors for robust concept erasure in generative models.
- [Negative Preference Optimization: From Catastrophic Collapse to Effective Unlearning](https://arxiv.org/abs/2404.05868): Uses an alignment-style negative preference loss for LLM unlearning, slowing catastrophic utility collapse relative to gradient-ascent forgetting.
- [Eraser: Jailbreaking Defense in Large Language Models via Unlearning Harmful Knowledge](https://arxiv.org/abs/2404.05880): Defends against jailbreaks by unlearning general answers related to harmful queries while retaining general knowledge and safety alignment.
- [Rethinking How to Evaluate Language Model Jailbreak](https://arxiv.org/abs/2404.06407): Replaces binary jailbreak success with safeguard violation, informativeness, and relative truthfulness metrics aligned to malicious-actor goals.
- [Goal-Guided Generative Prompt Injection Attack on Large Language Models](https://arxiv.org/abs/2404.07234): Optimizes prompt injections by maximizing KL divergence between clean and adversarial conditional probabilities, yielding constrained G2PIA attack text.
- [Sandwich attack: Multi-language Mixture Adaptive Attack on LLMs](https://arxiv.org/abs/2404.07242): Analyzes an adversarial risk through Multi-language Mixture Adaptive Attack on LLMs, focusing on the attack surface, transfer path, or defense failure mode.
- [NeuroNCAP: Photorealistic Closed-loop Safety Testing for Autonomous Driving](https://arxiv.org/abs/2404.07762): Uses a NeRF-based closed-loop simulator to reconfigure real driving sensor sequences into Euro NCAP-inspired safety-critical scenarios for end-to-end planners.
- [High-Dimension Human Value Representation in Large Language Models](https://arxiv.org/abs/2404.07900): Introduces UniVaR, a self-supervised high-dimensional representation of symbolic human-value distributions across 15 LLMs, 25 languages, and cultures.
- [AmpleGCG: Learning a Universal and Transferable Generative Model of Adversarial Suffixes for Jailbreaking Both Open and Closed LLMs](https://arxiv.org/abs/2404.07921): Trains a generative model on successful intermediate GCG suffixes so hundreds of transferable jailbreak suffixes can be sampled in seconds.
- [Rethinking Artistic Copyright Infringements in the Era of Text-to-Image Generative Models](https://arxiv.org/abs/2404.08030): Reformulates artistic-style infringement as image-set classification and introduces ArtSavant with TagMatch for interpretable style detection in generated images.
- [Latent Guard: a Safety Framework for Text-to-image Generation](https://arxiv.org/abs/2404.08031): Learns a harmful-concept latent space over a text-to-image model's text encoder using LLM-generated data, custom architecture, and contrastive learning.
- [Uncertain Boundaries: Multidisciplinary Approaches to Copyright Issues in Generative AI](https://arxiv.org/abs/2404.08221): Surveys generative-AI copyright risk across law, policy, economics, and computer science, including output-violation detection and mitigation methods.
- [Towards Building a Robust Toxicity Predictor](https://arxiv.org/abs/2404.08690): Introduces ToxicTrap word-level adversarial examples for toxicity classifiers and tests adversarial training variants for robustness to unseen attacks.
- [Analyzing the Impact of Data Selection and Fine-Tuning on Economic and Political Biases in LLMs](https://arxiv.org/abs/2404.08699): Introduces PoliTune, a parameter-efficient fine-tuning setup for steering LLMs toward target economic and political ideologies to measure bias shifts from data selection.
- [JailbreakLens: Visual Analysis of Jailbreak Attacks Against Large Language Models](https://arxiv.org/abs/2404.08793): Provides an LLM-assisted visual analytics system for assessing jailbreak attempts, inspecting prompt components, and refining prompt instances with expert feedback.
- [Foundational Challenges in Assuring Alignment and Safety of Large Language Models](https://arxiv.org/abs/2404.09932): Organizes 18 LLM alignment and safety assurance challenges into scientific-understanding, development-deployment, and sociotechnical categories with more than 200 research questions.
- [Reinforcement Learning from Multi-role Debates as Feedback for Bias Mitigation in LLMs](https://arxiv.org/abs/2404.10160): Replaces human bias labels with self-reflection or teacher-student multi-role debates to train a reward model for reinforcement-learning bias mitigation.
- [Generative Text Steganography with Large Language Model](https://arxiv.org/abs/2404.10229): Uses LLM-generated cover text and constrained word selection to hide secret messages while keeping the steganographic text fluent.
- [Social Choice for AI Alignment: Dealing with Diverse Human Feedback](https://arxiv.org/abs/2404.10271): Frames alignment from diverse feedback as a social-choice problem, comparing aggregation rules for conflicting human preferences.
- [What are human values, and how do we align AI to them?](https://arxiv.org/abs/2404.10636): Reviews value definitions from multiple disciplines and maps how value concepts affect technical AI-alignment targets and evaluation.
- [Offset Unlearning for Large Language Models](https://arxiv.org/abs/2404.11045): Applies offset-based parameter updates to remove targeted LLM knowledge while preserving unrelated capability.
- [TransLinkGuard: Safeguarding Transformer Models Against Model Stealing in Edge Deployment](https://arxiv.org/abs/2404.11121): Protects edge-deployed transformers from model stealing with linked watermarking and ownership verification mechanisms.
- [Physical Backdoor Attack can Jeopardize Driving with Vision-Large-Language Models](https://arxiv.org/abs/2404.12916): Demonstrates physical-world backdoor triggers that cause vision-language driving models to produce unsafe decisions.
- [Towards Better Text-to-Image Generation Alignment via Attention Modulation](https://arxiv.org/abs/2404.13899): Modulates cross-attention in text-to-image generation to improve prompt-image alignment without retraining the diffusion model.
- [Protecting Your LLMs with Information Bottleneck](https://arxiv.org/abs/2404.13968): Uses an information-bottleneck objective to limit leakage of sensitive or attack-relevant information from LLM representations.
- [RTP-LX: Can LLMs Evaluate Toxicity in Multilingual Scenarios?](https://arxiv.org/abs/2404.14397): Tests whether LLMs can judge toxicity across multilingual RTP-LX scenarios rather than relying on English-only toxicity assumptions.
- [A Mechanism-Based Approach to Mitigating Harms from Persuasive Generative AI](https://arxiv.org/abs/2404.15058): Connects persuasion-harm mechanisms to generative-AI safeguards, identifying mitigation points beyond output filtering.
- [CodeIP: A Grammar-Guided Multi-Bit Watermark for Large Language Models of Code](https://arxiv.org/abs/2404.15639): Embeds multi-bit watermarks into generated code by constraining grammar-equivalent choices so ownership can be verified with low functional impact.
- [Prompt Leakage effect and defense strategies for multi-turn LLM interactions](https://arxiv.org/abs/2404.16251): Studies how hidden prompts leak across multi-turn interactions and compares defense strategies for preserving prompt confidentiality.
- [Don't Say No: Jailbreaking LLM by Suppressing Refusal](https://arxiv.org/abs/2404.16369): Attacks safety-aligned LLMs by suppressing refusal behavior, exposing a jailbreak path through the model's refusal-generation mechanism.
- [AdvPrompter: Fast Adaptive Adversarial Prompting for LLMs](https://arxiv.org/abs/2404.16873): Trains an adaptive adversarial prompting model that rapidly generates suffixes against target LLMs instead of running slow per-query optimization.
- [Human-Imperceptible Retrieval Poisoning Attacks in LLM-Powered Applications](https://arxiv.org/abs/2404.17196): Studies poisoning attacks against retrieval in LLM-powered applications, showing how subtle injected content can steer downstream model behavior.
- [SOUL: Unlocking the Power of Second-Order Optimization for LLM Unlearning](https://arxiv.org/abs/2404.18239): Applies second-order optimization to improve LLM unlearning.
- [More RLHF, More Trust? On The Impact of Preference Alignment On Trustworthiness](https://arxiv.org/abs/2404.18870): Compares preference-aligned models to base models on trustworthiness dimensions, testing whether more RLHF consistently improves safety-relevant behavior.
- [TUBA: Cross-Lingual Transferability of Backdoor Attacks in LLMs with Instruction Tuning](https://arxiv.org/abs/2404.19597): Studies whether instruction-tuned LLM backdoors transfer across languages, revealing multilingual trigger persistence and safety risks.
- [Mixture of insighTful Experts (MoTE): The Synergy of Thought Chains and Expert Mixtures in Self-Alignment](https://arxiv.org/abs/2405.00557): Combines thought-chain reasoning with a mixture of expert roles to improve self-alignment without direct human preference labels.
- [Are Models Biased on Text without Gender-related Language?](https://arxiv.org/abs/2405.00588): Tests gender bias in ostensibly gender-neutral text, isolating stereotype effects that remain after explicit gender words are removed.
- [Boosting Jailbreak Attack with Momentum](https://arxiv.org/abs/2405.01229): Adds momentum to jailbreak optimization so adversarial prompt updates transfer more effectively across iterations and target models.
- [NeMo-Aligner: Scalable Toolkit for Efficient Model Alignment](https://arxiv.org/abs/2405.01481): Provides a scalable alignment toolkit for supervised fine-tuning, reward modeling, and RLHF-style optimization of large language models.
- [Uncovering Deceptive Tendencies in Language Models: A Simulated Company AI Assistant](https://arxiv.org/abs/2405.01576): Uses a simulated company-assistant setting to test whether language models pursue deceptive behavior under role, incentive, or oversight pressure.
- [To Each (Textual Sequence) Its Own: Improving Memorized-Data Unlearning in Large Language Models](https://arxiv.org/abs/2405.03097): Measures each sequence's memorization degree, then uses sequence-specific gradient-ascent and task-arithmetic unlearning to reduce verbatim regurgitation.
- [Explainable Fake News Detection with Large Language Model via Defense Among Competing Wisdom](https://arxiv.org/abs/2405.03371): Splits crowd opinions into competing evidence parties and uses an LLM-based defense process to produce explainable fake-news verdicts from contested narratives.
- [Can LLMs Deeply Detect Complex Malicious Queries? A Framework for Jailbreaking via Obfuscating Intent](https://arxiv.org/abs/2405.03654): Introduces IntentObfuscator, a black-box jailbreak that hides malicious goals through obscure intention and ambiguity variants across ChatGPT, Qwen, and Baichuan models.
- [Learning To See But Forgetting To Follow: Visual Instruction Tuning Makes LLMs More Prone To Jailbreak Attacks](https://arxiv.org/abs/2405.04403): Compares three VLMs with their LLM backbones and finds visual instruction tuning weakens jailbreak resistance, motivating safety-aware VLM tuning and evaluation.
- [Explanation as a Watermark: Towards Harmless and Multi-bit Model Ownership Verification via Watermarking Feature Attribution](https://arxiv.org/abs/2405.04825): Replaces backdoor-style model watermarks with feature-attribution explanations that encode multi-bit ownership signals without introducing malicious trigger behavior.
- [“They are uncultured”: Unveiling Covert Harms and Social Threats in LLM Generated Conversations](https://arxiv.org/abs/2405.05378): Introduces CHAST, seven social-science-grounded metrics for detecting covert cultural harms and social threats in LLM-generated recruitment conversations.
- [Mitigating Exaggerated Safety in Large Language Models](https://arxiv.org/abs/2405.05418): Uses XSTest prompts with contextual, interactive, and few-shot prompting to reduce over-refusal of benign prompts while preserving refusals for dangerous requests.
- [An Inversion-Based Measure of Memorization for Diffusion Models](https://arxiv.org/abs/2405.05846): Proposes InvMM, an inversion-based diffusion memorization measure that estimates sensitive latent-noise distributions responsible for image replication.
- [Value Augmented Sampling for Language Model Alignment and Personalization](https://arxiv.org/abs/2405.06639): Optimizes alignment rewards from samples of a frozen initial LLM, avoiding joint policy-value training while approaching Best-of-128 quality at lower inference cost.
- [LLM-Generated Black-box Explanations Can Be Adversarially Helpful](https://arxiv.org/abs/2405.06800): Shows LLM explanations can make wrong answers appear credible through reframing, overconfidence, and cherry-picked evidence, misleading both humans and LLM evaluators.
- [PLeak: Prompt Leaking Attacks against Large Language Model Applications](https://arxiv.org/abs/2405.06823): Formulates closed-box prompt leakage as adversarial-query optimization, training attack prompts that induce LLM applications to reveal confidential system prompts.
- [Stable Signature is Unstable: Removing Image Watermark from Diffusion Models](https://arxiv.org/abs/2405.07145): Demonstrates that fine-tuning a Stable Signature diffusion decoder can remove embedded image watermarks while preserving generated image quality.
- [Erasing Concepts from Text-to-Image Diffusion Models with Few-shot Unlearning](https://arxiv.org/abs/2405.07288): Updates a text-to-image diffusion model's text encoder with a few real images, erasing target concepts in seconds without full retraining.
- [A safety realignment framework via subspace-oriented model fusion for large language models](https://arxiv.org/abs/2405.09055): Uses subspace-oriented model fusion to combine an aligned base model's safeguards with a downstream fine-tuned model while reducing catastrophic forgetting.
- [Efficient LLM Jailbreak via Adaptive Dense-to-sparse Constrained Optimization](https://arxiv.org/abs/2405.09113): Introduces ADC, a token-level jailbreak optimizer that relaxes discrete suffix search into continuous optimization and then increases sparsity to recover effective prompts.
- [Spectral Editing of Activations for Large Language Model Alignment](https://arxiv.org/abs/2405.09719): Edits LLM activations at inference time by projecting representations toward positive demonstrations and away from negative ones to reduce untruthful or biased outputs.
- [Human-AI Safety: A Descendant of Generative AI and Control Systems Safety](https://arxiv.org/abs/2405.09794): Frames AI safety as a feedback-control problem over model outputs and human behavior, arguing that output-only alignment misses long-horizon interaction risks.
- [Adversarial Robustness for Visual Grounding of Multimodal Large Language Models](https://arxiv.org/abs/2405.09981): Attacks MLLM visual grounding with untargeted, exclusive targeted, and permuted targeted perturbations over referring-expression bounding boxes.
- [MarkLLM: An Open-Source Toolkit for LLM Watermarking](https://arxiv.org/abs/2405.10051): Provides an extensible LLM watermarking toolkit with algorithm implementations, mechanism visualizations, and evaluation pipelines spanning robustness, detectability, and text quality.
- ["What do you want from theory alone?" Experimenting with Tight Auditing of Differentially Private Synthetic Data Generation](https://arxiv.org/abs/2405.10994): Audits six differentially private synthetic-data generators with distinguishing games and membership-inference attacks to test whether empirical leakage matches theoretical guarantees.
- [Watermarking Language Models for Many Adaptive Users](https://arxiv.org/abs/2405.11109): Constructs adaptively robust multi-user language-model watermarks that can trace generated text to individual users or colluding groups across repeated prompts.
- [Single Image Unlearning: Efficient Machine Unlearning in Multimodal Large Language Models](https://arxiv.org/abs/2405.12523): Removes a leaked visual concept from an MLLM using a single target image, then evaluates whether the model forgets the image while retaining general ability.
- [TrojanRAG: Retrieval-Augmented Generation Can Be Backdoor Driver in Large Language Models](https://arxiv.org/abs/2405.13401): Shows RAG can act as a backdoor carrier by inserting poisoned retrieval content that steers otherwise benign LLM responses.
- [ConTrans: Weak-to-Strong Alignment Engineering via Concept Transplantation](https://arxiv.org/abs/2405.13578): Transfers alignment concepts from a weaker aligned model into a stronger target model, improving weak-to-strong safety behavior through concept transplantation.
- [Safety Alignment for Vision Language Models](https://arxiv.org/abs/2405.13581): Builds a VLM safety-alignment recipe using synthetic unsafe multimodal instructions, risk-aware preference data, and a safety-trained reward model.
- [Towards Comprehensive Post Safety Alignment of Large Language Models via Safety Patching](https://arxiv.org/abs/2405.13820): Introduces SafePatching, which inserts separate safety patches for harmfulness reduction and over-safety mitigation into aligned LLM backbones.
- [WordGame: Efficient & Effective LLM Jailbreak via Simultaneous Obfuscation in Query and Response](https://arxiv.org/abs/2405.14023): Obfuscates malicious query terms as word games and induces matching response-side obfuscation, bypassing safety alignment patterns that look for explicit harmful wording.
- [ChatScene: Knowledge-Enabled Safety-Critical Scenario Generation for Autonomous Vehicles](https://arxiv.org/abs/2405.14062): Uses an LLM agent and retrieval-backed domain-specific code generation to turn traffic descriptions into CARLA safety-critical driving scenarios.
- [Nearly Tight Black-Box Auditing of Differentially Private Machine Learning](https://arxiv.org/abs/2405.14106): Audits DP-SGD under black-box access by choosing worst-case initial parameters, producing empirical privacy estimates much closer to theoretical epsilon bounds.
- [Semantic-guided Prompt Organization for Universal Goal Hijacking against LLMs](https://arxiv.org/abs/2405.14189): Builds POUGH, which samples and ranks semantic prompt candidates before optimizing a universal suffix for arbitrary-prompt goal hijacking.
- [Tighter Privacy Auditing of DP-SGD in the Hidden State Threat Model](https://arxiv.org/abs/2405.14457): Crafts gradient sequences that maximize final-model privacy loss, tightening DP-SGD audits when adversaries cannot observe intermediate training states.
- [MoGU: A Framework for Enhancing Safety of Open-Sourced LLMs While Preserving Their Usability](https://arxiv.org/abs/2405.14488): Splits an open LLM into usable and safe variants and dynamically routes between them so malicious prompts emphasize harmlessness while benign prompts retain utility.
- [Impact of Non-Standard Unicode Characters on Security and Comprehension in Large Language Models](https://arxiv.org/abs/2405.14490): Tests fifteen LLMs with non-standard Unicode queries, measuring jailbreaks, hallucinations, and comprehension errors caused by character-level perturbations.
- [Representation Noising: A Defence Mechanism Against Harmful Finetuning](https://arxiv.org/abs/2405.14577): Removes harmful internal representations before release so attackers with model weights have difficulty recovering harmful behavior through fine-tuning.
- [Membership Inference on Text-to-Image Diffusion Models via Conditional Likelihood Discrepancy](https://arxiv.org/abs/2405.14800): Uses conditional likelihood discrepancy to exploit text-conditioned overfitting in diffusion models, enabling lower-cost membership inference for text-image training samples.
- [Cross-Task Defense: Instruction-Tuning LLMs for Content Safety](https://arxiv.org/abs/2405.15202): Creates safety examples for long harmful documents and instruction-tunes LLMs with single-task and mixed-task losses to improve cross-task content-safety behavior.
- [Efficient Adversarial Training in LLMs with Continuous Attacks](https://arxiv.org/abs/2405.15589): Replaces expensive discrete adversarial attacks with continuous embedding-space attacks during training, combining robustness and utility losses for safer LLMs.
- [Breaking the False Sense of Security in Backdoor Defense through Re-Activation Attack](https://arxiv.org/abs/2405.16134): Shows post-training backdoor defenses can leave dormant triggers, then reactivates them at inference with small universal perturbations.
- [Large Scale Knowledge Washing](https://arxiv.org/abs/2405.16720): Updates decoder-only LLM MLP layers to unlearn large amounts of factual knowledge while avoiding reverse-loss training that damages fluency or reasoning.
- [Safe LoRA: the Silver Lining of Reducing Safety Risks when Fine-tuning Large Language Models](https://arxiv.org/abs/2405.16833): Adds a one-line LoRA patch that projects selected adapter weights into a safety-aligned subspace to reduce safety degradation during fine-tuning.
- [RLAIF-V: Open-Source AI Feedback Leads to Super GPT-4V Trustworthiness](https://arxiv.org/abs/2405.17220): Aligns MLLMs with open-source AI feedback for preference learning and inference-time self-feedback, reducing object and overall hallucination on trustworthiness benchmarks.
- [AI Alignment with Changing and Influenceable Reward Functions](https://arxiv.org/abs/2405.17713): Formalizes Dynamic Reward MDPs to study alignment when user preferences change or can be influenced by the AI system.
- [White-box Multimodal Jailbreaks Against Large Vision-Language Models](https://arxiv.org/abs/2405.17894): Jointly optimizes adversarial image prefixes and text suffixes so VLMs generate affirmative, toxic responses under white-box multimodal attacks.
- [Cross-Context Backdoor Attacks against Graph Prompt Learning](https://arxiv.org/abs/2405.17984): Introduces CrossBA, a pretraining-only graph-prompt backdoor that transfers through trigger graphs and prompt transformations across downstream graph tasks.
- [LLMs and Memorization: On Quality and Specificity of Copyright Compliance](https://arxiv.org/abs/2405.18492): Quantifies potential copyright regurgitation in instruction-tuned LLMs with a 160-character threshold, fuzzy matching, and specificity checks against public-domain data.
- [Learning diverse attacks on large language models for robust red-teaming and safety tuning](https://arxiv.org/abs/2405.18540): Fine-tunes an attacker model with GFlowNet objectives and smoothing so automated red-teaming samples diverse harmful prompts instead of collapsing to one attack mode.
- [Lisa: Lazy Safety Alignment for Large Language Models against Harmful Fine-tuning Attack](https://arxiv.org/abs/2405.18641): Adds a proximal drift constraint to bi-state fine-tuning, stabilizing safety-alignment states when harmful and user datasets are optimized separately.
- [Voice Jailbreak Attacks Against GPT-4o](https://arxiv.org/abs/2405.19103): Studies voice-input jailbreaks against GPT-4o, showing the audio channel creates safety failures distinct from text-only prompt attacks.
- [Self-Exploring Language Models: Active Preference Elicitation for Online Alignment](https://arxiv.org/abs/2405.19332): Uses an optimistic bilevel objective to make an aligning model actively explore high-reward out-of-distribution responses for online preference elicitation.
- [X-VILA: Cross-Modality Alignment for Large Language Model](https://arxiv.org/abs/2405.19335): Aligns image, video, and audio encoders with LLM inputs and diffusion decoders with outputs, adding a visual embedding highway to reduce cross-modal information loss.
- [Robustifying Safety-Aligned Large Language Models through Clean Data Curation](https://arxiv.org/abs/2405.19358): Iteratively rewrites clean texts to lower model-perceived perplexity while preserving quality, then uses the curated data to blunt harmful pretraining or fine-tuning attacks.
- [Diffusion Policy Attacker: Crafting Adversarial Attacks for Diffusion-based Policies](https://arxiv.org/abs/2405.19424): Provides DP-Attacker algorithms for offline and online, global and patch-based adversarial attacks against stochastic diffusion-policy behavior-cloning models.
- [One-Shot Safety Alignment for Large Language Models via Optimal Dualization](https://arxiv.org/abs/2405.19544): Converts constrained RLHF safety alignment into an unconstrained objective by pre-optimizing a closed-form convex dual function, avoiding unstable primal-dual iterations.
- [Large Language Model Watermark Stealing With Mixed Integer Programming](https://arxiv.org/abs/2405.19677): Uses mixed-integer programming to infer LLM watermark vocabulary partitions, enabling watermark stealing against schemes that rely on secret green and red token lists.
- [Efficient LLM-Jailbreaking via Multimodal-LLM Jailbreak](https://arxiv.org/abs/2405.20015): Builds an MLLM around the target LLM, optimizes a multimodal jailbreak embedding, and converts it into a textual suffix for more efficient LLM jailbreaks.
- [Transfer Attack for Bad and Good: Explain and Boost Adversarial Transferability across Multimodal Large Language Models](https://arxiv.org/abs/2405.20090): Analyzes why adversarial examples transfer between MLLMs sharing a vision encoder and boosts transfer with image-patch and typography augmentation.
- [Defensive Prompt Patch: A Robust and Interpretable Defense of LLMs against Jailbreak Attacks](https://arxiv.org/abs/2405.20099): Adds interpretable defensive suffix prompts that reduce jailbreak attack success while preserving utility under standard and adaptive attacks.
- [Jailbreaking Large Language Models Against Moderation Guardrails via Cipher Characters](https://arxiv.org/abs/2405.20413): Builds JAMBench to trigger moderation guardrails and attacks them with cipher-character jailbreak prompts across four harmful-risk categories.
- [Transfer Q Star: Principled Decoding for LLM Alignment](https://arxiv.org/abs/2405.20495): Estimates the optimal value function for a target reward from a base model, enabling lightweight alignment through decoding without updating model parameters.
- [Visual-RolePlay: Universal Jailbreak Attack on MultiModal Large Language Models via Role-playing Image Characte](https://arxiv.org/abs/2405.20773): Uses LLM-generated role-play image characters to embed harmful semantics in visual structure, improving universal jailbreak transfer against MLLMs.
- [Can We Trust Embodied Agents? Exploring Backdoor Attacks against Embodied LLM-Based Decision-Making Systems](https://arxiv.org/abs/2405.20774): Defines BALD backdoors for embodied LLM decision systems, using word injection, scenario manipulation, and knowledge injection triggers in the decision pipeline.
- [Improved Generation of Adversarial Examples Against Safety-aligned LLMs](https://arxiv.org/abs/2405.20778): Adapts skip-gradient and intermediate-level ideas from transfer attacks to improve gradient-based adversarial prompt generation for safety-aligned LLMs.
- [Improved Techniques for Optimization-Based Jailbreaking on Large Language Models](https://arxiv.org/abs/2405.21018): Improves GCG-style jailbreak optimization with diverse harmful target templates and automatic multi-coordinate token updates.
- [DistilDIRE: A Small, Fast, Cheap and Lightweight Diffusion Synthesized Deepfake Detection](https://arxiv.org/abs/2406.00856): Distills diffusion reconstruction-error knowledge into a smaller detector, reducing the compute cost of identifying diffusion-synthesized and GAN-generated images.
- [Improved Few-Shot Jailbreaking Can Circumvent Aligned Language Models and Their Defenses](https://arxiv.org/abs/2406.01288): Strengthens few-shot jailbreaks with special system-token injections and demo-level random search, bypassing aligned Llama models and defenses such as SmoothLLM.
- [REvolve: Reward Evolution with Large Language Models using Human Feedback](https://arxiv.org/abs/2406.01309): Evolves reward functions with LLM proposals and human feedback for autonomous driving, humanoid locomotion, and dexterous manipulation tasks where good behavior is tacit.
- [Decoupled Alignment for Robust Plug-and-Play Adaptation](https://arxiv.org/abs/2406.01514): Distills safety knowledge from aligned LLMs into unaligned models with delta-debugged components, improving harmful-question defense without SFT or RLHF.
- [Bileve: Securing Text Provenance in Large Language Models Against Spoofing with Bi-level Signature](https://arxiv.org/abs/2406.01946): Embeds bi-level text signatures that pair fine-grained integrity bits with a coarse provenance trace, distinguishing spoofed, altered, and genuine LLM outputs.
- [RKLD: Reverse KL-Divergence-based Knowledge Distillation for Unlearning Personal Information in Large Language Models](https://arxiv.org/abs/2406.01983): Uses reverse-KL knowledge distillation as an LLM unlearning algorithm for personal information, balancing forget quality against retained model utility.
- [Towards Universal and Black-Box Query-Response Only Attack on LLMs with QROA](https://arxiv.org/abs/2406.02044): Frames query-response-only jailbreak suffix search as a bandit optimization problem and extends it to universal one-query suffixes for black-box LLMs.
- [Cross-Modal Safety Alignment: Is textual unlearning all you need?](https://arxiv.org/abs/2406.02575): Tests whether text-only unlearning transfers to VLM safety, reporting lower attack success for both text and vision-text attacks without multimodal safety data.
- [Edit Distance Robust Watermarks via Indexing Pseudorandom Codes](https://arxiv.org/abs/2406.02633): Builds language-model watermarks from indexing pseudorandom codes to retain undetectability while tolerating adversarial insertions, substitutions, and deletions.
- [PrE-Text: Training Language Models on Private Federated Data in the Age of LLMs](https://arxiv.org/abs/2406.02958): Generates differentially private synthetic text from federated data so small and large language models can train on private user distributions with less client compute and communication.
- [DifAttack++: Query-Efficient Black-Box Adversarial Attack via Hierarchical Disentangled Feature Space in Cross-Domain](https://arxiv.org/abs/2406.03017): Disentangles adversarial and visual image features, then optimizes only the adversarial feature with victim-model query feedback for query-efficient black-box attacks.
- [MUC: Machine Unlearning for Contrastive Learning with Black-box Evaluation](https://arxiv.org/abs/2406.03603): Extends machine unlearning to contrastive encoders, using black-box evaluation to test whether forgotten examples still influence representation similarity.
- [AutoJailbreak: Exploring Jailbreak Attacks and Defenses through a Dependency Lens](https://arxiv.org/abs/2406.03805): Models jailbreak optimization as dependencies among prompt components, using that structure to scale both attack generation and defense analysis.
- [Jailbreak Vision Language Models via Bi-Modal Adversarial Prompt](https://arxiv.org/abs/2406.04031): Attacks LVLM safeguards by jointly perturbing visual and textual prompt channels, targeting aligned models that fuse both modalities before generation.
- [LLM Whisperer: An Inconspicuous Attack to Bias LLM Responses](https://arxiv.org/abs/2406.04755): Shows prompt-optimization services can bias downstream LLM outputs through subtle synonym substitutions while leaving prompts natural-looking to users.
- [LLavaGuard: VLM-based Safeguards for Vision Dataset Curation and Safety Assessment](https://arxiv.org/abs/2406.05113): Trains VLM safeguards with a customizable safety taxonomy, multimodal safety data, and preprocessing pipelines for vision dataset curation and risk assessment.
- [SelfDefend: LLMs Can Defend Themselves against Jailbreaking in a Practical Manner](https://arxiv.org/abs/2406.05498): Uses the target LLM itself to detect and revise unsafe responses, covering human, optimization, generation, indirect, and multilingual jailbreaks in a practical defense loop.
- [How Alignment and Jailbreak Work: Explain LLM Safety through Intermediate Hidden States](https://arxiv.org/abs/2406.05644): Probes intermediate hidden states to explain how safety alignment suppresses harmful answers and how jailbreak prompts move representations past that guardrail.
- [Safety Alignment Should Be Made More Than Just a Few Tokens Deep](https://arxiv.org/abs/2406.05946): Identifies shallow first-token safety shortcuts in aligned LLMs and argues for alignment objectives that shape deeper generation distributions.
