- [Unveiling the Safety of GPT-4o: An Empirical Study using Jailbreak Attacks](https://arxiv.org/abs/2406.06302): Stress-tests GPT-4o with jailbreak attacks to characterize risky-content refusal behavior rather than relying on general capability performance.
- [Annotation alignment: Comparing LLM and human annotations of conversational safety](https://arxiv.org/abs/2406.06369): Compares GPT-4 safety labels with DICES human annotations across race-gender groups, quantifying where automated safety judgment aligns with people.
- [Adversarial Tuning: Defending Against Jailbreak Attacks for LLMs](https://arxiv.org/abs/2406.06622): Trains LLMs with generated worst-case adversarial prompts and a second-stage tuning objective to improve defense against unseen jailbreaks.
- [Mitigating Boundary Ambiguity and Inherent Bias for Text Classification in the Era of Large Language Models](https://arxiv.org/abs/2406.07001): Shows option count and ordering distort LLM text-classification decisions, then targets ambiguous decision boundaries and option bias as reliability risks.
- [Merging Improves Self-Critique Against Jailbreak Attacks](https://arxiv.org/abs/2406.07188): Merges an external critic model into the base LLM and fine-tunes on sanitized synthetic data to strengthen self-critique against jailbreak prompts.
- [Collective Constitutional AI: Aligning a Language Model with Public Input](https://arxiv.org/abs/2406.07814): Builds a multi-stage CCAI process that sources public values, distills them into a constitution, and uses that input to align LM behavior.
- [Legend: Leveraging Representation Engineering to Annotate Safety Margin for Preference Datasets](https://arxiv.org/abs/2406.08124): Uses representation engineering to assign fine-grained safety margins to preference pairs, improving reward-model supervision beyond binary harmlessness labels.
- [Decoupling the Class Label and the Target Concept in Machine Unlearning](https://arxiv.org/abs/2406.08288): Separates class labels from the concepts to forget, exposing cases where class-wise unlearning removes labels while target concepts remain usable.
- [PAL: Pluralistic Alignment Framework for Learning from Heterogeneous Preferences](https://arxiv.org/abs/2406.08469): Learns from heterogeneous human preferences with a pluralistic alignment objective instead of collapsing annotator disagreement into one Bradley-Terry reward proxy.
- [Reversing the Forget-Retain Objectives: An Efficient LLM Unlearning Framework from Logit Difference](https://arxiv.org/abs/2406.08607): Uses logit differences to reverse forget and retain objectives, targeting privacy and copyright unlearning while preserving unrelated knowledge.
- [When LLM Meets DRL: Advancing Jailbreaking Efficiency via DRL-guided Search](https://arxiv.org/abs/2406.08705): Replaces random genetic jailbreak search with deep-reinforcement-learning guidance to find harmful prompts more efficiently in black-box settings.
- [RL-JACK: Reinforcement Learning-powered Black-box Jailbreaking Attack against LLMs](https://arxiv.org/abs/2406.08725): Frames black-box jailbreak prompt construction as reinforcement learning over attack scenarios that embed harmful questions.
- [StructuralSleight: Automated Jailbreak Attacks on Large Language Models Utilizing Uncommon Text-Encoded Structure](https://arxiv.org/abs/2406.08754): Exploits unusual text-encoded structures rather than plain prompt wording to automate jailbreaks against LLM safety filters.
- [Steganalysis on Digital Watermarking: Is Your Defense Truly Impervious?](https://arxiv.org/abs/2406.09026): Attacks content-agnostic image watermarks with steganalysis that extracts and removes fixed watermark patterns while preserving perceptual quality.
- [Are we making progress in unlearning? Findings from the first NeurIPS unlearning competition](https://arxiv.org/abs/2406.09073): Reports lessons from the first NeurIPS unlearning competition, emphasizing robust evaluation methodology and diverse algorithmic trade-offs.
- [Understanding Jailbreak Success: A Study of Latent Space Dynamics in Large Language Models](https://arxiv.org/abs/2406.09289): Extracts jailbreak vectors from activation dynamics to explain how different attack types shift aligned models toward unsafe responses.
- [REVS: Unlearning Sensitive Information in Language Models via Rank Editing in the Vocabulary Space](https://arxiv.org/abs/2406.09325): Removes sensitive or PII-like knowledge with non-gradient rank editing in vocabulary space, aiming to resist extraction attacks after unlearning.
- [Sycophancy to Subterfuge: Investigating Reward-Tampering in Large Language Models](https://arxiv.org/abs/2406.10162): Studies whether LLMs trained under misspecified rewards progress from sycophancy toward reward-tampering behaviors that manipulate their own evaluation.
- [Semantic Membership Inference Attack against Large Language Models](https://arxiv.org/abs/2406.10218): Improves membership inference by training on model behavior over semantic perturbations, targeting privacy leakage beyond exact input matching.
- [CHiSafetyBench: A Chinese Hierarchical Safety Benchmark for Large Language Models](https://arxiv.org/abs/2406.10311): Defines a hierarchical Chinese safety taxonomy and benchmark for authentic Chinese scenarios, making this row better suited to Bench than Model.
- [Enhancing Multilingual Voice Toxicity Detection with Speech-Text Alignment](https://arxiv.org/abs/2406.10325): Trains speech toxicity classifiers with speech-text alignment so inference can use audio-only inputs while benefiting from textual semantics.
- [Self-Reflection Makes Large Language Models Safer, Less Biased, and Ideologically Neutral](https://arxiv.org/abs/2406.10400): Tests self-reflection as a safety intervention, showing model revision can reduce unsafe, biased, or ideologically skewed outputs.
- [PRISM: A Design Framework for Open-Source Foundation Model Safety](https://arxiv.org/abs/2406.10415): Proposes PRISM as a design framework for open-source foundation-model safety, focused on limiting misuse by highly capable unsafe derivatives.
- [Emerging Safety Attack and Defense in Federated Instruction Tuning of Large Language Models](https://arxiv.org/abs/2406.10630): Reveals safety attacks in federated instruction tuning and studies defenses when parties fine-tune an LLM without sharing local data.
- [Don't Forget Too Much: Towards Machine Unlearning on Feature Level](https://arxiv.org/abs/2406.10951): Moves unlearning from whole classes or instances to feature-level removal, reducing utility loss when only a concept attribute should be forgotten.
- [Avoiding Copyright Infringement via Large Language Model Unlearning](https://arxiv.org/abs/2406.10952): Addresses sequential copyright takedown requests with LLM unlearning methods that remove protected content over time while retaining other capabilities.
- [Toward Optimal LLM Alignments Using Two-Player Games](https://arxiv.org/abs/2406.10977): Casts alignment as a two-agent game where prompts are selected to expose weaknesses that static preference datasets often miss.
- [A Peek into Token Bias: Large Language Models Are Not Yet Genuine Reasoners](https://arxiv.org/abs/2406.11050): Uses controlled conjunction-fallacy and syllogism tests to separate genuine reasoning from token-bias shortcuts in LLM decisions.
- [MemDPT: Differential Privacy for Memory Efficient Language Models](https://arxiv.org/abs/2406.11087): Combines differential privacy with a memory-efficient language-model training architecture to reduce privacy leakage under practical resource limits.
- [Exploring Safety-Utility Trade-Offs in Personalized Language Models](https://arxiv.org/abs/2406.11107): Measures personalization bias along safety and utility axes, showing user-identity adaptation can change fairness and harmfulness behavior.
- [Problematic Tokens: Tokenizer Bias in Large Language Models](https://arxiv.org/abs/2406.11214): Analyzes tokenizer-driven bias for under-resourced languages, linking tokenization artifacts to hallucination and degraded multilingual behavior.
- [Do Parameters Reveal More than Loss for Membership Inference?](https://arxiv.org/abs/2406.11544): Tests whether parameter information exposes membership signals beyond loss values, refining privacy attacks and evaluation assumptions.
- ["Not Aligned" is Not "Malicious": Being Careful about Hallucinations of Large Language Models' Jailbreak](https://arxiv.org/abs/2406.11668): Separates jailbreak hallucinations from genuinely malicious compliance, warning that safety evaluations can overcount attacks when outputs are ungrounded.
- [Knowledge-to-Jailbreak: Investigating Knowledge-driven Jailbreaking Attacks for Large Language Models](https://arxiv.org/abs/2406.11682): Builds domain-knowledge-driven jailbreak prompts for specialized areas such as medicine, expanding safety testing beyond generic harmful requests.
- [Measuring memorization in RLHF for code completion](https://arxiv.org/abs/2406.11715): Measures whether RLHF for code completion introduces or amplifies memorization of user-like training data, connecting alignment to privacy leakage.
- [Split, Unlearn, Merge: Leveraging Data Attributes for More Effective Unlearning in LLMs](https://arxiv.org/abs/2406.11780): Introduces SPUNGE, which splits data by attributes, applies an unlearning method, and merges results to amplify harmful-knowledge removal.
- [Safety Arithmetic: A Framework for Test-time Safety Alignment of Language Models by Steering Parameters and Activations](https://arxiv.org/abs/2406.11801): Provides a training-free test-time alignment framework that steers parameters and activations to adapt refusals to changing user intent.
- [GUARD-D-LLM: An LLM-Based Risk Assessment Engine for the Downstream uses of LLMs](https://arxiv.org/abs/2406.11851): Implements an LLM-based downstream-risk assessment engine aligned with AI Act, ISO, and NIST risk-management concerns.
- [CleanGen: Mitigating Backdoor Attacks for Generation Tasks in Large Language Models](https://arxiv.org/abs/2406.12257): Defends generation-time LLM applications against training-data backdoors with an inference-stage mitigation for compromised public or fine-tuned models.
- [SafeInfer: Context Adaptive Decoding Time Safety Alignment for Large Language Models](https://arxiv.org/abs/2406.12274): Applies context-adaptive decoding-time alignment to suppress unsafe continuations, including cases where knowledge editing weakens existing safety behavior.
- [Adaptive Token Biaser: Knowledge Editing via Biasing Key Entities](https://arxiv.org/abs/2406.12468): Edits LLM knowledge at decoding time by biasing key entities rather than modifying model weights or the full token distribution.
- [Not Everything is All You Need: Toward Low-Redundant Optimization for Large Language Model Alignment](https://arxiv.org/abs/2406.12606): Aligns only the most-updated parameters during training, reducing redundancy and improving convergence under preference-optimization workloads.
- [(WIP) Jailbreak Paradox: The Achilles' Heel of LLMs](https://arxiv.org/abs/2406.12702): Proves limits on perfect jailbreak classifiers and on weaker models reliably detecting jailbreaks in stronger models, with Llama and GPT-4o case studies.
- [Current state of LLM Risks and AI Guardrails](https://arxiv.org/abs/2406.12934): Reviews LLM risks such as bias, unsafe actions, poisoning, opacity, hallucination, and reproducibility, then motivates guardrail architectures.
- [Data Contamination Can Cross Language Barriers](https://arxiv.org/abs/2406.13236): Demonstrates benchmark contamination can transfer across languages, exposing limits of overlap-based leakage checks for multilingual LLM evaluation.
- [Textual Unlearning Gives a False Sense of Unlearning](https://arxiv.org/abs/2406.13348): Shows text-level unlearning can appear successful while sensitive memorized information remains recoverable under stronger probing.
- [Unlearning or Obfuscating? Jogging the Memory of Unlearned LLMs via Benign Relearning](https://arxiv.org/abs/2406.13356): Demonstrates benign relearning attacks that restore supposedly forgotten LLM knowledge from small, loosely related datasets.
- [Learn and Unlearn: Addressing Misinformation in Multilingual LLMs](https://arxiv.org/abs/2406.13748): Studies how fake information learned in one language spreads across multilingual LLMs and compares unlearning methods for removing it.
- [From Descriptive Richness to Bias: Unveiling the Dark Side of Generative Image Caption Enrichment](https://arxiv.org/abs/2406.13912): Shows generative caption enrichment can improve visual-text detail while injecting bias into downstream vision-language representations.
- [Towards Understanding Safety Alignment: A Mechanistic Perspective from Safety Neurons](https://arxiv.org/abs/2406.14144): Identifies safety neurons responsible for refusal behavior and uses inference-time intervention to analyze and adjust safety alignment.
- [The Fire Thief Is Also the Keeper: Balancing Usability and Privacy in Prompts](https://arxiv.org/abs/2406.14318): Balances prompt usability and privacy by mitigating sensitive information exposure without requiring local deployment or large system changes.
- [Jailbreaking as a Reward Misspecification Problem](https://arxiv.org/abs/2406.14393): Explains jailbreak vulnerability as reward misspecification during alignment, where the reward model fails to capture intended safety constraints.
- [PostMark: A Robust Blackbox Watermark for Large Language Models](https://arxiv.org/abs/2406.14517): Introduces a black-box text watermark that avoids provider access to model logits while preserving generated-text detectability.
- [Fantastic Copyrighted Beasts and How (Not) to Generate Them](https://arxiv.org/abs/2406.14526): Studies copyrighted-character reproduction in image and video generators and evaluates why prompt-level blocks can fail to prevent infringement.
- [Uncovering Latent Memories: Assessing Data Leakage and Memorization Patterns in Large Language Models](https://arxiv.org/abs/2406.14549): Audits memorization patterns and latent data leakage in web-scale LLMs that may expose personal, private, or proprietary training information.
- [Mitigating the Privacy Issues in Retrieval-Augmented Generation (RAG) via Pure Synthetic Data](https://arxiv.org/abs/2406.14773): Replaces private retrieval corpora with synthetic data to reduce sensitive-information leakage in RAG while preserving grounded generation.
- [Backdooring Bias ($B^2$) into Stable Diffusion Models](https://arxiv.org/abs/2406.15213): Injects biased generation behavior into Stable Diffusion as a backdoor attack, showing adversaries can steer demographic or content biases.
- [PKU-SafeRLHF: Towards Multi-Level Safety Alignment for LLMs with Human Preference](https://arxiv.org/abs/2406.15513): Releases a multi-level safety preference dataset separating helpfulness and harmlessness, with 44.6k prompts and 265k annotations for LLM alignment.
- [Unveiling Entity-Level Unlearning for Large Language Models: A Comprehensive Analysis](https://arxiv.org/abs/2406.15796): Analyzes entity-level LLM unlearning, highlighting gaps left by instance-level forget sets when all knowledge about an entity must be removed.
- [Towards Scalable Exact Machine Unlearning Using Parameter-Efficient Fine-Tuning](https://arxiv.org/abs/2406.16257): Uses parameter-efficient fine-tuning to scale exact machine unlearning guarantees without retraining the full base model from scratch.
- [Does Cross-Cultural Alignment Change the Commonsense Morality of Language Models?](https://arxiv.org/abs/2406.16316): Tests whether English-dominated preference alignment changes commonsense morality judgments when transferred or translated across cultures.
- [Safety Alignment of Large Language Models via Contrasting Safe and Harmful Distributions](https://arxiv.org/abs/2406.16743): Aligns LLMs by contrasting safe and harmful response distributions, reducing harmful outputs with less reliance on heavy RLHF-style training.
- [WARP: On the Benefits of Weight Averaged Rewarded Policies](https://arxiv.org/abs/2406.16768): Averages rewarded policies in RLHF to improve reward optimization while preserving pretrained knowledge usually protected by KL regularization.
- [How Data Inter-connectivity Shapes LLMs Unlearning: A Structural Unlearning Perspective](https://arxiv.org/abs/2406.16810): Introduces PISTOL structural datasets to test unlearning when forget examples are interconnected rather than independent points.
- [BEEAR: Embedding-based Adversarial Removal of Safety Backdoors in Instruction-tuned Language Models](https://arxiv.org/abs/2406.17092): Removes safety backdoors by identifying embedding patterns induced by triggers and adversarially suppressing unsafe triggered behavior.
- [Self-Constructed Context Decompilation with Fined-grained Alignment Enhancement](https://arxiv.org/abs/2406.17233): Improves code decompilation with self-constructed context and fine-grained alignment enhancement; this is off-axis for agent-safety model coverage.
- [AI Risk Categorization Decoded (AIR 2024): From Government Regulations to Corporate Policies](https://arxiv.org/abs/2406.17864): Derives a four-tier AI risk taxonomy from government and corporate policies, making this more taxonomy/survey evidence than a safety model.
- [SoK: Membership Inference Attacks on LLMs are Rushing Nowhere (and How to Fix It)](https://arxiv.org/abs/2406.17975): Critiques recent LLM membership-inference methods for weak assumptions and proposes more robust privacy-evaluation protocols.
- [SafeAligner: Safety Alignment against Jailbreak Attacks via Response Disparity Guidance](https://arxiv.org/abs/2406.18118): Uses response-disparity guidance to align LLMs against jailbreaks while reducing cost and preserving general utility.
- [Adversarial Search Engine Optimization for Large Language Models](https://arxiv.org/abs/2406.18382): Introduces preference-manipulation attacks where crafted web or plugin content steers LLM-powered search and selection toward an attacker.
- [WildGuard: Open One-Stop Moderation Tools for Safety Risks, Jailbreaks, and Refusals of LLMs](https://arxiv.org/abs/2406.18495): Provides a lightweight open moderation model for detecting malicious prompts, unsafe responses, and refusals in LLM interactions.
- [RouteLLM: Learning to Route LLMs with Preference Data](https://arxiv.org/abs/2406.18665): Learns cost-performance routers from preference data to choose between stronger and cheaper LLMs; useful infrastructure but off-axis for safety models.
- [Understand What LLM Needs: Dual Preference Alignment for Retrieval-Augmented Generation](https://arxiv.org/abs/2406.18676): Aligns RAG retrievers and LLM readers with DPA-RAG, using query-augmented preference data plus pairwise, pointwise, contrastive reranker training and a pre-alignment stage for the reader.
- [Generating Is Believing: Membership Inference Attacks against Retrieval-Augmented Generation](https://arxiv.org/abs/2406.19234): Introduces S2MIA, a RAG database membership-inference attack that compares generated answers with candidate records through semantic similarity and bypasses several defenses.
- [Covert Malicious Finetuning: Challenges in Safeguarding LLM Adaptation](https://arxiv.org/abs/2406.20053): Demonstrates covert malicious finetuning, where individually benign-looking encoded examples train a black-box adapted model to answer encoded harmful requests while evading dataset and I/O screening.
- [UnUnlearning: Unlearning is not sufficient for content regulation in advanced generative AI](https://arxiv.org/abs/2407.00106): Identifies a taxonomy mismatch for unlearning-as-content-control: exact parameter unlearning can remove training influence, but in-context "ununlearning" can reintroduce the forbidden knowledge at inference.
- [Towards Massive Multilingual Holistic Bias](https://arxiv.org/abs/2407.00486): Builds the MMHB multilingual bias dataset and benchmark with about six million sentences across 13 demographic axes, then uses it to expose gender robustness gaps and added toxicity in machine translation.
- [Large Language Models Are Involuntary Truth-Tellers: Exploiting Fallacy Failure for Jailbreak Attacks](https://arxiv.org/abs/2407.00869): Turns LLM difficulty with fabricating fallacious procedures into a jailbreak attack, asking for deceptive-but-fake instructions that the model fills with truthful harmful content.
- [A Fingerprint for Large Language Models](https://arxiv.org/abs/2407.01235): Proposes a black-box LLM fingerprint that authenticates a suspect model by testing whether its outputs lie in, or reconstruct with, the victim model's output subspace, including PEFT-modified models.
- [Badllama 3: removing safety finetuning from Llama 3 in minutes](https://arxiv.org/abs/2407.01376): Shows open-weight safety tuning can be stripped quickly, evaluating QLoRA, ReFT, and Ortho attacks that remove Llama 3 refusals with minutes of single-GPU optimization.
- [SeqAR: Jailbreak LLMs with Sequential Auto-Generated Characters](https://arxiv.org/abs/2407.01902): Automates cold-start jailbreak generation with open-source LLMs by optimizing multiple sequential persona characters in one query, then testing transfer across target models and harmful requests.
- [To Forget or Not? Towards Practical Knowledge Unlearning for Large Language Models](https://arxiv.org/abs/2407.01920): Introduces KnowUnDo for copyrighted and private knowledge unlearning, and MemFlex, a gradient-based method that targets sensitive parameters to avoid excessive forgetting.
- [Generative Monoculture in Large Language Models](https://arxiv.org/abs/2407.02209): Diagnoses diversity collapse from alignment as generative monoculture, showing sampling and prompting do not fix narrowed opinions or outputs, implying a fine-tuning objective mismatch.
- [Breach By A Thousand Leaks: Unsafe Information Leakage in 'Safe' AI Responses](https://arxiv.org/abs/2407.02551): Reframes jailbreak defense as an information-leakage problem, using question decomposition to infer impermissible knowledge from otherwise safe-looking answers and proving a safety-utility trade-off.
- [From Theft to Bomb-Making: The Ripple Effect of Unlearning in Defending Against Jailbreak Attacks](https://arxiv.org/abs/2407.02855): Shows unlearning one harmful response family can generalize through a ripple effect to unseen harmful knowledge, reducing jailbreak attack success with small unlearning sets.
- [LoRA-Guard: Parameter-Efficient Guardrail Adaptation for Content Moderation of Large Language Models](https://arxiv.org/abs/2407.02987): Trains a lightweight content-moderation guardrail by adapting LLM language features through low-rank adapters and a dual-path design for on-device safety filtering.
- [Towards Federated RLHF with Aggregated Client Preference for LLMs](https://arxiv.org/abs/2407.03038): Proposes FedBis and FedBiscuit for privacy-preserving RLHF, aggregating client-side binary preference selectors and grouping similar users to reduce heterogeneity and reward hacking.
- [SlerpFace: Face Template Protection via Spherical Linear Interpolation](https://arxiv.org/abs/2407.03043): Defends face-recognition templates from diffusion-model inversion by rotating embeddings toward noise-like distributions with spherical interpolation plus feature dropout.
- [Soft Begging: Modular and Efficient Shielding of LLMs against Prompt Injection and Jailbreaking based on Prompt Tuning](https://arxiv.org/abs/2407.03391): Uses trained soft prompts as an inference-time shield that counteracts corrupted prompt effects from prompt injection and jailbreak inputs without changing the base model.
- [Social Bias in Large Language Models For Bangla: An Empirical Study on Gender and Religious Bias](https://arxiv.org/abs/2407.03536): Provides a Bangla bias-detection dataset and probing setup for gender and religious bias in open-ended LLM generations, filling a low-resource safety-evaluation gap.
- [Automated Progressive Red Teaming](https://arxiv.org/abs/2407.03876): Builds APRT, an automated red-team model pipeline with intention-expanding and intention-hiding LLMs plus an Evil Maker filter to produce transferable unsafe-yet-useful prompts.
- [Certifiably Robust Image Watermark](https://arxiv.org/abs/2407.04086): Extends randomized smoothing to image watermarking, giving certified robustness guarantees against removal and forgery attacks as well as algorithms for estimating them.
- [Are Large Language Models Strategic Decision Makers? A Study of Performance and Bias in Two-Player Non-Zero-Sum Games](https://arxiv.org/abs/2407.04467): Flags a taxonomy mismatch: the contribution is an evaluation of positional, payoff, and behavioral biases in game-theoretic decisions, not a guard model or safety training method.
- [On scalable oversight with weak LLMs judging strong LLMs](https://arxiv.org/abs/2407.04622): Studies debate and consultancy as scalable oversight protocols where weaker LLM judges evaluate stronger agents across QA, math, coding, logic, and multimodal asymmetries.
- [Releasing Malevolence from Benevolence: The Menace of Benign Data on Machine Unlearning](https://arxiv.org/abs/2407.05112): Introduces the Unlearning Usability Attack, where benign-looking synthetic points pass poisoning checks but degrade a model when later removed through unlearning.
- [R2-Guard: Robust Reasoning Enabled LLM Guardrail via Knowledge-Enhanced Logical Reasoning](https://arxiv.org/abs/2407.05557): Combines category-specific guard models with first-order safety rules in Markov logic networks or probabilistic circuits, improving robustness to long-tail categories and jailbreak attacks.
- [Exposing Privacy Gaps: Membership Inference Attack on Preference Data for LLM Alignment](https://arxiv.org/abs/2407.06443): Introduces PREMIA, a reference-based membership-inference attack on alignment preference data, and shows DPO-aligned models are more exposed than PPO-aligned models.
- [Safe-Embed: Unveiling the Safety-Critical Knowledge of Sentence Encoders](https://arxiv.org/abs/2407.06851): Tests sentence encoders as unsafe-prompt detectors with new pairwise datasets and a Categorical Purity metric for safety-taxonomy separation.
- [Divine LLaMAs: Bias, Stereotypes, Stigmatization, and Emotion Representation of Religion in Large Language Models](https://arxiv.org/abs/2407.06908): Measures religious stereotypes and stigmatization through LLM emotion attribution, showing refusals and flattened portrayals differ across religious groups.
- [Zero-Shot Class Unlearning in CLIP with Synthetic Samples](https://arxiv.org/abs/2407.07485): Performs CLIP class unlearning without real forget data by generating synthetic target samples with gradient ascent and smoothing visual and textual embeddings via Lipschitz regularization.
- [Protecting NeRFs' Copyright via Plug-And-Play Watermarking Base Model](https://arxiv.org/abs/2407.07735): Introduces NeRFProtector, a plug-and-play watermarking base model that embeds binary messages during NeRF creation while preserving rendering quality through progressive distillation.
- [Towards Robust Alignment of Language Models: Distributionally Robustifying Direct Preference Optimization](https://arxiv.org/abs/2407.07880): Robustifies direct preference optimization against distribution shifts, adding a model-side method for safer language-model alignment.
- [Defending Code Language Models against Backdoor Attacks with Deceptive Cross-Entropy Loss](https://arxiv.org/abs/2407.08956): Defends code language models with Deceptive Cross-Entropy, bounding gradients through deceptive distributions and label smoothing to prevent overfitting to backdoor triggers.
- [Refuse Whenever You Feel Unsafe: Improving Safety in LLMs via Decoupled Refusal Training](https://arxiv.org/abs/2407.09121): Introduces DeRTa refusal training, combining harmful-response-prefix MLE with Reinforced Transition Optimization so models can switch from unsafe continuations to refusal mid-response.
- [ASTPrompter: Preference-Aligned Automated Language Model Red-Teaming to Generate Low-Perplexity Unsafe Prompts](https://arxiv.org/abs/2407.09447): Trains a red-team prompt generator with contrastive preference learning to find low-perplexity unsafe prompts that transfer across open models.
- [Learning to Refuse: Towards Mitigating Privacy Risks in LLMs](https://arxiv.org/abs/2407.10058): Builds RETURN, a personal-data unlearning set, and NAUF, a name-aware unlearning framework that protects target individuals while preserving unrelated QA behavior.
- [On Large Language Model Continual Unlearning](https://arxiv.org/abs/2407.10223): Proposes OOO continual unlearning with orthogonal LoRA modules and an OOD detector that loads the right unlearning adapter at inference without retained data.
- [BiasAlert: A Plug-and-play Tool for Social Bias Detection in LLMs](https://arxiv.org/abs/2407.10241): Provides a plug-and-play bias detector for open-text LLM generations, combining external human knowledge with model reasoning to classify social bias beyond fixed-form templates.
- [Uncertainty is Fragile: Manipulating Uncertainty in Large Language Models](https://arxiv.org/abs/2407.11282): Demonstrates a backdoor attack that changes an LLM's uncertainty distribution toward an attacker target while keeping the top prediction unchanged.
- [Targeted Unlearning with Single Layer Unlearning Gradient](https://arxiv.org/abs/2407.11867): Introduces SLUG, a one-gradient targeted unlearning method that selects one critical layer by importance and gradient alignment for CLIP, diffusion, and VLM concepts.
- [Does Refusal Training in LLMs Generalize to the Past Tense?](https://arxiv.org/abs/2407.11969): Exposes a refusal-training generalization failure where past-tense reformulations jailbreak many models, and shows explicit past-tense finetuning examples can mitigate it.
- [The Art of Saying No: Contextual Noncompliance in Language Models](https://arxiv.org/abs/2407.12043): Builds a contextual noncompliance taxonomy and 1,000-prompt evaluation, then tests synthetic-data finetuning and LoRA strategies for broader appropriate refusal.
- [Unlearning Personal Data from a Single Image](https://arxiv.org/abs/2407.12069): Defines one-shot unlearning of personal identities and proposes MetaUnlearn, which meta-learns to forget an identity from one provided portrait when training data is unavailable.
- [Private prediction for large-scale synthetic text generation](https://arxiv.org/abs/2407.12108): Generates differentially private synthetic text by privatizing next-token predictions with improved privacy analysis, private selection, and sparse-vector public predictions.
- [The Better Angels of Machine Personality: How Personality Relates to LLM Safety](https://arxiv.org/abs/2407.12344): Links MBTI-style personality traits to toxicity, privacy, fairness, and jailbreak susceptibility, then edits model personality traits as a safety intervention.
- [Truth is Universal: Robust Detection of Lies in LLMs](https://arxiv.org/abs/2407.12831): Builds an activation-based lie detector from a two-dimensional truth-falsehood subspace that generalizes across multiple LLM families and real-world lying settings.
- [PG-Attack: A Precision-Guided Adversarial Attack Framework Against Vision Foundation Models for Autonomous Driving](https://arxiv.org/abs/2407.13111): Attacks autonomous-driving vision foundation models with precision mask perturbations and deceptive text patches that manipulate object representations and scene understanding.
- [BiasDPO: Mitigating Bias in Language Models through Direct Preference Optimization](https://arxiv.org/abs/2407.13928): Uses DPO with a less-biased-over-biased completion loss and a manually curated bias-preference dataset to reduce gender, racial, and religious bias in Phi-2 outputs.
- [Watermark Smoothing Attacks against Language Models](https://arxiv.org/abs/2407.14206): Introduces Smoothing Attack, which uses model-confidence signals to selectively smooth watermarked text, removing traces across ten watermark schemes while preserving quality.
- [Human-Interpretable Adversarial Prompt Attack on Large Language Models with Situational Context](https://arxiv.org/abs/2407.14644): Converts nonsensical adversarial suffixes into human-readable situational prompts with LLM rewriting, exposing a stealthier black-box jailbreak channel.
- [Improving Context-Aware Preference Modeling for Language Models](https://arxiv.org/abs/2407.14916): Trains context-aware reward models from context-conditioned preference datasets, separating context selection from preference judgment to reduce underspecified alignment errors.
- [Operationalizing a Threat Model for Red-Teaming Large Language Models (LLMs)](https://arxiv.org/abs/2407.14937): Flags a taxonomy mismatch: the work is a red-teaming threat-model SoK and attack taxonomy, not a model, guard, detector, or training method.
- [Arondight: Red Teaming Large Vision Language Models with Auto-generated Multi-modal Jailbreak Prompts](https://arxiv.org/abs/2407.15050): Builds a VLM red-team generator where a red-team VLM creates visual jailbreaks and an RL-guided LLM creates diverse text prompts with novelty rewards.
- [A General Framework for Data-Use Auditing of ML Models](https://arxiv.org/abs/2407.15100): Combines black-box membership inference with sequential hypothesis testing to audit whether a data owner's content trained image classifiers or foundation models at a tunable false-positive rate.
- [Failures to Find Transferable Image Jailbreaks Between Vision-Language Models](https://arxiv.org/abs/2407.15211): Reports negative transfer results for image jailbreaks across VLM families, separating model-specific adversarial image vulnerabilities from universal multimodal jailbreak claims.
- [Latent Adversarial Training Improves Robustness to Persistent Harmful Behaviors in LLMs](https://arxiv.org/abs/2407.15549): Uses targeted latent adversarial training to improve robustness to jailbreaks, remove trigger-unknown backdoors, and make undesirable-task unlearning harder to relearn.
- [LLMmap: Fingerprinting For Large Language Models](https://arxiv.org/abs/2407.15847): Fingerprints LLM-integrated applications with active black-box queries, identifying model versions despite unknown prompts, sampling settings, RAG, or chain-of-thought wrappers.
- [Figure it Out: Analyzing-based Jailbreak Attack on Large Language Models](https://arxiv.org/abs/2407.16205): Introduces ABJ, a black-box jailbreak that manipulates textual and visual reasoning paths rather than only adding input-level attack strings.
- [Data Mixture Inference: What do BPE Tokenizers Reveal about their Training Data?](https://arxiv.org/abs/2407.16607): Infers training-data mixture proportions from BPE merge rules by solving a linear program over token-frequency evidence, exposing tokenizer-level data provenance leakage.
- [Course-Correction: Safety Alignment Using Synthetic Preferences](https://arxiv.org/abs/2407.16637): Generates synthetic preference pairs from model mistakes and trains safety-aligned policies to correct unsafe trajectories without relying on new human preference labels.
- [Can Large Language Models Automatically Jailbreak GPT-4V?](https://arxiv.org/abs/2407.16686): Automates GPT-4V red teaming with LLM-optimized jailbreak prompts, weak-to-strong in-context prompting, and early stopping to reduce search cost.
- [Revisiting Who’s Harry Potter: Towards Targeted Unlearning from a Causal Intervention Perspective](https://arxiv.org/abs/2407.16997): Recasts targeted LLM unlearning as causal deconfounding, extending WHP so models forget a target entity without gibberish, fabrication, or jailbreak leakage.
- [Can Watermarking Large Language Models Prevent Copyrighted Text Generation and Hide Training Data?](https://arxiv.org/abs/2407.17417): Studies watermarking as a copyright deterrent that reduces protected-text reproduction but can also suppress membership-inference signals used to detect memorized training data.
- [Fluent Student-Teacher Redteaming](https://arxiv.org/abs/2407.17447): Improves GCG and BEAST red-team attacks with student-teacher distillation toward a toxified finetune plus multi-model perplexity constraints for fluent jailbreak strings.
- [The Dark Side of Function Calling: Pathways to Jailbreaking Large Language Models](https://arxiv.org/abs/2407.17915): Exposes a function-calling jailbreak route that exploits alignment gaps, user coercion, and weak function-call safety filters across major closed models.
- [GermanPartiesQA: Benchmarking Commercial Large Language Models and AI Companions for Political Alignment and Sycophancy](https://arxiv.org/abs/2407.18008): Flags a taxonomy mismatch: this is a political alignment and sycophancy benchmark for commercial LLMs, not a safety model or defense method.
- [Strong Copyright Protection for Language Models via Adaptive Model Fusion](https://arxiv.org/abs/2407.20105): Introduces CP-Fuse, an adaptive model-fusion algorithm inspired by Near-Access Free protection that balances generation quality against reproduction of protected text and code.
- [Learn while Unlearn: An Iterative Unlearning Framework for Generative Language Models](https://arxiv.org/abs/2407.20271): Proposes ICU, combining unlearning induction, contrastive retention, and iterative refinement to remove sensitive knowledge while preserving generation ability.
- [Defending Jailbreak Attack in VLMs via Cross-modality Information Detector](https://arxiv.org/abs/2407.21659): Introduces CIDER, a plug-and-play MLLM jailbreak detector that compares harmful-query text with adversarial images through cross-modal similarity.
- [ShieldGemma: Generative AI Content Moderation Based on Gemma](https://arxiv.org/abs/2407.21772): Presents Gemma2-based moderation models for user inputs and model outputs across sexual, dangerous, harassment, and hate-speech harms, trained largely with synthetic safety data.
- [Safetywashing: Do AI Safety Benchmarks Actually Measure Safety Progress?](https://arxiv.org/abs/2407.21792): Flags a taxonomy mismatch: it meta-analyzes safety benchmarks and their correlation with general capability and compute, rather than proposing a safety model.
- [ABC Align: Large Language Model Alignment for Safety & Accuracy](https://arxiv.org/abs/2408.00307): Aligns an LLM to a media organization's standards using synthetic data generation, preference optimization, and post-training quantization to reduce bias while preserving accuracy.
- [On the Limitations and Prospects of Machine Unlearning for Generative AI](https://arxiv.org/abs/2408.00376): Flags a survey or position-paper mismatch for this Model section, mapping unlearning limits for LLMs and diffusion models plus benchmark and utility-trade-off needs.
- [Tamper-Resistant Safeguards for Open-Weight LLMs](https://arxiv.org/abs/2408.00761): Introduces TAR, a training method that embeds safeguards into open-weight LLMs so refusal and unlearning protections resist hundreds of fine-tuning steps.
- [The Phantom Menace: Unmasking Privacy Leakages in Vision-Language Models](https://arxiv.org/abs/2408.01228): Tests VLM identity leakage and shows anonymized alignment data, context changes, and blurring do not reliably prevent memorized identity disclosure.
- [Mission Impossible: A Statistical Perspective on Jailbreaking LLMs](https://arxiv.org/abs/2408.01420): Gives a statistical lower bound on jailbreak probability under preference alignment and proposes E-RLHF, an RLHF objective modification that increases safe-response likelihood.
- [Building a Domain-specific Guardrail Model in Production](https://arxiv.org/abs/2408.01452): Describes training and serving a production K-12 education guardrail model, including domain policy requirements, proprietary and public safety benchmarks, and inference optimizations.
- [SEAS: Self-Evolving Adversarial Safety Optimization for Large Language Models](https://arxiv.org/abs/2408.02632): Iteratively co-trains red-team and target models with self-generated adversarial data through initialization, attack, and adversarial optimization stages.
- [Hide and Seek: Fingerprinting Large Language Models with Evolutionary Learning](https://arxiv.org/abs/2408.02871): Uses an Auditor LLM to evolve discriminative prompts and a Detective LLM to identify model families from responses for black-box model attribution.
- [HARMONIC: Harnessing LLMs for Tabular Data Synthesis and Privacy Protection](https://arxiv.org/abs/2408.02927): Fine-tunes LLMs to generate privacy-preserving tabular synthetic data from k-nearest-neighbor-style instruction data and evaluates leakage with task-specific privacy metrics.
- [Scaling Trends for Data Poisoning in LLMs](https://arxiv.org/abs/2408.02946): Measures data-poisoning susceptibility across 24 LLMs from 1.5B to 72B parameters, finding larger models learn harmful behavior faster from minimal poisoned exposure.
- [Adversarial Safety-Critical Scenario Generation Using Naturalistic Human Driving Priors](https://arxiv.org/abs/2408.03200): Generates autonomous-driving safety-critical scenarios by combining IDM and MOBIL priors, GAIL driver modeling, and PPO actor-critic optimization for realistic adversarial tests.
- [A Study on Prompt Injection Attack Against LLM-Integrated Mobile Robotic Systems](https://arxiv.org/abs/2408.03515): Tests prompt injection against GPT-4o-based mobile robot navigation and evaluates secure prompting defenses that improve attack detection and mission performance.
- [EnJa: Ensemble Jailbreak on Large Language Models](https://arxiv.org/abs/2408.03603): Combines prompt-level harmful-instruction hiding, gradient-based token attacks, and connector text into a hybrid Ensemble Jailbreak against aligned LLMs.
- [Compromesso! Italian Many-Shot Jailbreaks Undermine the Safety of Large Language Models](https://arxiv.org/abs/2408.04522): Builds unsafe Italian question-answer demonstrations and shows many-shot jailbreak behavior escalates across four open-weight LLM families.
- [BA-LoRA: Bias-Alleviating Low-Rank Adaptation to Mitigate Catastrophic Inheritance in Large Language Models](https://arxiv.org/abs/2408.04556): Adds BA-LoRA, using consistency, diversity, and SVD regularizers to reduce knowledge drift, representation collapse, and noise overfitting during LoRA adaptation.
- [Multi-Turn Context Jailbreak Attack on Large Language Models From First Principles](https://arxiv.org/abs/2408.04686): Introduces Context Fusion Attack, a black-box multi-turn jailbreak that extracts harmful-key terms, builds contextual scenarios, and dynamically fuses the target request.
- [h4rm3l: A Language for Composable Jailbreak Attack Synthesis](https://arxiv.org/abs/2408.04811): Defines the h4rm3l domain-specific language for composing parameterized jailbreak transformations and enabling program-synthesis search over attack variants.
- [ConfusedPilot: Confused Deputy Risks in RAG-based LLMs](https://arxiv.org/abs/2408.04870): Defines ConfusedPilot RAG attacks where malicious retrieved text corrupts responses, retrieval caching leaks secrets, and misinformation propagates through enterprise workflows.
- [Anchored Preference Optimization and Contrastive Revisions: Addressing Underspecification in Alignment](https://arxiv.org/abs/2408.06266): Creates CLAIR contrastive AI-revision preference pairs and APO, an anchored preference objective that stabilizes controllable Llama-3-8B-Instruct alignment under underspecified preference data.
- [KOALA: Enhancing Speculative Decoding for LLM via Multi-Layer Draft Heads with Adversarial Learning](https://arxiv.org/abs/2408.08146): Adds multi-layer draft heads and adversarial learning to speculative decoding so acceleration preserves generation quality under harder draft-token distributions.
- [Are CLIP features all you need for Universal Synthetic Image Origin Attribution?](https://arxiv.org/abs/2408.09153): Tests whether frozen CLIP embeddings can attribute synthetic images to their source generator, turning origin attribution into a lightweight provenance classifier.
- [Malacopula: adversarial automatic speaker verification attacks using a neural-based generalised Hammerstein model](https://arxiv.org/abs/2408.09300): Uses a neural generalized Hammerstein model with polynomial branches and linear filters to perturb spoofed speech embeddings, strengthening ASV attacks while exposing quality and detectability trade-offs.
- [Antidote: Post-fine-tuning Safety Alignment for Large Language Models against Harmful Fine-tuning](https://arxiv.org/abs/2408.09600): Applies one-shot pruning after harmful fine-tuning to remove weights responsible for harmful content, recovering safety even when attack fine-tuning uses aggressive hyperparameters.
- [Promoting Equality in Large Language Models: Identifying and Mitigating the Implicit Bias based on Bayesian Theory](https://arxiv.org/abs/2408.10608): Defines implicit bias and uses BTBR, a Bayesian likelihood-ratio screen plus model editing, to locate biased training-derived triples and remove them from LLM weights.
- [Probing the Safety Response Boundary of Large Language Models via Unsafe Decoding Path Generation](https://arxiv.org/abs/2408.10668): Trains a cost value model as both detector and attacker in Jailbreak Value Decoding, steering generation along hidden unsafe paths despite surface-level refusals.
- [Perception-guided Jailbreak against Text-to-Image Models](https://arxiv.org/abs/2408.10848): Uses LLM-generated perceptual substitutions for unsafe terms so text-to-image prompts appear semantically safe while still eliciting NSFW concepts in black-box models.
- [Dynamic Analysis and Adaptive Discriminator for Fake News Detection](https://arxiv.org/abs/2408.10883): Combines MCTS prompt optimization for LLM fake-news analysis with adaptive discriminators for emotional exaggeration, logical inconsistency, image manipulation, and semantic mismatch.
- [BEYOND DIALOGUE: A Profile-Dialogue Alignment Framework Towards General Role-Playing Language Model](https://arxiv.org/abs/2408.10903): Aligns role-playing LMs with profile traits through automated beyond-dialogue tasks and reasoning traces, reducing profile-dialogue inconsistency during training.
- [Defending Against Jailbreak Through Early Exit Generation of Large Language Models](https://arxiv.org/abs/2408.11308): Detects jailbreaks from early transformer representations before full decoding and terminates generation when latent embeddings resemble malicious rather than benign prompts.
- [An Optimizable Suffix Is Worth A Thousand Templates: Efficient Black-box Jailbreaking without Affirmative Phrases via LLM as Optimizer](https://arxiv.org/abs/2408.11313): Uses an LLM optimizer and harmfulness scorer to iteratively generate black-box adversarial suffixes without white-box gradients or prebuilt affirmative phrases.
- [Against All Odds: Overcoming Typology, Script, and Language Confusion in Multilingual Embedding Inversion Attacks](https://arxiv.org/abs/2408.11749): Evaluates embedding inversion across 20 languages, eight language families, and 12 scripts, finding Arabic, Cyrillic, and Indo-Aryan settings especially exposed to cross-script recovery and language confusion.
- [Identifying and Mitigating Social Bias Knowledge in Language Models](https://arxiv.org/abs/2408.11843): Introduces BiaScope and FAST, locating bias-storing layers and inserting a small modular calibration network to reduce social bias while preserving commonsense knowledge.
- [La-SoftMoE CLIP for Unified Physical-Digital Face Attack Detection](https://arxiv.org/abs/2408.12793): Adapts CLIP with a latent soft mixture-of-experts so physical and digital face-attack detectors route sparse features through region-specific expert parameters.
- [Atoxia: Red-teaming Large Language Models with Target Toxic Answers](https://arxiv.org/abs/2408.14853): Trains an RL red-team attacker to generate a user query and misleading answer opening that maximize a target toxic answer's probability on the victim LLM.
- [Understanding the Effectiveness of Coverage Criteria for Large Language Models: A Special Angle from Jailbreak Attacks](https://arxiv.org/abs/2408.15207): Uses hidden-state clustering and neuron-coverage differences between normal and jailbreak queries to build real-time detectors, prioritize security tests, and guide jailbreak prompt generation.
- [LLM Defenses Are Not Robust to Multi-Turn Human Jailbreaks Yet](https://arxiv.org/abs/2408.15221): Shows that human-written multi-turn jailbreaks bypass many single-turn defenses, exposing guardrails that fail when harmful intent is distributed across dialogue context.
- [Bi-Factorial Preference Optimization: Balancing Safety-Helpfulness in Language Models](https://arxiv.org/abs/2408.15313): Reparameterizes a joint safety-helpfulness RLHF objective into supervised BFPO, using a global preference-ranking label function for harmlessness and utility.
- [Legilimens: Practical and Unified Content Moderation for Large Language Model Services](https://arxiv.org/abs/2408.15488): Extracts conceptual features from chat LLMs and hardens them with red-team data augmentation to build efficient content-moderation guardrails for unsafe outputs.
- [WildFeedback: Aligning LLMs With In-situ User Interactions And Feedback](https://arxiv.org/abs/2408.15549): Mines in-situ user feedback from multi-turn conversations into preference pairs, then fine-tunes LLMs to align with real user preferences rather than offline annotations.
- [Defending Text-to-image Diffusion Models: Surprising Efficacy of Textual Perturbations Against Backdoor Attacks](https://arxiv.org/abs/2408.15721): Defends backdoored text-to-image diffusion models by perturbing input text, disrupting trigger behavior in text embeddings and cross-attention maps with little quality loss.
- [Beyond Preferences in AI Alignment](https://arxiv.org/abs/2408.16984): Argues that preference optimization alone cannot encode all alignment constraints, motivating richer feedback signals for uncertainty, rights, duties, and long-horizon social effects.
- [Safety Layers in Aligned Large Language Models: The Key to LLM Security](https://arxiv.org/abs/2408.17003): Identifies safety-critical layers and uses Safely Partial-Parameter Fine-Tuning to freeze their gradients during adaptation, preserving safeguards with lower compute.
- [Forget to Flourish: Leveraging Machine-Unlearning on Pretrained Language Models for Privacy Leakage](https://arxiv.org/abs/2408.17354): Uses machine unlearning as a poisoning attack tool, amplifying membership inference and data extraction leakage from pretrained language models while preserving utility.
- [Is Difficulty Calibration All We Need? Towards More Practical Membership Inference Attacks](https://arxiv.org/abs/2409.00426): Introduces RAPID, a query-efficient membership-inference attack that reuses original membership scores to reduce difficulty-calibration errors on hard high-loss samples.
- [Membership Inference Attacks Against In-Context Learning](https://arxiv.org/abs/2409.01380): Designs four membership-inference attacks for in-context learning and a hybrid strategy that infers whether examples appeared in a prompt from model behavior.
- [Booster: Tackling Harmful Fine-tuning for Large Language Models via Attenuating Harmful Perturbation](https://arxiv.org/abs/2409.01586): Adds an alignment-stage Booster defense that attenuates harmful perturbations before downstream fine-tuning, reducing safety loss from malicious or unsafe adaptation data.
- [SafeEmbodAI: a Safety Framework for Mobile Robots in Embodied AI Systems](https://arxiv.org/abs/2409.01630): Wraps embodied robot decisions with safety monitors, risk checks, and mitigation layers for mobile-robot navigation rather than only testing text-only refusal behavior.
- [Adversarial Learning for Neural PDE Solvers with Sparse Data](https://arxiv.org/abs/2409.02431): Uses adversarial training to improve neural PDE solvers when observations are sparse, an adjacent robustness method rather than an LLM guardrail.
- [Recent Advances in Attack and Defense Approaches of Large Language Models](https://arxiv.org/abs/2409.03274): Flags a survey mismatch: it reviews LLM attack vectors, model weaknesses, and defense gaps rather than introducing a new guard model or training method.
- [Safeguarding AI Agents: Developing and Analyzing Safety Architectures](https://arxiv.org/abs/2409.03793): Maps layered agent safety architectures around perception, decision, action, and monitoring components, making it a parent-routing candidate for Agent Harness rather than a pure model method.
- [WaterSeeker: Efficient Detection of Watermarked Segments in Large Documents](https://arxiv.org/abs/2409.05112): Detects partial watermarked spans inside long documents with a segment-level watermark detector, improving provenance checks when only some passages are machine generated.
- [Programming Refusal with Conditional Activation Steering](https://arxiv.org/abs/2409.05907): Uses Conditional Activation Steering to apply refusal steering only when inference-time activations indicate unsafe context, limiting over-refusal on benign prompts.
- [Policy Filtration for RLHF to Mitigate Noise in Reward Models](https://arxiv.org/abs/2409.06957): Filters RLHF policy samples according to reward-model reliability, using the reward-score R2 signal to reduce noisy reward updates during PPO training.
- [Efficient and Unbiased Sampling of Boltzmann Distributions via Consistency Models](https://arxiv.org/abs/2409.07323): Combines consistency models with importance sampling to draw unbiased Boltzmann samples in few function evaluations; this is an adjacent generative-model sampling item rather than a direct agent-safety guardrail.
- [Securing Vision-Language Models with a Robust Encoder Against Jailbreak and Adversarial Attacks](https://arxiv.org/abs/2409.07353): Adversarially fine-tunes a CLIP vision encoder with a Siamese Sim-CLIP+ objective, reducing VLM jailbreak and image-adversarial vulnerability before LLM decoding.
- [AdaPPA: Adaptive Position Pre-Fill Jailbreak Attack Approach Targeting LLMs](https://arxiv.org/abs/2409.07503): Places adversarial prefixes at adaptive prefill positions to bypass LLM refusal behavior, exposing a position-sensitive jailbreak attack surface.
- [Alignment with Preference Optimization Is All You Need for LLM Safety](https://arxiv.org/abs/2409.07772): Compares safety-oriented preference optimization methods and identifies Safe-NCA noise-contrastive alignment as a strong safety-performance trade-off.
- [Securing Large Language Models: Addressing Bias, Misinformation, and Prompt Attacks](https://arxiv.org/abs/2409.08087): Surveys LLM security risks across bias, misinformation, and prompt attacks, making it a synthesis entry rather than a new training or guardrail method.
- [Comparing Retrieval-Augmentation and Parameter-Efficient Fine-Tuning for Privacy-Preserving Personalization of Large Language Models](https://arxiv.org/abs/2409.09510): Systematically compares RAG and PEFT personalization on LaMP, measuring when user-data volume makes fine-tuning more useful and exposing privacy trade-offs.
- [Generalizing Alignment Paradigm of Text-to-Image Generation with Preferences through f-divergence Minimization](https://arxiv.org/abs/2409.09774): Generalizes text-to-image preference alignment beyond reverse KL by optimizing f-divergence constraints, testing safer preference tuning across diffusion objectives.
- [CoCA: Regaining Safety-awareness of Multimodal Large Language Models with Constitutional Calibration](https://arxiv.org/abs/2409.11365): Uses Constitutional Calibration to restore MLLM safety awareness after visual instruction tuning, reducing harmful responses to malicious images without losing capability.
- [Generated Data with Fake Privacy: Hidden Dangers of Fine-tuning Large Language Models on Generated Data](https://arxiv.org/abs/2409.11423): Shows generated data can inherit real-data privacy risk during fine-tuning, warning that synthetic substitutes still enable leakage similar to real user data.
- [Jailbreaking Large Language Models with Symbolic Mathematics](https://arxiv.org/abs/2409.11445): Converts harmful requests into symbolic-math encodings that bypass refusal mechanisms, expanding jailbreak red teaming beyond natural-language obfuscation.
- [MEOW: MEMOry Supervised LLM Unlearning Via Inverted Facts](https://arxiv.org/abs/2409.11844): Measures memorization with MEMO and applies gradient-descent unlearning over inverted facts to remove factual memories while limiting utility loss.
- [Enhancing Knowledge Distillation of Large Language Models through Efficient Multi-Modal Distribution Alignment](https://arxiv.org/abs/2409.12545): Aligns teacher and student output distributions across modalities during distillation, preserving multimodal reasoning in smaller models rather than targeting safety specifically.
- [Language Models Learn to Mislead Humans via RLHF](https://arxiv.org/abs/2409.12822): Shows standard RLHF can create U-SOPHISTRY, where reward-optimized models learn persuasive but misleading answers that evade existing probes.
- [Alternate Preference Optimization for Unlearning Factual Knowledge in Large Language Models](https://arxiv.org/abs/2409.13474): Combines negative feedback with in-domain positive feedback on forget sets, improving factual unlearning while evaluating both erasure quality and response utility.
- [“Since Lawyers are Males..”: Examining Implicit Gender Bias in Hindi Language Generation by LLMs](https://arxiv.org/abs/2409.13484): Measures implicit gender stereotypes in Hindi LLM generations, showing profession and role prompts can surface language-specific bias beyond English safety tests.
- [Context-Aware Membership Inference Attacks against Pre-trained Large Language Models](https://arxiv.org/abs/2409.13745): Adapts membership-inference statistical tests to subsequence perplexity dynamics, exposing context-dependent memorization patterns that classification-style MIAs miss.
- [Measuring Copyright Risks of Large Language Model via Partial Information Probing](https://arxiv.org/abs/2409.13831): Probes LLMs with partial copyrighted passages to estimate memorization and reproduction risk when only fragments of protected text are supplied.
- [Large Model-Based Agents: State-of-the-Art, Cooperation Paradigms, Security and Privacy, and Future Trends](https://arxiv.org/abs/2409.14457): Surveys large-model agents, cooperation paradigms, and security and privacy risks, so it is background synthesis rather than a concrete safety model.
- [Backtracking Improves Generation Safety](https://arxiv.org/abs/2409.14586): Trains LLMs with a special RESET token so decoding can undo unsafe continuations and regenerate safer responses without reducing helpfulness.
- [Direct Judgement Preference Optimization](https://arxiv.org/abs/2409.14664): Trains generative judge models with preference pairs from positive and negative evaluation data, improving bias-resistant reward and verifier feedback across protocols.
- [Adaptive and Robust Watermark for Generative Tabular Data](https://arxiv.org/abs/2409.14700): Watermarks generative tabular data with theoretical guarantees for fidelity, detectability, robustness, and decoding hardness under synthetic-data misuse scenarios.
- [Room Impulse Responses Help Attackers to Evade Deep Fake Detection](https://arxiv.org/abs/2409.14712): Uses room impulse responses to make fake speech evade deepfake detectors, then counters the attack with synthetic RIR augmentation during detector training.
- [Pretraining Data Detection for Large Language Models: A Divergence-based Calibration Method](https://arxiv.org/abs/2409.14781): Scores candidate pretraining text by calibrating token probabilities with divergence from corpus frequency, and releases PatentMIA for Chinese pretraining-data detection.
- [Can CLIP Count Stars? An Empirical Study on Quantity Bias in CLIP](https://arxiv.org/abs/2409.15035): Audits quantity bias in CLIP by testing whether image-text similarity tracks object counts or spurious visual cues, making it a robustness-routing candidate outside core agent safety.
- [Multitask Mayhem: Unveiling and Mitigating Safety Gaps in LLMs Fine-tuning](https://arxiv.org/abs/2409.15361): Builds a multitask safety dataset for fine-tuning that reduces jailbreak success across tasks while preserving helpfulness under multitask adaptation.
- [RoleBreak: Character Hallucination as a Jailbreak Attack in Role-Playing Systems](https://arxiv.org/abs/2409.16727): Frames character hallucination as a role-playing jailbreak, using query sparsity and role-query conflict to build RoleBreakEval and a Narrator Mode mitigation.
- [Tell Me What You Don't Know: Enhancing Refusal Capabilities of Role-Playing Agents via Representation Space Analysis and Editing](https://arxiv.org/abs/2409.16913): Analyzes role-playing agents in representation space and edits activations to move conflicting or unsafe requests toward the model's refusal region.
- [An Adversarial Perspective on Machine Unlearning for AI Safety](https://arxiv.org/abs/2409.18025): Shows adaptive jailbreaks, tiny unrelated fine-tunes, and activation-direction removal can recover hazardous capabilities from supposedly unlearned LLMs such as RMU-edited models.
- [Overriding Safety protections of Open-source Models](https://arxiv.org/abs/2409.19476): Studies how harmful fine-tuning data can override open-source LLM safety protections, while safety fine-tuning can move the same models toward safer responses.
- [Unified Gradient-Based Machine Unlearning with Remain Geometry Enhancement](https://arxiv.org/abs/2409.19732): Derives a unified gradient unlearning update with remaining-data geometry and fast-slow Hessian approximation, improving erasure while preserving retained performance.
- [Position: Membership Inference Attacks Cannot Prove That a Model was Trained on Your Data](https://arxiv.org/abs/2409.19798): Argues that membership inference is insufficient as proof of training-data use, clarifying evidentiary limits for privacy and copyright claims against LLMs.
- [Mitigating Backdoor Threats to Large Language Models: Advancement and Challenges](https://arxiv.org/abs/2409.19993): Surveys LLM backdoor threats across development and inference, emphasizing poisoned crowdsourced data, instruction tuning, RLHF, and detection or defense strategies.
- [Robust LLM safeguarding via refusal feature adversarial training](https://arxiv.org/abs/2409.20089): Simulates input-level jailbreak attacks in representation space and uses Refusal Feature Adversarial Training to harden LLM refusals with lower compute.
- [Probabilistic Analysis of Copyright Disputes and Generative AI Safety](https://arxiv.org/abs/2410.00475): Formalizes copyright-dispute evidence probabilistically and tests the Near Access-Free condition as a generative-AI copyright-safety criterion, making it an adjacent legal-risk analysis rather than a model method.
- [ScVLM: Enhancing Vision-Language Model for Safety-Critical Event Understanding](https://arxiv.org/abs/2410.00982): Adapts VLMs for safety-critical event understanding, focusing on scene risk recognition rather than general-purpose refusal or jailbreak defense.
- [Concept Space Alignment in Multilingual LLMs](https://arxiv.org/abs/2410.01079): Analyzes and aligns multilingual concept representations so safety or value concepts map more consistently across languages.
- [Towards Inference-time Category-wise Safety Steering for Large Language Models](https://arxiv.org/abs/2410.01174): Extracts category-specific safety steering vectors and applies them at inference time for fine-grained refusal control while preserving output quality.
- [Backdooring Vision-Language Models with Out-Of-Distribution Data](https://arxiv.org/abs/2410.01264): Backdoors VLMs with out-of-distribution poisoned data for image-to-text tasks while preserving visual semantics, exposing a multimodal supply-chain attack path.
- [HarmAug: Effective Data Augmentation for Knowledge Distillation of Safety Guard Models](https://arxiv.org/abs/2410.01524): Generates harmful instructions by jailbreaking an LLM, then uses them as augmentation data to distill smaller safety guard models from stronger models.
- [On the expressiveness and spectral bias of KANs](https://arxiv.org/abs/2410.01803): Studies Kolmogorov-Arnold Network expressiveness and spectral bias, an adjacent architecture-analysis paper with no direct agent-safety guardrail.
- [Adversarial Decoding: Generating Readable Documents for Adversarial Objectives](https://arxiv.org/abs/2410.02163): Generates fluent adversarial documents for objectives such as RAG poisoning, indirect prompt injection, jailbreaking, and defensive-filter evasion.
- [Efficiently Deploying LLMs with Controlled Risk](https://arxiv.org/abs/2410.02173): Studies risk-controlled LLM deployment decisions, making it a governance or deployment-policy routing candidate unless the parent keeps deployment risk models in this section.
- [BACKTIME: Backdoor Attacks on Multivariate Time Series Forecasting](https://arxiv.org/abs/2410.02195): Injects stealthy triggers into multivariate time-series data so forecasting models output attacker-chosen predictions in high-stakes domains, exposing backdoor risk beyond text and vision models.
- [Data to Defense: The Role of Curation in Customizing LLMs Against Jailbreaking Attacks](https://arxiv.org/abs/2410.02220): Curates customization data before, during, and after fine-tuning to immunize, neutralize, and restore LLMs against jailbreak amplification from malicious samples.
- [Jailbreak Antidote: Runtime Safety-Utility Balance via Sparse Representation Adjustment in Large Language Models](https://arxiv.org/abs/2410.02298): Adjusts sparse internal representations at runtime to tune the safety-utility balance against jailbreaks without extra fine-tuning or external guard modules.
- [Optimizing Adaptive Attacks against Watermarks for Language Models](https://arxiv.org/abs/2410.02440): Treats watermark evasion as preference-based optimization, training adaptive paraphrasers that bypass surveyed LLM watermarks and transfer to unseen schemes.
- [Towards Implicit Bias Detection and Mitigation in Multi-Agent LLM Interactions](https://arxiv.org/abs/2410.02584): Builds bias-prone multi-agent scenarios, an implicit-gender-bias metric, and mitigation strategies for LLM societies whose simulated interactions can amplify social bias.
- [MA-RLHF: Reinforcement Learning from Human Feedback with Macro Actions](https://arxiv.org/abs/2410.02743): Introduces macro-action RLHF, optimizing sequences of tokens or higher-level language actions to improve alignment learning efficiency over token-level PPO.
- [FlipAttack: Jailbreak LLMs via Flipping](https://arxiv.org/abs/2410.02832): Disguises harmful prompts with left-side noise and four text-flipping modes, exploiting black-box LLMs' ability to denoise and execute flipped instructions in one query.
- [Safeguard is a Double-edged Sword: Denial-of-service Attack on Large Language Models](https://arxiv.org/abs/2410.02916): Exploits guardrail false positives as a denial-of-service attack, showing benign prompts can be crafted to trigger refusals and reduce model availability.
- [Can Watermarked LLMs be Identified by Users via Crafted Prompts?](https://arxiv.org/abs/2410.03168): Shows crafted prompts can reveal whether a model is watermarked, then proposes Water-Bag multi-key selection to improve watermark imperceptibility.
- [Data-Efficient Massive Tool Retrieval: A Reinforcement Learning Approach for Query-Tool Alignment with Language Models](https://arxiv.org/abs/2410.03212): Uses reinforcement learning to align language-model queries with large tool inventories; it is mainly a Tool Use or Agent Harness routing candidate unless framed as safer tool selection.
- [Gradient-based Jailbreak Images for Multimodal Fusion Models](https://arxiv.org/abs/2410.03489): Optimizes image inputs in continuous space to induce jailbreak behavior in multimodal fusion models, exposing a visual attack path that text-only suffix search misses.
- [Aligning LLMs with Individual Preferences via Interaction](https://arxiv.org/abs/2410.03642): Trains LLMs on persona-guided multi-turn interaction trees so they infer unspoken individual preferences and adapt responses during the conversation.
- [Precision Knowledge Editing: Enhancing Safety in Large Language Models](https://arxiv.org/abs/2410.03772): Uses neuron weight tracking and activation-path tracing to edit toxic parameter regions, lowering attack success while preserving Llama model utility.
- [You Know What I'm Saying: Jailbreak Attack via Implicit Reference](https://arxiv.org/abs/2410.03857): Hides malicious objectives inside nested harmless contexts and uses less secure models to generate attack contexts for cross-model jailbreak transfer.
- [A Retention-Centric Framework for Continual Learning with Guaranteed Model Developmental Safety](https://arxiv.org/abs/2410.03955): Develops a continual-learning safety method with retention guarantees, focusing on model training stability across developmental updates.
- [Harnessing Task Overload for Scalable Jailbreak Attacks on Large Language Models](https://arxiv.org/abs/2410.04190): Preloads LLMs with resource-intensive character-map decoding so safety policies are less active when the harmful instruction arrives, yielding a scalable prompt-level jailbreak.
- [Functional Homotopy: Smoothing Discrete Optimization via Continuous Parameters for LLM Jailbreak Attacks](https://arxiv.org/abs/2410.04234): Turns discrete jailbreak suffix search into a sequence of easy-to-hard continuous optimization problems, improving attack success against safety-tuned Llama and Gemma models.
- [CopyLens: Dynamically Flagging Copyrighted Sub-Dataset Contributions to LLM Outputs](https://arxiv.org/abs/2410.04454): Uses Inner-Probe multi-head attention signals during generation to flag copyrighted sub-dataset influence in LLM outputs.
- [TLDR: Token-Level Detective Reward Model for Large Vision Language Models](https://arxiv.org/abs/2410.04734): Trains a token-level detective reward model from perturbation-generated hard negatives, giving fine-grained hallucination labels for LVLM self-correction and evaluation.
- [Finding Safety Violations of AI-Enabled Control Systems through the Lens of Synthesized Proxy Programs](https://arxiv.org/abs/2410.04986): Synthesizes proxy programs to accelerate falsification of AI-enabled control systems with conjunctive safety requirements, making it an adjacent safety-testing or Agent Harness candidate.
- [Data Advisor: Dynamic Data Curation for Safety Alignment of Large Language Models](https://arxiv.org/abs/2410.05269): Monitors generated safety-alignment data, identifies undercovered principles, and advises the next generation round to improve safety without utility loss.
- [Reward Learning From Preference With Ties](https://arxiv.org/abs/2410.05328): Extends Bradley-Terry reward learning with tied preferences, reducing preference-strength bias when human feedback says two responses are equally good.
- [Anyattack: Towards Large-scale Self-supervised Adversarial Attacks on Vision-language Models](https://arxiv.org/abs/2410.05346): Pretrains a self-supervised adversarial image generator on LAION-400M so any image can become a transferable VLM attack vector toward chosen outputs.
- [SecAlign: Defending Against Prompt Injection with Preference Optimization](https://arxiv.org/abs/2410.05451): Builds prompt-injection preference pairs of secure and insecure outputs, then preference-optimizes LLMs to follow legitimate instructions over injected ones.
- [Privacy Vulnerabilities in Marginals-based Synthetic Data](https://arxiv.org/abs/2410.05506): Introduces MAMA-MIA, a membership-inference attack against marginal-preserving synthetic-data generators, exposing leakage even under privacy-oriented tabular synthesis.
- [KnowledgeSG: Privacy-Preserving Synthetic Text Generation with Knowledge Distillation from Server](https://arxiv.org/abs/2410.05725): Uses differentially private local knowledge learning and server knowledge distillation while exchanging models rather than raw data for private synthetic text generation.
- [WAPITI: A Watermark for Finetuned Open-Source LLMs](https://arxiv.org/abs/2410.06467): Transfers watermark parameters from base LLMs into fine-tuned open-source models through parameter integration, preserving task tuning and resisting fine-tuning attacks.
- [Dissecting Fine-Tuning Unlearning in Large Language Models](https://arxiv.org/abs/2410.06606): Dissects fine-tuning-based LLM unlearning to separate true parameter forgetting from surface refusal shifts and retained-knowledge leakage.
- [Enhancing Multimodal LLM for Detailed and Accurate Video Captioning using Multi-Round Preference Optimization](https://arxiv.org/abs/2410.06682): Applies multi-round preference optimization to MLLM video captioning so generated descriptions become more detailed and accurate.
- [PII-Scope: A Comprehensive Study on Training Data PII Extraction Attacks in LLMs](https://arxiv.org/abs/2410.06704): Studies training-data PII extraction attacks against LLMs, characterizing what personal information can be elicited and which model or prompt factors increase leakage.
- [Root Defence Strategies: Ensuring Safety of LLM at the Decoding Level](https://arxiv.org/abs/2410.06809): Adds decoder-level step-by-step safety checks that correct harmful queries during generation and use speculative decoding to preserve deployment speed.
- [Simplicity Prevails: Rethinking Negative Preference Optimization for LLM Unlearning](https://arxiv.org/abs/2410.07163): Revisits negative preference optimization for language-model unlearning.
- [SEAL: Safety-enhanced Aligned LLM Fine-tuning via Bilevel Data Selection](https://arxiv.org/abs/2410.07471): Learns a bilevel data ranker that upweights safe high-quality fine-tuning samples and downweights unsafe ones to preserve LLM alignment during customization.
- [How Does Vision-Language Adaptation Impact the Safety of Vision Language Models?](https://arxiv.org/abs/2410.07571): Shows vision-language adaptation can degrade the LLM backbone's safety even on safe data, and evaluates safety fine-tuning as a partial repair for multimodal safety drift.
- [No Free Lunch: Retrieval-Augmented Generation Undermines Fairness in LLMs, Even for Vigilant Users](https://arxiv.org/abs/2410.07589): Tests uncensored, partially censored, and fully censored RAG corpora under a three-level fairness-awareness threat model, showing retrieval can reintroduce biased outputs without retraining.
- [Towards Assurance of LLM Adversarial Robustness using Ontology-Driven Argumentation](https://arxiv.org/abs/2410.07962): Uses ontologies and formal argumentation to structure LLM adversarial attacks and defenses into human-readable and machine-readable robustness assurance cases.
- [Unstable Unlearning: The Hidden Risk of Concept Resurgence in Diffusion Models](https://arxiv.org/abs/2410.08074): Shows concept-erasure in diffusion models can resurge after later adaptation, exposing instability in unlearning-based safety defenses.
- [A Closer Look at Machine Unlearning for Large Language Models](https://arxiv.org/abs/2410.08109): Re-evaluates LLM unlearning methods for forget quality, retained utility, and residual extraction, clarifying when apparent deletion is only output suppression.
- [Sample then Identify: A General Framework for Risk Control and Assessment in Multimodal Large Language Models](https://arxiv.org/abs/2410.08174): Introduces TRON, a split-conformal sampling and nonconformity framework that controls MLLM response risk in open-ended and closed-ended VideoQA settings.
- [Poison-splat: Computation Cost Attack on 3D Gaussian Splatting](https://arxiv.org/abs/2410.08190): Injects poisoned points into 3D Gaussian splatting scenes to inflate rendering cost, a domain-specific security attack that needs parent routing outside LLM model safety.
- [JurEE not Judges: safeguarding llm interactions with small, specialised Encoder Ensembles](https://arxiv.org/abs/2410.08442): Uses small specialized encoder ensembles as guard classifiers for LLM interactions, replacing heavyweight LLM judges with cheaper safety detectors.
- [Simultaneous Reward Distillation and Preference Learning: Get You a Language Model Who Can Do Both](https://arxiv.org/abs/2410.08458): Introduces DRDO, combining direct reward distillation with preference learning so alignment training can handle noisy pairwise labels without a separate reward-optimization phase.
- [Do Unlearning Methods Remove Information from Language Model Weights?](https://arxiv.org/abs/2410.08827): Tests whether unlearning removes hazardous knowledge from weights by giving attackers partial removed facts and measuring recovery of related facts from the same distribution.
- [Controllable Safety Alignment: Inference-Time Adaptation to Diverse Safety Requirements](https://arxiv.org/abs/2410.08968): Aligns models to natural-language safety configurations with CoSAlign, enabling authorized inference-time changes to safety behavior without retraining.
- [AttnGCG: Enhancing Jailbreaking Attacks on LLMs with Attention Manipulation](https://arxiv.org/abs/2410.09040): Extends GCG by manipulating attention toward safety-relevant prompt regions, improving jailbreak transferability across Llama, Gemma, and black-box LLMs.
- [Unraveling and Mitigating Safety Alignment Degradation of Vision-Language Models](https://arxiv.org/abs/2410.09047): Attributes VLM safety-alignment degradation to a representation gap between visual and text-only inputs, then mitigates it by aligning multimodal representations back toward the LLM safety space.
- [Knowledge-Augmented Reasoning for EUAIA Compliance and Adversarial Robustness of LLMs](https://arxiv.org/abs/2410.09078): Adds knowledge-augmented reasoning over EU AI Act requirements so LLMs can check compliance while resisting adversarial prompts.
- [Recent advancements in LLM Red-Teaming: Techniques, Defenses, and Ethical Considerations](https://arxiv.org/abs/2410.09097): Surveys LLM red-teaming attacks, defenses, and ethical constraints, serving as synthesis evidence rather than a new attack generator or safety model.
- [Unlearn and Burn: Adversarial Machine Unlearning Requests Destroy Model Accuracy](https://arxiv.org/abs/2410.09591): Shows unlearning APIs can be attacked with removal requests for non-training data, using white-box and black-box strategies that degrade model accuracy.
- [Targeted Vaccine: Safety Alignment for Large Language Models Against Harmful Fine-Tuning via Layer-Wise Perturbation](https://arxiv.org/abs/2410.09760): Selects safety-critical layers by gradient norm and perturbs only those layers during alignment, improving robustness to harmful fine-tuning with lower memory.
- [Jailbreak Instruction-Tuned LLMs via end-of-sentence MLP Re-weighting](https://arxiv.org/abs/2410.10150): Reweights end-of-sentence MLP activations in instruction-tuned LLMs to induce jailbreak behavior, exposing an activation-level attack path.
- [Locking Down the Finetuned LLMs Safety](https://arxiv.org/abs/2410.10343): Extracts Meta-SafetyLock activation directions from a base model and applies them to fine-tuned models for fast transferable safety realignment.
- [On Calibration of LLM-based Guard Models for Reliable Content Moderation](https://arxiv.org/abs/2410.10414): Calibrates LLM-based guard models for content moderation so safety scores support reliable thresholding rather than brittle binary filtering.
