- [LLMs know their vulnerabilities: Uncover Safety Gaps through Natural Distribution Shifts](https://arxiv.org/abs/2410.10700): Introduces ActorBreaker, a multi-turn jailbreak that uses actors related to toxic pretraining distributions to exploit natural distribution shifts in safety filters.
- [Superficial Safety Alignment Hypothesis](https://arxiv.org/abs/2410.10862): Treats safety alignment as learning a binary fulfill-versus-refuse direction, identifying safety-critical and redundant units that can be frozen or reused during tuning.
- [Gender Bias in Decision-Making with Large Language Models: A Study of Relationship Conflicts](https://arxiv.org/abs/2410.11084): Uses DeMET relationship-conflict prompts across nine gender-name configurations to measure how safety guardrails change LLM decision bias.
- [LLM Unlearning via Loss Adjustment with Only Forget Data](https://arxiv.org/abs/2410.11143): Performs LLM unlearning with loss adjustment using only forget data, avoiding explicit retained-data access while trying to limit utility collapse.
- [Multi-objective Reinforcement Learning: A Tool for Pluralistic Alignment](https://arxiv.org/abs/2410.11221): Frames pluralistic alignment as multi-objective RL with vector rewards, offering a model-side alternative to collapsing conflicting stakeholder values into one scalar reward.
- [Deciphering the Chaos: Enhancing Jailbreak Attacks via Adversarial Prompt Translation](https://arxiv.org/abs/2410.11317): Translates garbled gradient-optimized jailbreak strings into coherent natural-language adversarial prompts, improving transfer to unknown LLMs.
- [Jigsaw Puzzles: Splitting Harmful Questions to Jailbreak Large Language Models](https://arxiv.org/abs/2410.11459): Splits harmful requests into benign-looking multi-turn fragments and asks models to reconstruct them, bypassing safeguards across Gemini, Llama, GPT-4, and GPT-4o variants.
- [Understanding Likelihood Over-optimisation in Direct Alignment Algorithms](https://arxiv.org/abs/2410.11677): Analyzes direct alignment algorithms such as DPO and IPO through completion likelihood, showing likelihood over-optimization can separate preference gains from actual model performance.
- [Evaluation of Attribution Bias in Retrieval-Augmented Large Language Models](https://arxiv.org/abs/2410.12380): Counterfactually varies source-document authorship metadata in RAG, measuring attribution sensitivity and bias toward human-written versus AI-generated sources.
- [RosePO: Aligning LLM-based Recommenders with Human Values](https://arxiv.org/abs/2410.12519): Defines RosePO for LLM-based recommenders, smoothing personalized preference optimization so post-training can balance helpful recommendation behavior with harmlessness.
- [Weak-to-Strong Generalization beyond Accuracy: a Pilot Study in Safety, Toxicity, and Legal Reasoning](https://arxiv.org/abs/2410.12621): Uses weak-to-strong generalization experiments in safety, toxicity, and legal reasoning to test whether weak supervisors can elicit aligned behavior from stronger LLMs beyond accuracy tasks.
- [Cross-Modal Safety Mechanism Transfer in Large Vision-Language Models](https://arxiv.org/abs/2410.12662): Uses text-guided vision-language alignment to project image features into LLM hidden states where text safety mechanisms activate, reducing toxic-image vulnerabilities.
- [SAFREE: Training-Free and Adaptive Guard for Safe Text-to-Image And Video Generation](https://arxiv.org/abs/2410.12761): Finds toxic-concept subspaces in text embeddings and steers prompts away during diffusion denoising, providing training-free guards for image and video generation.
- [Mechanistic Unlearning: Robust Knowledge Unlearning and Editing via Mechanistic Localization](https://arxiv.org/abs/2410.12949): Localizes factual-recall mechanisms with mechanistic interpretability and edits those components, making knowledge unlearning more robust and less damaging.
- [POROver: Improving Safety and Reducing Overrefusal in Large Language Models with Overgeneration and Preference Optimization](https://arxiv.org/abs/2410.12999): Overgenerates safe and toxic-prompt completions with teacher models, then uses preference optimization to reduce over-refusal while maintaining safety.
- [AERO: Entropy-Guided Framework for Private LLM Inference](https://arxiv.org/abs/2410.13060): Uses entropy signals to decide when private LLM inference needs stronger protection, reducing privacy leakage while avoiding uniform high-cost defenses.
- [SPIN: Self-Supervised Prompt INjection](https://arxiv.org/abs/2410.13236): Detects and reverses adversarial prompts at inference time with a self-supervised prompt defense, reducing attack success while preserving benign request performance.
- [Breaking Chains: Unraveling the Links in Multi-Hop Knowledge Unlearning](https://arxiv.org/abs/2410.13274): Tests whether unlearning one fact breaks multi-hop knowledge chains, exposing residual linked facts that survive ordinary forget objectives.
- [BiasJailbreak:Analyzing Ethical Biases and Jailbreak Vulnerabilities in Large Language Models](https://arxiv.org/abs/2410.13334): Connects ethical-bias prompts with jailbreak vulnerability, showing biased framing can weaken refusal behavior and safety judgments.
- [CLEAR: Towards Contextual LLM-Empowered Privacy Policy Analysis and Risk Generation for Large Language Model Applications](https://arxiv.org/abs/2410.13387): Uses LLMs to parse privacy policies and generate contextual privacy risks for LLM applications, acting as a policy-analysis guardrail aid.
- [On the Role of Attention Heads in Large Language Model Safety](https://arxiv.org/abs/2410.13708): Studies safety-relevant attention heads, showing how multi-head attention components contribute to LLM guardrails and how suppressing them weakens safety capability.
- [Persistent Pre-Training Poisoning of LLMs](https://arxiv.org/abs/2410.13722): Measures persistent pretraining poisoning by training LLMs from scratch on poisoned web data and checking whether SFT and DPO leave the backdoor active.
- [De-mark: Watermark Removal in Large Language Models](https://arxiv.org/abs/2410.13808): Removes n-gram LLM watermarks through random-selection probing that estimates watermark strength and recovers red-green token lists.
- [S4ST: A Strong, Self-transferable, faSt, and Simple Scale Transformation for Transferable Targeted Attack](https://arxiv.org/abs/2410.13891): Shows that simple scale transformations can improve data-free black-box targeted transfer attacks, using blind self-alignment and self-transferability criteria.
- [Mitigating the Backdoor Effect for Multi-Task Model Merging via Safety-Aware Subspace](https://arxiv.org/abs/2410.13910): Introduces Defense-Aware Merging with dual masks and meta-learning to merge multi-task models while suppressing backdoor succession and transfer.
- [Unlearning Backdoor Attacks for LLMs with Weak-to-Strong Knowledge Distillation](https://arxiv.org/abs/2410.14425): Defends PEFT-tuned LLMs against backdoors with W2SDefense, using a clean small teacher and feature-alignment knowledge distillation to guide the larger model away from triggered behavior.
- [Enhancing Prompt Injection Attacks to LLMs via Poisoning Alignment](https://arxiv.org/abs/2410.14827): Poisons a small fraction of alignment samples so later prompt-injection attacks become more successful, shifting the threat from inference prompts to alignment data.
- [Bias Amplification: Large Language Models as Increasingly Biased Media](https://arxiv.org/abs/2410.15234): Measures how repeated LLM-mediated content generation can amplify bias over media-like information cycles, highlighting a generation-risk mechanism.
- [Jailbreaking and Mitigation of Vulnerabilities in Large Language Models](https://arxiv.org/abs/2410.15236): Reviews prompt-based, model-based, multimodal, and multilingual jailbreak attacks together with filtering, alignment, multi-agent, and self-regulation defenses; it is better treated as a Survey candidate.
- [When Machine Unlearning Meets Retrieval-Augmented Generation (RAG): Keep Secret or Forget Knowledge?](https://arxiv.org/abs/2410.15267): Simulates behavioral unlearning by editing a RAG knowledge base instead of model weights, comparing retrieval hiding with direct model forgetting.
- [Faster-GCG: Efficient Discrete Optimization Jailbreak Attacks against Aligned Large Language Models](https://arxiv.org/abs/2410.15362): Improves GCG-style jailbreak generation by analyzing discrete-optimization bottlenecks and reducing the evaluations needed to find adversarial suffixes.
- [Hallucination Detox: Sensitivity Dropout (SenD) for Large Language Model Training](https://arxiv.org/abs/2410.15460): Links hallucinations to unstable training dynamics and proposes Sensitivity Dropout, which drops high-variance embedding indices while monitoring hallucination with Efficient EigenScore.
- [Feint and Attack: Jailbreaking and Protecting LLMs via Attention Distribution Modeling](https://arxiv.org/abs/2410.16327): Models attention distributions to craft feint-style jailbreaks and corresponding protections that redirect attention away from unsafe instruction fragments.
- [Catastrophic Failure of LLM Unlearning via Quantization](https://arxiv.org/abs/2410.16454): Shows post-unlearning quantization can restore forgotten LLM knowledge, making compression a stress test for unlearning robustness.
- [SafetyAnalyst: Interpretable, transparent, and steerable LLM safety moderation](https://arxiv.org/abs/2410.16665): Expands an AI behavior into harm-benefit trees, scores stakeholder effects with interpretable weights, and lets moderators steer the safety standard.
- [Insights on Disagreement Patterns in Multimodal Safety Perception across Diverse Rater Groups](https://arxiv.org/abs/2410.17032): Measures safety ratings for about 1,000 text-to-image generations across 630 demographically balanced raters, exposing group-dependent multimodal harm judgments.
- [WAGLE: Strategic Weight Attribution for Effective and Modular Unlearning in Large Language Models](https://arxiv.org/abs/2410.17509): Uses strategic weight attribution to identify parameters most responsible for LLM unlearning, enabling modular WAGLE updates that improve forgetting while preserving retained behavior.
- [Guide for Defense (G4D): Dynamic Guidance for Robust and Balanced Defense in Large Language Models](https://arxiv.org/abs/2410.17922): Uses a multi-agent defense workflow to summarize user intent with external information and guide safe responses in general and chemistry-specific jailbreak cases.
- [CLEAR: Character Unlearning in Textual and Visual Modalities](https://arxiv.org/abs/2410.18057): Unlearns fictional or copyrighted characters across text and visual modalities, testing whether both language and image generators stop reproducing target character concepts.
- [Iterative Self-Tuning LLMs for Enhanced Jailbreaking Capabilities](https://arxiv.org/abs/2410.18469): Trains ADV-LLM through iterative self-tuning so the model itself generates low-cost adversarial suffixes with high jailbreak success against aligned LLMs.
- [LOGO - Long cOntext aliGnment via efficient preference Optimization](https://arxiv.org/abs/2410.18533): Extends preference optimization to long-context settings with efficient training so models better follow alignment preferences over long inputs.
- [Provably Robust Watermarks for Open-Source Language Models](https://arxiv.org/abs/2410.18861): Designs an open-source LLM watermark that remains provably robust even when model weights and generation code are public rather than secret.
- [Adversarial Attacks on Large Language Models Using Regularized Relaxation](https://arxiv.org/abs/2410.19160): Uses regularized relaxation to optimize adversarial LLM inputs, avoiding brittle discrete-token search while preserving attack effectiveness for safety evaluation.
- [Enhancing Safety in Reinforcement Learning with Human Feedback via Rectified Policy Optimization](https://arxiv.org/abs/2410.19933): Introduces Rectified Policy Optimization for RLHF, adjusting preference updates to improve harmlessness without collapsing helpfulness.
- [RobustKV: Defending Large Language Models against Jailbreak Attacks via KV Eviction](https://arxiv.org/abs/2410.19937): Defends against jailbreaks by evicting low-ranked harmful-query tokens from KV caches, reducing malicious-response generation while maintaining benign-task utility.
- [DeTeCtive: Detecting AI-generated Text via Multi-Level Contrastive Learning](https://arxiv.org/abs/2410.20964): Detects AI-generated text with multi-level contrastive learning, replacing hand-crafted features with representations intended to generalize to unseen generators and OOD text.
- [BlueSuffix: Reinforced Blue Teaming for Vision-Language Models Against Jailbreak Attacks](https://arxiv.org/abs/2410.20971): Trains defensive suffixes for VLMs with reinforcement-style blue teaming so appended safety tokens counter multimodal jailbreak prompts.
- [Stealthy Jailbreak Attacks on Large Language Models via Benign Data Mirroring](https://arxiv.org/abs/2410.21083): Trains a local mirror model from benign target outputs, then crafts transferable jailbreak prompts without sending obviously malicious search queries to the black-box model.
- [Unlearning as multi-task optimization: A normalized gradient difference approach with an adaptive learning rate](https://arxiv.org/abs/2410.22086): Frames LLM unlearning as regularized multi-task optimization and uses normalized gradient difference with adaptive learning rate to balance forgetting and retained performance.
- [The Impact of Inference Acceleration Strategies on Bias of LLMs](https://arxiv.org/abs/2410.22118): Measures how quantization, pruning, and caching alter demographic bias in LLM generations, treating inference acceleration as a safety-relevant model behavior change.
- [AmpleGCG-Plus: A Strong Generative Model of Adversarial Suffixes to Jailbreak LLMs with Higher Success Rates in Fewer Attempts](https://arxiv.org/abs/2410.22143): Improves the AmpleGCG adversarial-suffix generator with training strategies that produce stronger gibberish jailbreak suffixes in fewer attempts.
- [Auditing f-Differential Privacy in One Run](https://arxiv.org/abs/2410.22235): Estimates f-differential privacy from a single training run, tightening empirical privacy auditing without repeated retraining.
- [Embedding-based classifiers can detect prompt injection attacks](https://arxiv.org/abs/2410.22284): Fine-tunes embedding-based prompt-injection classifiers, showing curated embeddings and low-false-positive evaluation can support deployable LLM input guards.
- [Smaller Large Language Models Can Do Moral Self-Correction](https://arxiv.org/abs/2410.23496): Shows smaller LLMs can revise morally problematic answers after self-critique prompts, making moral self-correction a test-time alignment mechanism.
- [Transferable&Stealthy Ensemble Attacks: A Black-Box Jailbreaking Framework for Large Language Models](https://arxiv.org/abs/2410.23558): Combines multiple LLM-as-attacker strategies in a black-box ensemble, tailoring attack difficulty and semantic-coherence disruption for transferable jailbreaks.
- [Scalable Reinforcement Post-Training Beyond Static Human Prompts: Evolving Alignment via Asymmetric Self-Play](https://arxiv.org/abs/2411.00062): Casts reinforcement post-training as asymmetric self-play, evolving prompt distributions during RL instead of optimizing alignment only on static human prompts.
- [RESTOR: Knowledge Recovery in Machine Unlearning](https://arxiv.org/abs/2411.00204): Proposes RESTOR to audit unlearning by checking both targeted forgetting and recovery of the model state that would have existed without the removed data.
- [Multi-expert Prompting Improves Reliability, Safety and Usefulness of Large Language Models](https://arxiv.org/abs/2411.00492): Simulates multiple experts in one reasoning trace, aggregates their candidate answers, and selects a response that improves truthfulness, usefulness, and toxicity reduction.
- [Fairness Testing of Large Language Models in Role-Playing](https://arxiv.org/abs/2411.00585): Generates 550 social roles and 33,000 role-specific bias questions to test whether LLM role-playing amplifies fairness harms across eleven demographic attributes.
- [When Speculation Spills Secrets: Side Channels via Speculative Decoding In LLMs](https://arxiv.org/abs/2411.01076): Shows speculative decoding can leak information through side channels created by draft-token generation and verification, making fast inference a privacy surface.
- [Self-Consistency Falls Short! The Adverse Effects of Positional Bias on Long-Context Problems](https://arxiv.org/abs/2411.01101): Demonstrates that self-consistency can worsen long-context tasks because repeated samples preserve positional bias instead of averaging it out, an adjacent robustness concern.
- [Rule Based Rewards for Language Model Safety](https://arxiv.org/abs/2411.01111): Uses fine-grained rule-based rewards with LLM graders as direct RL training signals, making safety behavior easier to update than relabeling broad human-preference data.
- [$B^4$: A Black-Box Scrubbing Attack on LLM Watermarks](https://arxiv.org/abs/2411.01222): Formulates black-box watermark scrubbing as constrained optimization over watermark and fidelity distributions, attacking LLM text watermarks without knowing the watermark type or parameters.
- [UniGuard: Towards Universal Safety Guardrails for Jailbreak Attacks on Multimodal Large Language Models](https://arxiv.org/abs/2411.01703): Builds universal multimodal guardrails that detect and block jailbreak prompts across image-text inputs rather than relying on model-specific refusal wording.
- [Traffic and Safety Rule Compliance of Humans in Diverse Driving Situations](https://arxiv.org/abs/2411.01909): Defines time-to-collision, speed-limit, and interaction metrics across trajectory datasets, giving autonomous-driving models human compliance baselines.
- [Stochastic Monkeys at Play: Random Augmentations Cheaply Break LLM Safety Alignment](https://arxiv.org/abs/2411.02785): Shows random augmentations can cheaply bypass LLM safety alignment, challenging the assumption that jailbreakers need sophisticated optimization or high resources.
- [Membership Inference Attacks against Large Vision-Language Models](https://arxiv.org/abs/2411.02902): Develops membership-inference attacks for large vision-language models, targeting sensitive image-text training data and exposing missing multimodal privacy safeguards.
- [What Features in Prompts Jailbreak LLMs? Investigating the Mechanisms Behind Attacks](https://arxiv.org/abs/2411.03343): Analyzes prompt features that drive jailbreak success, separating mechanism-level attack factors from surface prompt templates.
- [Both Text and Images Leaked! A Systematic Analysis of Multimodal LLM Data Contamination](https://arxiv.org/abs/2411.03823): Analyzes contamination in multimodal LLMs across text and image channels, showing benchmark leakage can affect both modalities.
- [Diversity Helps Jailbreak Large Language Models](https://arxiv.org/abs/2411.04223): Uses diversity instructions that make LLMs deviate from prior attack context, increasing jailbreak success while reducing query cost against leading chatbots.
- [Layer-wise Alignment: Examining Safety Alignment Across Image Encoder Layers in Vision Language Models](https://arxiv.org/abs/2411.04291): Identifies ICET, where early exits from VLM image-encoder layers increase harmful responses, then trains Layer-Wise PPO for layer-level multimodal safety alignment.
- [Exploring the Danmaku Content Moderation on Video-Sharing Platforms: Existing Limitations, Challenges, and Design Opportunities](https://arxiv.org/abs/2411.04529): Uses interviews and co-design with video-platform users to identify moderation limits for toxic overlaid comments, making it a design study rather than a model method.
- [Revisiting the Robustness of Watermarking to Paraphrasing Attacks](https://arxiv.org/abs/2411.05277): Re-evaluates text-watermark robustness against paraphrasing attacks, separating detector failures under rewriting from the stronger claims made by prior watermark studies.
- [Ablation is Not Enough to Emulate DPO: How Neuron Dynamics Drive Toxicity Reduction](https://arxiv.org/abs/2411.06424): Explains DPO toxicity reduction through distributed MLP neuron activation shifts, showing simple toxic-neuron ablation accounts for only a minority of the effect.
- [SequentialBreak: Large Language Models Can be Fooled by Embedding Jailbreak Prompts into Sequential Prompt Chains](https://arxiv.org/abs/2411.06426): Embeds harmful requests inside benign sequential prompt chains such as question banks or games, exploiting context manipulation that makes LLMs overlook the risky segment.
- [Meta-Learning Objectives for Preference Optimization](https://arxiv.org/abs/2411.06568): Meta-learns preference-optimization objectives so alignment losses adapt to feedback quality instead of fixing a single DPO-style surrogate.
- [Combining Domain and Alignment Vectors to Achieve Better Knowledge-Safety Trade-offs in LLMs](https://arxiv.org/abs/2411.06824): Interpolates domain and alignment vectors with MergeAlign so medicine and finance expert LLMs retain domain utility while recovering safety behavior.
- [Model Stealing for Any Low-Rank Language Model](https://arxiv.org/abs/2411.07536): Analyzes model stealing for low-rank language models through query-based recovery, clarifying when proprietary LLM parameters or training-data privacy can leak.
- [Zer0-Jack: A Memory-efficient Gradient-based Jailbreaking Method for Black-box Multi-modal Large Language Models](https://arxiv.org/abs/2411.07559): Uses memory-efficient gradient search to craft black-box multimodal jailbreaks, reducing the cost of attacking MLLM safety filters.
- [World Models: The Safety Perspective](https://arxiv.org/abs/2411.07690): Surveys world-model safety for AI agents, analyzing how predictive environment models affect planning, trustworthiness, and failure modes in critical applications.
- [On the Role of Speech Data in Reducing Toxicity Detection Bias](https://arxiv.org/abs/2411.08135): Uses speech data to reduce toxicity-detector bias, testing whether audio modality cues mitigate demographic skew in moderation models.
- [The VLLM Safety Paradox: Dual Ease in Jailbreak Attack and Defense](https://arxiv.org/abs/2411.08410): Explains why visual inputs make VLLMs easy to jailbreak and why simple defenses can look strong, highlighting over-prudence and benign-input abstention.
- [Quantifying Risk Propensities of Large Language Models: Ethical Focus and Bias Detection through Role-Play](https://arxiv.org/abs/2411.08884): Uses role-play scenarios to quantify LLM risk propensities, with ethical-focus and bias-detection probes rather than generic safety scores.
- [Mitigating Sycophancy in Decoder-Only Transformer Architectures: Synthetic Data Intervention](https://arxiv.org/abs/2411.10156): Mitigates sycophancy in decoder-only transformers with synthetic data intervention, using diversified true/false examples to reduce agreement-seeking behavior.
- [On the Privacy Risk of In-context Learning](https://arxiv.org/abs/2411.10512): Shows in-context learning prompts can leak private task data through membership inference, making prompt-contained examples a privacy attack surface.
- [Debiasing Watermarks for Large Language Models via Maximal Coupling](https://arxiv.org/abs/2411.11203): Debiases green-red list LLM watermarks with maximal coupling, reducing token-distribution distortion while keeping machine-text detectability.
- [The Dark Side of Trust: Authority Citation-Driven Jailbreak Attacks on Large Language Models](https://arxiv.org/abs/2411.11407): Introduces authority-citation jailbreaks that exploit LLM trust in authoritative sources, using fabricated or persuasive citations to push unsafe compliance.
- [Membership Inference Attack against Long-Context Large Language Models](https://arxiv.org/abs/2411.11424): Attacks long-context LLMs with membership inference over extended prompts, showing larger context windows create additional training-data privacy leakage channels.
- [Enhancing Vision-Language Model Safety through Progressive Concept-Bottleneck-Driven Alignment](https://arxiv.org/abs/2411.11543): Aligns VLMs through progressive concept bottlenecks so unsafe visual concepts are made explicit and corrected before final response generation.
- [Combinational Backdoor Attack against Customized Text-to-Image Models](https://arxiv.org/abs/2411.12389): Splits backdoors across the text encoder and diffusion model in customized T2I pipelines, so separately adopted components combine into triggered unsafe generation.
- [Debias Your Large Multi-Modal Model at Test-Time via Non-Contrastive Visual Attribute Steering](https://arxiv.org/abs/2411.12590): Steers non-contrastive visual attribute directions at test time to reduce multimodal bias without retraining the full model.
- [CROW: Eliminating Backdoors from Large Language Models via Internal Consistency Regularization](https://arxiv.org/abs/2411.12768): Removes LLM backdoors with CROW, enforcing layer-wise hidden-state consistency under adversarial perturbations during fine-tuning without trigger knowledge or a clean reference model.
- [A Flexible Large Language Models Guardrail Development Methodology Applied to Off-Topic Prompt Detection](https://arxiv.org/abs/2411.12946): Presents a guardrail-development workflow for off-topic prompt detection, covering data design, classifier tuning, and thresholding for deployment moderation.
- [Safety Without Semantic Disruptions: Editing-free Safe Image Generation via Context-preserving Dual Latent Reconstruction](https://arxiv.org/abs/2411.13982): Uses safe embeddings and a tunable latent-space diffusion process to suppress unsafe image concepts while preserving nearby benign semantics that model editing can distort.
- [GASP: Efficient Black-Box Generation of Adversarial Suffixes for Jailbreaking LLMs](https://arxiv.org/abs/2411.14133): Generates readable black-box jailbreak suffixes with GASP, combining latent Bayesian optimization and iterative refinement to balance attack success and prompt coherence.
- [HateDay: Insights from a Global Hate Speech Dataset Representative of a Day on Twitter](https://arxiv.org/abs/2411.15462): Constructs a globally sampled one-day Twitter hate-speech dataset and shows academic evaluations overestimate real-world detector performance, especially outside European languages.
- [Towards Robust Evaluation of Unlearning in LLMs via Data Transformations](https://arxiv.org/abs/2411.15477): Evaluates LLM unlearning under transformed versions of forget data, testing whether methods remove concepts rather than only memorized surface forms.
- [MUNBa: Machine Unlearning via Nash Bargaining](https://arxiv.org/abs/2411.15537): Reformulates machine unlearning as Nash bargaining between forgetting and preservation gradients, producing Pareto-balanced updates across classification, CLIP, and diffusion tasks.
- [Steering Away from Harm: An Adaptive Approach to Defending Vision Language Model Against Jailbreaks](https://arxiv.org/abs/2411.16721): Defends VLMs with ASTRA, deriving harmful-response steering vectors from adversarial visual tokens and adaptively removing those directions at inference time.
- ["Moralized" Multi-Step Jailbreak Prompts: Black-Box Testing of Guardrails in Large Language Models for Verbal Attacks](https://arxiv.org/abs/2411.16730): Uses moralized multi-step verbal-attack prompts to black-box test whether LLM guardrails can be persuaded into unsafe escalation.
- [Edit Away and My Face Will not Stay: Personal Biometric Defense against Malicious Generative Editing](https://arxiv.org/abs/2411.16832): Protects portraits with FaceLock, optimizing perturbations that destroy biometric identity under diffusion editing while preserving visual plausibility.
- [BadScan: An Architectural Backdoor Attack on Visual State Space Models](https://arxiv.org/abs/2411.17283): Attacks VMamba-style visual state-space models with an architecture-aware backdoor after showing existing trigger attacks under-cover their state-space patch sequencing behavior.
- [Different Bias Under Different Criteria: Assessing Bias in LLMs with a Fact-Based Approach](https://arxiv.org/abs/2411.17338): Assesses LLM bias with fact-based criteria, showing measured bias changes when evaluation distinguishes factual grounding from stereotyped association.
- [Adaptive Deployment of Untrusted LLMs Reduces Distributed Threats](https://arxiv.org/abs/2411.17693): Uses adaptive deployment protocols that update trust in an untrusted LLM and route tasks through monitored micro-protocols to reduce distributed misalignment threats.
- [Visual Adversarial Attack on Vision-Language Models for Autonomous Driving](https://arxiv.org/abs/2411.18275): Introduces ADvLM, an autonomous-driving VLM attack that combines semantic-invariant instruction generation with time-series visual perturbations for safety-critical scenarios.
- [Don't Let Your Robot be Harmful: Responsible Robotic Manipulation via Safety-as-Policy](https://arxiv.org/abs/2411.18289): Builds Safety-as-Policy for robotic manipulation, pairing a risk-generating world model with a mental model that reflects on hazards before executing instructions.
- [Immune: Improving Safety Against Jailbreaks in Multi-modal LLMs via Inference-Time Alignment](https://arxiv.org/abs/2411.18688): Introduces Immune, an inference-time MLLM defense that uses a safe reward model and controlled decoding to resist visual and text jailbreaks.
- [SEUF: Is Unlearning One Expert Enough for Mixture-of-Experts LLMs?](https://arxiv.org/abs/2411.18797): Tests whether unlearning a single expert in MoE LLMs can remove targeted knowledge while preserving routing and utility across the remaining experts.
- [PEFT-as-an-Attack! Jailbreaking Language Models during Federated Parameter-Efficient Fine-Tuning](https://arxiv.org/abs/2411.19335): Shows federated PEFT can become PEFT-as-an-Attack, using small LoRA-style updates to bypass safety alignment while keeping raw user data decentralized.
- [Quantized Delta Weight Is Safety Keeper](https://arxiv.org/abs/2411.19530): Evaluates delta-weight quantization as a safety keeper, finding partial compression can reduce fine-tuning-based alignment, backdoor, and hallucination risks with limited utility loss.
- [LUMIA: Linear probing for Unimodal and MultiModal Membership Inference Attacks leveraging internal LLM states](https://arxiv.org/abs/2411.19876): Uses layer-by-layer linear probes on internal activations for LUMIA membership inference, extending privacy attacks to unimodal and multimodal LLM states.
- [Safe to Serve: Aligning Instruction-Tuned Models for Safety and Helpfulness](https://arxiv.org/abs/2412.00074): Compares safety instruction tuning, RAFT, and DPO for instruction-tuned LLMs, finding DPO especially effective at reducing toxic responses while preserving helpfulness.
- [Adversarial Exploitation of Data Diversity Improves Visual Localization](https://arxiv.org/abs/2412.00138): Uses adversarially selected data diversity to improve visual localization robustness, an adjacent autonomous-system robustness item rather than a direct LLM safety method.
- [Dynamic High-Order Control Barrier Functions With Diffuser for Safety-Critical Trajectory Planning at Signal-Free Intersections](https://arxiv.org/abs/2412.00162): Combines dynamic high-order control barrier functions with a diffusion planner for safe autonomous-vehicle trajectories at signal-free intersections.
- [Unified Parameter-Efficient Unlearning for LLMs](https://arxiv.org/abs/2412.00383): Uses parameter-efficient adapters for LLM unlearning so targeted forgetting can be applied without rewriting all model weights.
- [Jailbreak Large Vision-Language Models Through Multi-Modal Linkage](https://arxiv.org/abs/2412.00473): Links harmful semantics across image and text channels to jailbreak VLMs, showing single-modality safety checks miss cross-modal attack composition.
- [A Cognac Shot To Forget Bad Memories: Corrective Unlearning for Graph Neural Networks](https://arxiv.org/abs/2412.00789): Proposes corrective unlearning for GNNs, removing harmful graph-memory effects while preserving graph utility in an adjacent unlearning setting.
- [AlignMamba: Enhancing Multimodal Mamba with Local and Global Cross-Modal Alignment](https://arxiv.org/abs/2412.00833): Adds local and global cross-modal alignment objectives to Multimodal Mamba, improving vision-language representation alignment rather than a direct safety guard.
- [CopyrightShield: Enhancing Diffusion Model Security Against Copyright Infringement Attacks](https://arxiv.org/abs/2412.01528): Protects diffusion models from copyright-infringement attacks with a shield that suppresses protected concept reproduction during generation.
- [Noise Injection Reveals Hidden Capabilities of Sandbagging Language Models](https://arxiv.org/abs/2412.01784): Uses noise injection to reveal hidden capabilities in sandbagging language models, treating capability concealment as the safety risk.
- [Trust & Safety of LLMs and LLMs in Trust & Safety](https://arxiv.org/abs/2412.02113): Surveys both risks from LLM deployment and uses of LLMs inside trust-and-safety operations, so it is better treated as a Survey or governance candidate than as a model method.
- [Jailbreak Defense in a Narrow Domain: Limitations of Existing Methods and a New Transcript-Classifier Approach](https://arxiv.org/abs/2412.02159): Tests safety training, adversarial training, and classifiers on bomb-making jailbreaks, then proposes a transcript classifier that improves narrow-domain defense.
- [Enhancing Trust in Large Language Models via Uncertainty-Calibrated Fine-Tuning](https://arxiv.org/abs/2412.02904): Adds uncertainty-calibrated fine-tuning with an uncertainty-aware causal language modeling loss so LLMs can signal unreliable or hallucination-prone answers.
- [CredID: Credible Multi-Bit Watermark for Large Language Models Identification](https://arxiv.org/abs/2412.03107): Embeds credible multi-bit watermarks in LLM generations so outputs can carry model identifiers for provenance and accountability checks.
- [Robust Multi-bit Text Watermark with LLM-based Paraphrasers](https://arxiv.org/abs/2412.03123): Embeds multi-bit text watermarks by alternating fine-tuned LLM paraphrasers and decoding sentence-level semantic differences with a classifier.
- [Does Safety Training of LLMs Generalize to Semantically Related Natural Prompts?](https://arxiv.org/abs/2412.03235): Tests aligned LLMs on natural prompts semantically related to toxic seeds, showing safety fine-tuning can fail even without explicitly optimized jailbreaks.
- [SIDA: Social Media Image Deepfake Detection, Localization and Explanation with Large Multimodal Model](https://arxiv.org/abs/2412.04292): Combines a social-media deepfake dataset with an LMM-based detector that localizes and explains forged image regions, targeting misinformation safety.
- [Targeting the Core: A Simple and Effective Method to Attack RAG-based Agents via Direct LLM Manipulation](https://arxiv.org/abs/2412.04415): Shows that simple prefixes such as ignoring retrieved documents can manipulate the LLM core of RAG agents and bypass contextual safeguards.
- [Hidden in the Noise: Two-Stage Robust Watermarking for Images](https://arxiv.org/abs/2412.04653): Embeds image watermarks through diffusion initial noise and a two-stage detection framework, aiming for robust provenance without visible distortion.
- [SleeperMark: Towards Robust Watermark against Fine-Tuning Text-to-image Diffusion Models](https://arxiv.org/abs/2412.04852): Studies robust watermarks against fine-tuning of text-to-image diffusion models.
- [LIAR: Leveraging Inference Time Alignment (Best-of-N) to Jailbreak LLMs in Seconds](https://arxiv.org/abs/2412.05232): Recasts jailbreaks as inference-time misalignment and uses black-box best-of-N sampling to find harmful completions without training an adversarial generator.
- [LeakAgent: RL-based Red-teaming Agent for LLM Privacy Leakage](https://arxiv.org/abs/2412.05734): Trains an RL attack agent with fine-grained privacy-leakage rewards to elicit both training data and system prompts from black-box LLMs.
- [SABER: Model-agnostic Backdoor Attack on Chain-of-Thought in Neural Code Generation](https://arxiv.org/abs/2412.05829): Injects chain-of-thought backdoors into code language models by selecting malicious outputs through code mutation and exploiting self-attention patterns.
- [PBI-Attack: Prior-Guided Bimodal Interactive Black-Box Jailbreak Attack for Toxicity Maximization](https://arxiv.org/abs/2412.05892): Builds PBI-Attack by extracting malicious features with an alternative LVLM, embedding them into benign images, and alternating image-text greedy search for black-box jailbreaks.
- [Heuristic-Induced Multimodal Risk Distribution Jailbreak Attack for Multimodal Large Language Models](https://arxiv.org/abs/2412.05934): Uses HIMRD to distribute harmful semantics across image and text while heuristic search finds prompts that bypass single-modality MLLM protections.
- [Precise, Fast, and Low-cost Concept Erasure in Value Space: Orthogonal Complement Matters](https://arxiv.org/abs/2412.06143): Performs fast low-cost concept erasure through orthogonal complements in value space.
- [Exploring Memorization and Copyright Violation in Frontier LLMs: A Study of the New York Times v. OpenAI 2023 Lawsuit](https://arxiv.org/abs/2412.06370): Uses the New York Times v. OpenAI dispute as a case study for frontier-model memorization and copyrighted-text reproduction risk.
- [Copyright-Protected Language Generation via Adaptive Model Fusion](https://arxiv.org/abs/2412.06619): Fuses outputs from models trained on disjoint copyrighted corpora at inference time, adaptively reducing memorized text or code reproduction without retraining.
- [Take Fake as Real: Realistic-Like Robust Black-Box Adversarial Attack to Evade AIGC Detection](https://arxiv.org/abs/2412.06727): Uses realistic post-processing such as blur, compression, noise, and light spots to create black-box adversarial images that evade AIGC detectors.
- [Refusal Tokens: A Simple Way to Calibrate Refusals in Large Language Models](https://arxiv.org/abs/2412.06748): Introduces refusal tokens that let users calibrate category-specific refusal rates without retraining separate models for every desired sensitivity setting.
- [SafeWatch: An Efficient Safety-Policy Following Video Guardrail Model with Transparent Explanations](https://arxiv.org/abs/2412.06878): Trains SafeWatch as a video guardrail model that follows detailed safety policies and returns transparent explanations more efficiently than long-prompted MLLMs.
- [PrisonBreak: Jailbreaking Large Language Models with at Most Twenty-Five Targeted Bit-flips](https://arxiv.org/abs/2412.07192): Flips a small set of targeted model bits to break safety alignment, turning hardware or weight corruption into a jailbreak vector.
- [FlexLLM: Exploring LLM Customization for Moving Target Defense on Black-Box LLMs Against Jailbreak Attacks](https://arxiv.org/abs/2412.07672): Defends black-box LLM APIs by dynamically changing decoding hyperparameters and system prompts, creating a moving target against jailbreak attacks without retraining.
- [MAGIC: Mastering Physical Adversarial Generation in Context through Collaborative LLM Agents](https://arxiv.org/abs/2412.08014): Uses collaborative multimodal LLM agents to generate context-aware physical adversarial patches for driving scenes, making it an Agent Harness candidate around adversarial generation.
- [Antelope: Potent and Concealed Jailbreak Attack Strategy](https://arxiv.org/abs/2412.08156): Generates concealed jailbreak prompts that hide harmful intent while preserving attack potency against aligned LLMs.
- [Underestimated Privacy Risks for Minority Populations in Large Language Model Unlearning](https://arxiv.org/abs/2412.08559): Shows LLM unlearning can leave higher privacy leakage for minority populations, making subgroup-aware extraction risk part of unlearning evaluation.
- [AdvWave: Stealthy Adversarial Jailbreak Attack against Large Audio-Language Models](https://arxiv.org/abs/2412.08608): Crafts stealthy waveform perturbations that jailbreak audio-language models, extending safety attacks from text and image prompts into speech inputs.
- [Test-Time Alignment via Hypothesis Reweighting](https://arxiv.org/abs/2412.08812): Personalizes reward models at test time with HyRe, reweighting multi-head preference hypotheses from one to five target examples in a single forward pass.
- [AdvPrefix: An Objective for Nuanced LLM Jailbreaks](https://arxiv.org/abs/2412.10321): Introduces AdvPrefix, a prefix-forcing jailbreak objective that chooses model-dependent harmful-response prefixes to make optimized attacks more realistic and controllable.
- [AlignGuard: Scalable Safety Alignment for Text-to-Image Generation](https://arxiv.org/abs/2412.10493): Aligns text-to-image models with AlignGuard, using synthetic harmful and safe image-text pairs, custom DPO, and merged LoRA safety experts to suppress unsafe concepts.
- [RAT: Adversarial Attacks on Deep Reinforcement Agents for Targeted Behaviors](https://arxiv.org/abs/2412.10713): Trains an intention policy aligned to target behaviors and an adversary that steers DRL agents toward those behaviors, evaluating safety beyond reward reduction.
- [Towards Action Hijacking of Large Language Model-based Agent](https://arxiv.org/abs/2412.10807): Demonstrates action-hijacking attacks that redirect LLM-based agents toward attacker-chosen actions, suggesting parent routing to Agent Harness for tool/action control.
- [Separate the Wheat from the Chaff: A Post-Hoc Approach to Safety Re-Alignment for Fine-Tuned Language Models](https://arxiv.org/abs/2412.11041): Identifies, removes, and recalibrates unsafe delta parameters after fine-tuning, restoring LLM safety under full fine-tuning and LoRA while preserving utility.
- [Glimpse: Enabling White-Box Methods to Use Proprietary Models for Zero-Shot LLM-Generated Text Detection](https://arxiv.org/abs/2412.11506): Estimates full token probability distributions from partial proprietary-model API observations, enabling white-box generated-text detectors to use closed models.
- [Bias Vector: Mitigating Biases in Language Models with Task Arithmetic Approach](https://arxiv.org/abs/2412.11679): Introduces or adapts agent safety capabilities, adding coverage for bias Vector: Mitigating Biases in Language Models with Task Arithmetic Approach to the Model section.
- [Stepwise Reasoning Error Disruption Attack of LLMs](https://arxiv.org/abs/2412.11934): Injects subtle errors into earlier reasoning steps so LLMs propagate mistakes through later reasoning, exposing robustness failures in zero-shot and few-shot settings.
- [Gramian Multimodal Representation Learning and Alignment](https://arxiv.org/abs/2412.11959): Uses Gramian-based objectives to align multimodal representations, improving cross-modal feature consistency in an adjacent model-alignment setting.
- [Na'vi or Knave: Jailbreaking Language Models via Metaphorical Avatars](https://arxiv.org/abs/2412.12145): Maps harmful entities to innocuous metaphorical avatars and embeds the target in adaptive human-like interactions to produce transferable jailbreaks.
- [NLSR: Neuron-Level Safety Realignment of Large Language Models Against Harmful Fine-Tuning](https://arxiv.org/abs/2412.12497): Realigns safety at neuron level after harmful fine-tuning, identifying and correcting safety-critical neurons instead of retraining the whole LLM.
- [Jailbreaking? One Step Is Enough!](https://arxiv.org/abs/2412.12621): Uses REDA to disguise jailbreak intent as a defensive task, starting from the target harmful response and inducing one-step cross-model attacks through in-context examples.
- [Lightweight Safety Classification Using Pruned Language Models](https://arxiv.org/abs/2412.13435): Trains penalized logistic-regression classifiers on intermediate hidden states of small LLMs for lightweight content-safety and prompt-injection detection.
- [Large Language Model Federated Learning with Blockchain and Unlearning for Cross-Organizational Collaboration](https://arxiv.org/abs/2412.13551): Combines federated LLM learning with blockchain trust and unlearning support so cross-organization collaboration can protect data sharing, auditability, and deletion rights.
- [Clio: Privacy-Preserving Insights into Real-World AI Use](https://arxiv.org/abs/2412.13678): Uses Clio to summarize millions of AI-assistant conversations with privacy-preserving model analysis, surfacing abuse patterns and unknown safety issues without human review of raw chats.
- [Towards Efficient and Explainable Hate Speech Detection via Model Distillation](https://arxiv.org/abs/2412.13698): Distills large LLM hate-speech classifiers into smaller explainable models using chain-of-thought rationales, reducing moderation cost while preserving interpretability.
- [Cal-DPO: Calibrated Direct Preference Optimization for Language Model Alignment](https://arxiv.org/abs/2412.14516): Calibrates DPO by matching implicit reward scales to ground-truth reward values, improving preference alignment beyond purely contrastive response comparisons.
- [SATA: A Paradigm for LLM Jailbreak via Simple Assistive Task Linkage](https://arxiv.org/abs/2412.15289): Introduces SATA, a simple assistive task linkage jailbreak that masks harmful keywords into benign sub-tasks and links them back to elicit unsafe responses.
- [FedRLHF: A Convergence-Guaranteed Federated Framework for Privacy-Preserving and Personalized RLHF](https://arxiv.org/abs/2412.15538): Decentralizes RLHF with FedRLHF, letting clients update local reward functions and policies from human feedback without sharing raw data or labels.
- [EMPRA: Embedding Perturbation Rank Attack against Neural Ranking Models](https://arxiv.org/abs/2412.16382): Perturbs sentence embeddings in black-box neural rankers to promote attacker-chosen documents on MS MARCO while preserving semantic readability.
- [Privacy in Fine-tuning Large Language Models: Attacks, Defenses, and Future Directions](https://arxiv.org/abs/2412.16504): Surveys fine-tuning privacy risks, including membership inference, extraction, and backdoors, alongside defenses such as differential privacy, federated learning, and unlearning.
- [POEX: Towards Policy Executable Jailbreak Attacks Against the LLM-based Robots](https://arxiv.org/abs/2412.16633): Studies jailbreaks that make LLM-based robots output executable harmful policies, introduces Harmful-RLBench, and analyzes why ordinary text jailbreaks do not transfer directly.
- [Unpacking Political Bias in Large Language Models: Insights Across Topic Polarization](https://arxiv.org/abs/2412.16746): Measures LLM political bias across topics with different polarization levels, showing where ideological skew is strongest or evaluation-sensitive.
- [Shaping the Safety Boundaries: Understanding and Defending Against Jailbreaks in Large Language Models](https://arxiv.org/abs/2412.17034): Defines a safety-boundary view of jailbreaks and defends with ABD, constraining low- and middle-layer activations back inside the safe region.
- [DiffusionAttacker: Diffusion-Driven Prompt Manipulation for LLM Jailbreak](https://arxiv.org/abs/2412.17522): Uses a sequence-to-sequence text diffusion generator with an attack loss to rewrite prompts into diverse jailbreaks beyond suffix templates.
- [Retention Score: Quantifying Jailbreak Risks for Vision Language Models](https://arxiv.org/abs/2412.17544): Measures jailbreak or prompt-injection risk with Quantifying Jailbreak Risks for Vision Language Models, separating unsafe-response behavior from ordinary task failure.
- [Token Highlighter: Inspecting and Mitigating Jailbreak Prompts for Large Language Models](https://arxiv.org/abs/2412.18171): Uses Token Highlighter to locate jailbreak-critical tokens via affirmation-loss gradients and soften their embeddings in a single protected-model query.
- [ICM-Assistant: Instruction-tuning Multimodal Large Language Models for Rule-based Explainable Image Content Moderation](https://arxiv.org/abs/2412.18216): Builds an instruction-tuned MLLM for rule-based image moderation using decomposed safety rules, enriched annotations, and explainable moderation question-answer data.
- [Diverse and Effective Red Teaming with Auto-generated Rewards and Multi-step Reinforcement Learning](https://arxiv.org/abs/2412.18693): Automates red teaming with diverse LLM-generated attack goals, rule-based rewards, and multi-step RL that rewards attacks unlike past attempts.
- [Attack-in-the-Chain: Bootstrapping Large Language Models for Attacks Against Black-box Neural Ranking Models](https://arxiv.org/abs/2412.18770): Uses LLM chain-of-thought prompting to generate adversarial documents against black-box neural rankers, exposing retrieval robustness risks for LLM-powered search pipelines.
- [RAG with Differential Privacy](https://arxiv.org/abs/2412.19291): Applies differentially private token generation to RAG so responses can use personal or confidential documents while bounding leakage from retrieved context.
- [No Preference Left Behind: Group Distributional Preference Optimization](https://arxiv.org/abs/2412.20299): Introduces GDPO, aligning LLMs to group preference distributions by estimating belief distributions and conditioning optimization on pluralistic preferences.
- [LLM-Virus: Evolutionary Jailbreak Attack on Large Language Models](https://arxiv.org/abs/2501.00055): Uses evolutionary search to mutate prompts into LLM-Virus jailbreaks, automating attack discovery against aligned language models.
- [MLLM-as-a-Judge for Image Safety without Human Labeling](https://arxiv.org/abs/2501.00192): Uses pretrained MLLMs as image-safety judges that follow changing safety rules without human relabeling or task-specific fine-tuning.
- [Boosting Adversarial Transferability with Spatial Adversarial Alignment](https://arxiv.org/abs/2501.01015): Aligns spatial adversarial patterns so image attacks transfer better across vision-language models, making transferability itself the robustness stressor.
- [SaLoRA](https://arxiv.org/abs/2501.01765): Preserves safety alignment during low-rank adaptation, reducing fine-tuning drift in aligned LLMs.
- [Real-time tunable LLM safeguarding](https://arxiv.org/abs/2501.02018): Provides a runtime safeguard with adjustable safety-performance trade-offs, adding a model-side control mechanism for deployment-time safety.
- [Safety Attention Heads in LVLMs](https://arxiv.org/abs/2501.02029): Identifies safety-relevant attention heads in large vision-language models, adding an internal-monitoring route for multimodal safety.
- [Align-Pro: A Principled Approach to Prompt Optimization for LLM Alignment](https://arxiv.org/abs/2501.03486): Formulates prompt optimization as an alignment alternative for frozen LLMs, giving a principled route to steer values without RLHF-style parameter updates.
- [SC-Pro: Training-Free Framework for Defending Unsafe Image Synthesis Attack](https://arxiv.org/abs/2501.05359): Applies a training-free defense against unsafe image-synthesis attacks, filtering or steering risky generation without updating the text-to-image model.
- [HFMF: Hierarchical Fusion Meets Multi-Stream Models for Deepfake Detection](https://arxiv.org/abs/2501.05631): Proposes HFMF, a two-stage deepfake detector combining hierarchical cross-modal fusion with multi-stream feature extraction for generative-image forensics.
- [Concept Activation Vectors for LLM Control](https://arxiv.org/abs/2501.05764): steers large language models through concept activation vectors.
- [Formal Trust and Threat Modeling Using Large Language Models](https://doi.org/10.1109/acsacw65225.2024.00033): Uses LLMs to support formal trust and threat modeling in an ACSAC workshop setting, making it a security-analysis or Agent Harness candidate rather than model-side training work.
- [Reinforcement Learning-powered Effectiveness and Efficiency Few-shot Jailbreaking Attack LLMs](https://doi.org/10.1109/ispa63168.2024.00269): Uses reinforcement learning to search few-shot jailbreak prompts, optimizing attack effectiveness and query efficiency against safety-aligned LLMs.
- [Low-Code, High Privacy: Leveraging Open-Source LLMs for On-Premises AI in Oracle APEX](https://doi.org/10.36948/ijfmr.2024.v06i05.57940): Describes on-premises open-source LLM integration for Oracle APEX to keep enterprise prompts and outputs local, making it deployment-privacy guidance rather than a new model method.
- [The EU's AI act: A framework for collaborative governance](https://doi.org/10.1016/j.iot.2024.101291): Explains the EU AI Act as a collaborative-governance framework; it is policy context for safety controls, not a model-side method.
- [Please Tell Me More: Privacy Impact of Explainability through the Lens of Membership Inference Attack](https://doi.org/10.1109/sp54263.2024.00120): Shows that explanation interfaces can increase membership-inference leakage, using explainability outputs as extra evidence for privacy attacks.
- [To Authenticity, and Beyond! Building Safe and Fair Generative AI Upon the Three Pillars of Provenance](https://doi.org/10.1109/mcg.2024.3380168): Frames safe and fair generative AI around provenance, authenticity, and traceability pillars rather than proposing a new detector or watermark.
- [Deepfake Detection and Localization Using Multi-View Inconsistency Measurement](https://doi.org/10.1109/tdsc.2024.3472064): Detects and localizes deepfakes by measuring inconsistencies across multiple visual views, producing spatial evidence instead of only binary authenticity labels.
- [AI Risk Categorization Decoded (AIR 2024)](https://doi.org/10.70777/agi.v1i1.10603): Decodes AI risk categories for governance and evaluation use, making it taxonomy context rather than an implementation of a safety model.
- [Deepfake Detection Based on the Adaptive Fusion of Spatial-Frequency Features](https://doi.org/10.1155/2024/7578036): Fuses spatial and frequency-domain cues adaptively to detect deepfake artifacts that may be missed by single-domain detectors.
- [WaterDiff: Perceptual Image Watermarks Via Diffusion Model](https://doi.org/10.1109/icassp48485.2024.10447095): Embeds perceptual image watermarks through a diffusion-model process, targeting provenance while preserving visual quality.
- [Enhancing Robustness of Speech Watermarking Using a Transformer-Based Framework Exploiting Acoustic Features](https://doi.org/10.1109/taslp.2024.3486206): Uses transformer modeling of acoustic features to make speech watermarks more robust against audio transformations and attacks.
- [Defending Large Language Models Against Jailbreak Attacks Through Chain of Thought Prompting](https://doi.org/10.1109/nana63151.2024.00028): Uses chain-of-thought prompting as a jailbreak defense strategy for LLMs, testing whether explicit reasoning can improve refusal behavior under attack prompts.
- [An input-denoising-based defense against stealthy backdoor attacks in large language models for code](https://doi.org/10.1016/j.infsof.2024.107661): Defends code LLMs against stealthy backdoors by denoising inputs before generation, aiming to remove trigger patterns while preserving normal code behavior.
- [Cross-lingual Transfer and Multilingual Learning for Detecting Harmful Behaviour in African Under-Resourced Language Dialogue](https://doi.org/10.18653/v1/2024.sigdial-1.49): Studies cross-lingual transfer and multilingual learning for harmful-behavior detection in African under-resourced dialogue where labeled moderation data is scarce.
- [Semantic-Preserving Adversarial Example Attack against BERT](https://doi.org/10.18653/v1/2024.trustnlp-1.17): Crafts semantic-preserving adversarial examples against BERT, stressing robustness by changing inputs without changing their intended meaning.
- [Addressing Data Poisoning and Model Manipulation Risks using LLM Models in Web Security](https://doi.org/10.1109/icdscnc62492.2024.10941696): Uses LLM-based web-security analysis to address data-poisoning and model-manipulation risks, making it a broad security-risk mitigation item.
- [State Diagram Extension and Test Case Generation Based on Large Language Models for Improving Test Engineers’ Efficiency in Safety Testing](https://doi.org/10.1109/issrew63542.2024.00092): Uses LLMs to extend state diagrams and generate safety-test cases, making it software safety-testing automation rather than a core model-alignment method.
- [A Low-cost Black-box Jailbreak Based on Custom Mapping Dictionary with Multi-round Induction](https://doi.org/10.1109/trustcom63139.2024.00131): Uses a custom mapping dictionary and multi-round induction to construct low-cost black-box jailbreaks that replace direct harmful wording with attacker-defined substitutions.
- [A Waterlog for Detecting and Tracing Synthetic Text from Large Language Models](https://eprint.iacr.org/2024/1424): Proposes a synthetic-text watermarking scheme for detecting and tracing LLM outputs, adding a provenance mechanism for generated-text accountability.
- [Dual Defense: Adversarial, Traceable, and Invisible Robust Watermarking Against Face Swapping](https://doi.org/10.1109/tifs.2024.3383648): Combines adversarial robustness, traceability, and invisibility in image watermarking to protect faces against face-swapping misuse.
- [Mitigating Sycophancy in Large Language Models via Direct Preference Optimization](https://doi.org/10.1109/bigdata62323.2024.10825538): Applies direct preference optimization to reduce sycophancy, training LLMs to avoid uncritical agreement when user claims conflict with evidence or correctness.
- [Large Language Models Can Be Contextual Privacy Protection Learners](https://doi.org/10.18653/v1/2024.emnlp-main.785): Trains or prompts LLMs to learn context-dependent privacy protection decisions, distinguishing sensitive disclosures from benign contextual use.
- [Fine-grained Pluggable Gradient Ascent for Knowledge Unlearning in Language Models](https://doi.org/10.18653/v1/2024.emnlp-main.566): Applies pluggable gradient-ascent modules for fine-grained LLM knowledge unlearning, reducing target recall while limiting damage to unrelated knowledge.
- [SrcMarker: Dual-Channel Source Code Watermarking via Scalable Code Transformations](https://doi.org/10.1109/sp54263.2024.00097): Embeds source-code watermarks through scalable semantics-preserving code transformations, using dual channels for ownership tracing.
- [Mitigating Privacy Seesaw in Large Language Models: Augmented Privacy Neuron Editing via Activation Patching](https://doi.org/10.18653/v1/2024.findings-acl.315): Edits privacy-related neurons with activation patching to reduce PII leakage without triggering a privacy-utility seesaw on non-target data.
- [Latent Flow Diffusion for Deepfake Video Generation](https://doi.org/10.1109/cvprw63382.2024.00382): Generates deepfake videos with latent-flow diffusion, providing an attack-generation model relevant to media-forensics defenses.
- [Jailbreak Attacks on Large Language Models and Possible Defenses: Present Status and Future Possibilities](https://doi.org/10.1109/istas61960.2024.10732418): Reviews jailbreak attack families and possible defenses, offering status-and-outlook synthesis rather than a specific guardrail implementation.
- [Mamba-Based Federated Learning Architecture for Privacy-Preserving Machine Learning](https://doi.org/10.1109/iccasit62299.2024.10827895): Applies a Mamba-based federated architecture to privacy-preserving machine learning, an adjacent privacy-preserving training item rather than an agent-specific guardrail.
- [BOLT: Privacy-Preserving, Accurate and Efficient Inference for Transformers](https://doi.org/10.1109/sp54263.2024.00130): Provides privacy-preserving transformer inference with secure computation techniques designed to keep accuracy high while reducing encrypted-inference cost.
- [On the Vulnerability of Safety Alignment in Open-Access LLMs](https://doi.org/10.18653/v1/2024.findings-acl.549): Shows open-access LLM safety alignment can be weakened by downstream adaptation and adversarial use, measuring how easily safeguards erode.
- [Jailbreak Open-Sourced Large Language Models via Enforced Decoding](https://doi.org/10.18653/v1/2024.acl-long.299): Forces open-source LLM decoding toward affirmative harmful continuations, showing that decoding-time constraints can bypass refusal behavior without retraining.
- [Using Graph Neural Networks to Improve Generalization Capability of the Models for Deepfake Detection](https://doi.org/10.1109/tifs.2024.3451356): Uses graph neural networks to model relationships among facial or forensic features, improving cross-dataset deepfake detector generalization.
- [Multi-Scale Permutation Entropy for Audio Deepfake Detection](https://doi.org/10.1109/icassp48485.2024.10448095): Detects audio deepfakes with multi-scale permutation entropy features that capture temporal irregularities in synthetic speech.
- [A CycleGAN Watermarking Method for Ownership Verification](https://doi.org/10.1109/tdsc.2024.3424900): Uses CycleGAN transformations to embed ownership watermarks, supporting verification without visibly degrading generated or transformed images.
- [Safeguarding media integrity: A hybrid optimized deep feature fusion based deepfake detection in videos](https://doi.org/10.1016/j.cose.2024.103860): Combines optimized deep feature fusion for video deepfake detection, targeting media-integrity verification across temporal and spatial artifacts.
- [On the Security and Privacy Implications of Large Language Models: In-Depth Threat Analysis](https://doi.org/10.1109/ithings-greencom-cpscom-smartdata-cybermatics62450.2024.00102): Provides a threat-analysis view of LLM security and privacy implications, so it is better treated as Survey or risk-modeling context than as a model entry.
- [Data Breach Prevention in AI Systems: Employing Event-Driven Architecture to Combat Prompt Injection Attacks in Chatbots](https://doi.org/10.1109/icicn62625.2024.10761619): Uses an event-driven architecture to prevent chatbot data breaches from prompt injection, making it a runtime Agent Harness or system-guard candidate.
- [How to Make Reproducible Research in Machine Unlearning with ERASURE](https://doi.org/10.24963/ijcai.2024/1255): Studies machine unlearning through ERASURE, focusing on targeted removal, residual leakage, and retained-model utility.
- [Integrating security, privacy, and advanced cyber technologies for Resilient Urban, IoT, and AI Systems](https://doi.org/10.30574/wjaets.2024.13.1.0394): Reviews cybersecurity, privacy, machine learning, blockchain, and secure-routing techniques for resilient urban and IoT AI systems.
- [Graph-Based Prompt Injection Attacks Against Large Language Models](https://doi.org/10.1109/time-e62724.2024.10919819): Models prompt-injection attacks with graph structure, capturing relationships among injected instructions and LLM responses for attack construction or analysis.
- [Improved Safety and 3D Scanning with Human-Robot Collaboration](https://doi.org/10.1109/dicta63115.2024.00108): Combines human-robot collaboration with safer 3D scanning, an adjacent robotics safety application rather than a reusable LLM safety model.
- [Confirmation and Specificity Biases in Large Language Models: An Explorative Study](https://doi.org/10.1109/mis.2024.3513992): Measures confirmation and specificity biases in LLM responses, adding a bias-audit signal for safety evaluation rather than a training recipe.
- [Supervision policies can shape long-term risk management in general-purpose AI models](https://arxiv.org/abs/2501.06137): Studies how supervision policies alter long-term risk management in general-purpose AI models, making it a policy-model or governance-routing candidate.
- [Enhancing AI Safety Through the Fusion of Low Rank Adapters](https://arxiv.org/abs/2501.06208): Fuses a task LoRA with a safety LoRA to reduce harmful responses while preserving instruction-following ability, with over-refusal as the main trade-off.
- [Risk-Averse Fine-tuning](https://arxiv.org/abs/2501.06911): Applies CVaR-style risk-averse optimization to LLM fine-tuning so rare harmful or toxic outputs are penalized more strongly.
- [Refusal Behavior from a Nonlinear Perspective](https://arxiv.org/abs/2501.08145): Studies refusal behavior through nonlinear model analysis, adding a mechanistic safety view of when LLMs refuse or comply.
- [WMamba: Wavelet-based Mamba for Face Forgery Detection](https://arxiv.org/abs/2501.09617): Builds WMamba, a wavelet-based Mamba feature extractor with dynamic contour convolution to capture subtle face-forgery artifacts that spatial detectors miss.
- [Beyond Reward Hacking: Causal Rewards for Large Language Model Alignment](https://arxiv.org/abs/2501.09620): Builds causal reward models with counterfactual invariance so RLHF-style alignment is less driven by spurious cues such as length, sycophancy, or demographic correlations.
- [Behavioral Self-Awareness](https://arxiv.org/abs/2501.11120): Shows that fine-tuned LLMs can sometimes report learned behaviors and backdoor-like policies without examples, making self-disclosure and latent behavior awareness a safety-relevant model property.
- [Can Safety Fine-Tuning Be More Principled? Lessons Learned from Cybersecurity](https://arxiv.org/abs/2501.11183): Transfers cybersecurity principles into safety fine-tuning design, emphasizing threat modeling, adversarial coverage, and principled harmful-capability controls.
- [Verifying Cross-modal Entity Consistency in News using Vision-language Models](https://arxiv.org/abs/2501.11403): Uses VLMs to verify whether entities mentioned in news text match visual evidence, supporting misinformation detection through cross-modal consistency checks.
- [MONA](https://arxiv.org/abs/2501.13011): Combines myopic optimization with non-myopic approval to reduce multi-step reward hacking in RL agents, including settings with LLM-based delegated oversight.
- [Refining Input Guardrails: Enhancing LLM-as-a-Judge Efficiency Through Chain-of-Thought Fine-Tuning and Alignment](https://arxiv.org/abs/2501.13080): Fine-tunes and aligns chain-of-thought moderation traces so smaller LLM judges can act as more efficient input guardrails against malicious interactions.
- [Preference Curriculum](https://arxiv.org/abs/2501.13126): Studies preference-aware pretraining data curricula as a model-side alignment recipe.
- [Watching the AI Watchdogs: A Fairness and Robustness Analysis of AI Safety Moderation Classifiers](https://arxiv.org/abs/2501.13302): Audits safety moderation classifiers for fairness and robustness, checking whether guardrail models fail unevenly across demographic groups or adversarial perturbations.
- [HumorReject: Decoupling LLM Safety from Refusal Prefix via A Little Humor](https://arxiv.org/abs/2501.13677): Uses humorous refusal styles to decouple safety behavior from rigid refusal prefixes, testing whether models can stay safe without formulaic disclaimers.
- [Towards Safer Social Media Platforms: Scalable and Performant Few-Shot Harmful Content Moderation Using Large Language Models](https://arxiv.org/abs/2501.13976): Studies scalable few-shot LLM moderation for evolving harmful social-media content.
- [Interpretable Neuron Editing for Gender Bias](https://arxiv.org/abs/2501.14457): Locates and edits interpretable neurons to mitigate gender bias in LLMs, adding a model-internal fairness intervention.
- [PromptShield: Deployable Detection for Prompt Injection Attacks](https://arxiv.org/abs/2501.15145): Curates PromptShield for deployable prompt-injection detection and fine-tunes a detector that performs better in low-false-positive deployment regimes.
- [Data-adaptive Safety Rules for Training Reward Models](https://arxiv.org/abs/2501.15453): Learns data-adaptive safety rules for reward-model training so feedback labels reflect the local risk pattern rather than one static rubric.
- [Information-theoretic Distinctions Between Deception and Confusion](https://arxiv.org/abs/2501.16448): Separates deception from confusion with information-theoretic criteria, clarifying when wrong model behavior reflects hidden intent versus uncertainty.
- [xJailbreak](https://arxiv.org/abs/2501.16727): Uses representation-space-guided reinforcement learning to construct interpretable jailbreak attacks against LLMs.
- [Challenges in Ensuring AI Safety in DeepSeek-R1 Models: The Shortcomings of Reinforcement Learning Strategies](https://arxiv.org/abs/2501.17030): Diagnoses DeepSeek-R1 harmlessness failures under RL, highlighting reward hacking, language mixing, generalization gaps, and hybrid RL plus SFT mitigation paths.
- [Virus](https://arxiv.org/abs/2501.17433): Shows a harmful fine-tuning attack that bypasses guardrail moderation and erodes LLM safety alignment.
- [Privacy Audit as Bits Transmission: (Im)possibilities for Audit by One Run](https://arxiv.org/abs/2501.17750): Frames one-run privacy auditing as bits transmission, deriving limits on how much leakage evidence a single audit can certify.
- [Differentially Private Steering for Large Language Model Alignment](https://arxiv.org/abs/2501.18532): Adds privacy guarantees to activation-steering alignment so private demonstration data can shape behavior without being directly exposed through the steered model.
- [Graph of Attacks with Pruning: Optimizing Stealthy Jailbreak Prompt Generation for Enhanced LLM Content Moderation](https://arxiv.org/abs/2501.18638): Optimizes stealthy jailbreak prompt generation through pruned attack graphs, adding an attack-generation method for safety alignment evaluation.
- [Exploring Audio Editing Features as User-Centric Privacy Defenses Against Large Language Model(LLM) Based Emotion Inference Attacks](https://arxiv.org/abs/2501.18727): Tests pitch and tempo editing as user-controlled defenses against emotion inference from speech by DNNs and LLMs, balancing privacy, usability, and reversibility.
- [Scaling Laws for Differentially Private Language Models](https://arxiv.org/abs/2501.18914): Studies scaling laws for differentially private language models, linking privacy-preserving training with model capability trade-offs.
- [The Energy Loss Phenomenon in RLHF: A New Perspective on Mitigating Reward Hacking](https://arxiv.org/abs/2501.19358): Explains reward hacking through an energy-loss perspective in RLHF.
- [Defense Against the Dark Prompts](https://arxiv.org/abs/2502.00580): Uses prompt evaluation to mitigate Best-of-N jailbreaking, adding a lightweight defense route against optimization-based prompt attacks.
- [Activation Approximations Can Incur Safety Vulnerabilities Even in Aligned LLMs: Comprehensive Analysis and Defense](https://arxiv.org/abs/2502.00840): Shows approximate activation computation can create safety vulnerabilities in aligned LLMs and evaluates defenses against approximation-induced failures.
- [SimPER: A Minimalist Approach to Preference Alignment without Hyperparameters](https://arxiv.org/abs/2502.00883): Proposes a hyperparameter-free preference optimization objective for alignment tuning.
- [SafeSwitch: Steering Unsafe LLM Behavior via Internal Activation Signals](https://arxiv.org/abs/2502.01042): Steers unsafe LLM behavior through internal activation signals.
- [Picky LLMs and Unreliable RMs](https://arxiv.org/abs/2502.01116): studies how benign instruction tuning can degrade safety alignment and expose reward-model unreliability.
- [Jailbreaking with Universal Multi-Prompts](https://arxiv.org/abs/2502.01154): Builds universal multi-prompt jailbreaks that transfer across harmful requests, reducing the need to optimize a new attack for each target.
- [Fine-tuning Attacks on CoT-Enabled DeepSeek](https://arxiv.org/abs/2502.01225): Studies how fine-tuning can amplify harmful behavior in reasoning models and interact with chain-of-thought safety alignment.
- [Eliciting Language Model Behaviors with Investigator Agents](https://arxiv.org/abs/2502.01236): Trains investigator models to search for prompts that elicit target behaviors such as hallucination or harmful responses, making red-team prompt discovery a learned model-side process.
- [Backdoor Sample Detection in CLIP](https://arxiv.org/abs/2502.01385): detects backdoor samples in contrastive language-image pretraining, adding a multimodal training-safety method.
- [Robust LLM Alignment via Distributionally Robust Direct Preference Optimization](https://arxiv.org/abs/2502.01930): Extends preference optimization with distributionally robust objectives so alignment is less brittle under demographic, linguistic, or regional preference shifts.
- [Reasoning Bias of Next Token Prediction Training](https://arxiv.org/abs/2502.02007): Analyzes how next-token prediction induces reasoning bias, linking pretraining objectives to systematic errors in model reasoning behavior.
- [Safety Debiasing](https://arxiv.org/abs/2502.02153): Mitigates vulnerabilities in safety-aligned language models through debiasing.
- [STAIR](https://arxiv.org/abs/2502.02384): Improves safety alignment with introspective reasoning, training models to examine their own risky intermediate rationale before answering.
- [Privacy Attacks on Image AutoRegressive Models](https://arxiv.org/abs/2502.02514): Introduces membership and dataset inference attacks for image autoregressive models, showing substantially higher training-image leakage than comparable diffusion-model attacks.
- [SimMark: A Robust Sentence-Level Similarity-Based Watermarking Algorithm for Large Language Models](https://arxiv.org/abs/2502.02787): Introduces robust sentence-level watermarking for large language models, adding a provenance and misuse-detection control method.
- [Universal Steering and Monitoring](https://arxiv.org/abs/2502.03708): Extracts scalable concept representations across language, vision-language, and reasoning models for steering and monitoring.
- ["Short-length" Adversarial Training Helps LLMs Defend "Long-length" Jailbreak Attacks: Theoretical and Empirical Evidence](https://arxiv.org/abs/2502.04204): Shows short adversarial suffixes can train defenses that transfer to longer jailbreak prompts, reducing the cost of adversarial safety alignment.
- [On the Difficulty of Constructing a Robust and Publicly-Detectable Watermark](https://arxiv.org/abs/2502.04901): Studies trade-offs that make public LLM watermarks hard to keep both robust and detectable under adaptive removal attacks.
- [KDA: A Knowledge-Distilled Attacker for Generating Diverse Prompts to Jailbreak LLMs](https://arxiv.org/abs/2502.05223): Distills attack knowledge into a prompt generator that produces diverse jailbreak prompts, improving attack coverage without manual prompt engineering.
- [Koel-TTS: Enhancing LLM based Speech Generation with Preference Alignment and Classifier Free Guidance](https://arxiv.org/abs/2502.05236): Aligns autoregressive speech generation with ASR and speaker-verification preference signals plus classifier-free guidance to reduce hallucinated or off-condition speech.
- [Can LLMs Rank the Harmfulness of Smaller LLMs? We are Not There Yet](https://arxiv.org/abs/2502.05291): Evaluates LLM-as-judge harmfulness ranking for smaller models and finds current judges unreliable for precise safety ordering.
- [You Are What You Eat](https://arxiv.org/abs/2502.05475): Studies how training data shapes model structure and generalization, framing data effects as an alignment concern.
- [Mix Data or Merge Models?](https://arxiv.org/abs/2502.06876): Studies data mixing versus model merging for balancing helpfulness, honesty, and harmlessness in LLM alignment.
- [General-Purpose f-DP Estimation and Auditing in a Black-Box Setting](https://arxiv.org/abs/2502.07066): Estimates f-Differential Privacy from black-box access, giving privacy auditors a model-agnostic way to bound leakage without inspecting training code.
- [LUNAR: LLM Unlearning via Neural Activation Redirection](https://arxiv.org/abs/2502.07218): Redirects activations for forgotten data into refusal-like regions, giving controllable LLM unlearning without relying on contrastive features or destructive weight edits.
- [JBShield: Defending Large Language Models from Jailbreak Attacks through Activated Concept Analysis and Manipulation](https://arxiv.org/abs/2502.07557): Extracts and manipulates toxic and jailbreak concepts in model activations to analyze and defend against jailbreak prompts.
- [MAA: Meticulous Adversarial Attack against Vision-Language Pre-trained Models](https://arxiv.org/abs/2502.08079): Improves transferable adversarial attacks against vision-language pretrained models through fine-grained image optimization, resizing and sliding crops, and multi-granularity similarity disruption.
- [LLM-Enhanced Multiple Instance Learning for Joint Rumor and Stance Detection with Social Context Information](https://arxiv.org/abs/2502.08888): Combines LLM-enhanced multiple-instance learning with social context to jointly detect rumors and stance, a misinformation-safety model that may need parent routing.
- [Diffusing DeBias: Synthetic Bias Amplification for Model Debiasing](https://arxiv.org/abs/2502.09564): Amplifies bias synthetically to create stronger debiasing supervision, then trains models to reduce biased associations.
- [Jailbreaking to Jailbreak](https://arxiv.org/abs/2502.09638): Studies using jailbreaks to produce or strengthen further jailbreak attacks, relevant to adversarial safety evaluation.
- [Making Them a Malicious Database: Exploiting Query Code to Jailbreak Aligned Large Language Models](https://arxiv.org/abs/2502.09723): Turns aligned LLMs into malicious database-like responders by encoding harmful requests as query code, exposing a code-structured jailbreak path.
- [Jailbreak Attack Initializations as Extractors of Compliance Directions](https://arxiv.org/abs/2502.09755): Uses jailbreak initializations to extract compliance directions in representation space, revealing the latent geometry that separates refusal from harmful obedience.
- [Injecting Universal Jailbreak Backdoors into LLMs in Minutes](https://arxiv.org/abs/2502.10438): Studies fast insertion of universal jailbreak backdoors, adding a model-side threat reference for safety alignment.
- [VLM-Guard](https://arxiv.org/abs/2502.10486): Addresses the safety-alignment gap in vision-language models, adding a model-side safeguard line for multimodal agent inputs.
- [Towards Watermarking of Open-Source LLMs](https://arxiv.org/abs/2502.10525): Studies watermarking open-source LLMs, relevant to model provenance and misuse mitigation.
- [RLHF in an SFT Way: From Optimal Solution to Reward-Weighted Alignment](https://arxiv.org/abs/2502.11026): Recasts RLHF in an SFT-style reward-weighted form, simplifying preference alignment for safer model behavior.
- [MMUnlearner: Reformulating Multimodal Machine Unlearning in the Era of Multimodal Large Language Models](https://arxiv.org/abs/2502.11051): Defines MLLM unlearning as erasing visual entity patterns while retaining textual knowledge, using geometry-constrained gradient ascent with weight saliency.
- [Rewrite to Jailbreak](https://arxiv.org/abs/2502.11084): Learns transferable implicit harmful instructions through rewriting, adding a model-side jailbreak-generation route for safety evaluation.
