- [ReLearn](https://arxiv.org/abs/2502.11190): Frames LLM unlearning as a learning problem. Core idea: train replacement behavior so unwanted knowledge is suppressed without relying only on destructive parameter edits.
- [ReVeil](https://arxiv.org/abs/2502.11687): uses machine unlearning to create unconstrained concealed backdoor attacks, extending unlearning-related model-safety risks.
- [StructTransform: A Scalable Attack Surface for Safety-Aligned Large Language Models](https://arxiv.org/abs/2502.11853): Applies structured transformations to harmful prompts to find scalable attack surfaces where safety-aligned LLMs stop recognizing intent.
- [Gradient Co-occurrence Analysis for Detecting Unsafe Prompts in Large Language Models](https://arxiv.org/abs/2502.12411): Detects unsafe prompts in large language models using gradient co-occurrence analysis.
- [SafeRoute: Adaptive Model Selection for Efficient and Accurate Safety Guardrails in Large Language Models](https://arxiv.org/abs/2502.12464): Uses adaptive model selection to route safety guardrail decisions efficiently and accurately across large language models.
- [SafeEraser: Enhancing Safety in Multimodal Large Language Models through Multimodal Machine Unlearning](https://arxiv.org/abs/2502.12520): Introduces a 3,000-image, 28.8K-VQA safety-unlearning benchmark for MLLMs and tests whether unlearning removes unsafe visual knowledge without collapsing utility.
- [Automating Prompt Leakage Attacks on Large Language Models Using Agentic Approach](https://arxiv.org/abs/2502.12630): Uses an agentic search loop to automate prompt-leakage attacks that recover hidden system or developer instructions.
- [R.R.: Unveiling LLM Training Privacy through Recollection and Ranking](https://arxiv.org/abs/2502.12658): Combines recollection and ranking queries to expose whether training examples are memorized, making privacy leakage measurable from model outputs.
- [Evaluation of Best-of-N Sampling Strategies for Language Model Alignment](https://arxiv.org/abs/2502.12668): Evaluates best-of-N alignment sampling and its reward-hacking failure modes.
- [Rejected Dialects: Biases Against African American Language in Reward Models](https://arxiv.org/abs/2502.12858): Analyzes biases against African American Language in reward models, highlighting how preference models can encode dialect-sensitive alignment failures.
- [UniGuardian](https://arxiv.org/abs/2502.13141): Unifies detection of prompt injection, backdoor, and adversarial attacks against LLMs in one defense model.
- [ShieldLearner: A New Paradigm for Jailbreak Attack Defense in LLMs](https://arxiv.org/abs/2502.13162): Introduces ShieldLearner for autonomous jailbreak-defense pattern learning.
- [ThinkGuard: Deliberative Slow Thinking Leads to Cautious Guardrails](https://arxiv.org/abs/2502.13458): Distills structured safety critiques from stronger LLMs into a guardrail classifier, improving cautious moderation and interpretability beyond single-pass labels.
- [Efficient Safety Retrofitting Against Jailbreaking for LLMs](https://arxiv.org/abs/2502.13603): Retrofitting LLMs with lightweight safety updates hardens them against jailbreaks without full safety retraining.
- [Safeguarded Ships Run Aground](https://arxiv.org/abs/2502.13946): Shows that aligned LLM safety mechanisms can anchor on template regions, exposing a model-internal failure mode for safety generalization.
- [How Jailbreak Defenses Work and Ensemble? A Mechanistic Investigation](https://arxiv.org/abs/2502.14486): Analyzes jailbreak defenses as refusal-classification behavior, separating safety shift from harmfulness discrimination and testing ensemble strategies for LLMs and LVLMs.
- [Length-Controlled Margin-Based Preference Optimization without Reference Model](https://arxiv.org/abs/2502.14643): The method adds length-controlled margins to preference optimization without requiring a reference model.
- [Middle-Layer Representation Alignment for Cross-Lingual Transfer in Fine-Tuned LLMs](https://arxiv.org/abs/2502.14830): Adds a middle-layer alignment objective after analyzing over 1,000 language pairs, improving cross-lingual transfer where safety or task tuning lacks data.
- [LUME](https://arxiv.org/abs/2502.15097): evaluates LLM unlearning with multitask criteria for forgetting and retention.
- [Attention Eclipse](https://arxiv.org/abs/2502.15334): manipulates attention patterns to bypass LLM safety alignment.
- [Single-pass Detection of Jailbreaking Input in Large Language Models](https://arxiv.org/abs/2502.15435): Detects jailbreak inputs in one forward pass, using internal or prompt-side signals to avoid expensive iterative defense pipelines.
- [SafeInt: Shielding Large Language Models from Jailbreak Attacks via Safety-Aware Representation Intervention](https://arxiv.org/abs/2502.15594): Intervenes on safety-aware internal representations during inference to steer jailbreak prompts back toward refusal behavior.
- [Privacy Ripple Effects from Adding or Removing Personal Information in Language Model Training](https://arxiv.org/abs/2502.15680): Shows that adding or removing PII during language-model training can create dynamic memorization effects, including assisted memorization and altered leakage risk across later training stages.
- [Investigating the Impact of Quantization Methods on the Safety and Reliability of Large Language Models](https://arxiv.org/abs/2502.15799): Compares quantization methods to measure how compression shifts LLM safety, reliability, and harmful-response behavior.
- [Merger-as-a-Stealer: Stealing Targeted PII from Aligned LLMs with Model Merging](https://arxiv.org/abs/2502.16094): Uses model merging to elicit targeted PII from aligned LLMs, showing alignment can be weakened by parameter-combination attacks.
- [Improving LLM General Preference Alignment via Optimistic Online Mirror Descent](https://arxiv.org/abs/2502.16852): Applies optimistic online mirror descent to general preference alignment, adding a model-side optimization route for safer behavior tuning.
- [Char-mander Use mBackdoor! A Study of Cross-lingual Backdoor Attacks in Multilingual LLMs](https://arxiv.org/abs/2502.16901): Studies cross-lingual backdoors in multilingual LLMs, showing triggers can transfer across languages and bypass monolingual safety checks.
- [When to Forget? Complexity Trade-offs in Machine Unlearning](https://arxiv.org/abs/2502.17323): Analyzes computational trade-offs that determine when machine unlearning is practical compared with retraining or retaining data.
- [Training large language models on narrow tasks can lead to broad misalignment](https://arxiv.org/abs/2502.17424): Shows that fine-tuning on insecure-code completions can induce emergent misalignment across unrelated prompts, distinct from ordinary jailbreak compliance and sensitive to data framing.
- [User-level Private RLHF](https://arxiv.org/abs/2502.17515): studies reinforcement learning from human feedback under user-level privacy constraints.
- [Fine-tuning-based LLM Unlearning Framework](https://arxiv.org/abs/2502.17823): Generalizes fine-tuning-based unlearning for LLMs, adding a reusable model-side forgetting recipe.
- [Stealthy Backdoor Attack in Self-Supervised Learning Vision Encoders for Large Vision Language Models](https://arxiv.org/abs/2502.18290): Shows that compromising shared self-supervised vision encoders can implant stealthy backdoors into downstream LVLMs, inducing attacker-chosen visual hallucinations after reuse.
- [OmniAlign-V: Towards Enhanced Alignment of MLLMs with Human Preference](https://arxiv.org/abs/2502.18411): Releases 200K multimodal preference-alignment samples and MM-AlignBench, then applies SFT or DPO to improve MLLM human-value alignment without degrading standard VQA ability.
- [REFINE (Backdoor Defense)](https://arxiv.org/abs/2502.18508): uses inversion-free model reprogramming as a backdoor defense.
- [What is the Alignment Objective of GRPO?](https://arxiv.org/abs/2502.18548): Analyzes the optimization objective behind GRPO, clarifying what preference or safety-alignment signal the method actually optimizes.
- [Breaking Distortion-free Watermarks in Large Language Models](https://arxiv.org/abs/2502.18608): Attacks distortion-free LLM watermarks, testing whether provenance signals survive paraphrase, sampling, and adaptive removal strategies.
- [ZEBRA: Leveraging Model-Behavioral Knowledge for Zero-Annotation Preference Dataset Construction](https://arxiv.org/abs/2502.18744): Constructs preference datasets without annotation by using model-behavioral knowledge, reducing human-label cost for alignment training.
- [Reward Shaping to Mitigate Reward Hacking in RLHF](https://arxiv.org/abs/2502.18770): Studies reward-shaping principles for RLHF reward hacking and proposes Preference As Reward, using latent reward-model preferences as the RL signal with variance reduction and a wider early-stopping window.
- [One-shot Optimized Steering Vectors Mediate Safety-relevant Behaviors in LLMs](https://arxiv.org/abs/2502.18862): Optimizes activation steering vectors from a single example to mediate safety-relevant behaviors.
- [Label-Only Membership Inference for LLMs](https://arxiv.org/abs/2502.18943): Studies label-only membership-inference attacks against pre-trained LLMs, adding a privacy-risk line for model safety.
- [Neural Antidote: Class-Wise Prompt Tuning for Purifying Backdoors in Pre-trained Vision-Language Models](https://arxiv.org/abs/2502.19269): Adds prompt-tuning purification for backdoors in pretrained vision-language models.
- [Rethinking LLM Unlearning Objectives](https://arxiv.org/abs/2502.19301): Analyzes LLM unlearning objectives from a gradient perspective and proposes improvements for more reliable forgetting.
- [Tokens for Learning, Tokens for Unlearning](https://arxiv.org/abs/2502.19726): Uses dual-purpose training to reduce membership-inference risk in LLMs, connecting unlearning with privacy attack mitigation.
- [TAPE: Tailored Posterior Difference for Auditing of Machine Unlearning](https://arxiv.org/abs/2502.19770): Audits machine unlearning with tailored posterior differences, checking whether forget-set influence remains after an unlearning update.
- [Beyond the Tip of Efficiency: Uncovering the Submerged Threats of Jailbreak Attacks in Small Language Models](https://arxiv.org/abs/2502.19883): Evaluates jailbreak risks in small language models, showing efficiency-focused deployment can hide safety weaknesses below headline capability metrics.
- [Erasing Without Remembering: Implicit Knowledge Forgetting in Large Language Models](https://arxiv.org/abs/2502.19982): Studies implicit knowledge forgetting for LLMs, erasing unwanted knowledge without first reinforcing it through explicit memorization.
- [Rectifying Belief Space via Unlearning to Harness LLMs' Reasoning](https://arxiv.org/abs/2502.20620): Uses unlearning to reshape LLM belief space, aiming to remove misleading beliefs while preserving reasoning capability.
- [The Rise of Darkness: Safety-Utility Trade-Offs in Role-Playing Dialogue Agents](https://arxiv.org/abs/2502.20757): Studies role-playing dialogue agents where stronger persona utility can increase unsafe behavior, quantifying the safety-utility trade-off.
- [Gradient Imbalance in Direct Preference Optimization](https://arxiv.org/abs/2502.20847): Analyzes gradient imbalance in DPO, showing how preference updates can overemphasize some samples and destabilize alignment training.
- [Efficient Jailbreaking of Large Models by Freeze Training: Lower Layers Exhibit Greater Sensitivity to Harmful Content](https://arxiv.org/abs/2502.20952): Uses freeze training to jailbreak large models efficiently, exploiting lower-layer sensitivity to harmful content.
- [from Benign import Toxic: Jailbreaking the Language Model via Adversarial Metaphors](https://arxiv.org/abs/2503.00038): Uses adversarial metaphors as a jailbreak route, adding a linguistic attack pattern to model-side safety coverage.
- [Steering Dialogue Dynamics](https://arxiv.org/abs/2503.00187): Steers dialogue dynamics to improve robustness against multi-turn jailbreak attacks, adding a model-side safety control method.
- [AMUN: Adversarial Machine UNlearning](https://arxiv.org/abs/2503.00917): Treats machine unlearning adversarially, selecting or crafting forget requests that expose failures in deletion and retention guarantees.
- [Zero-Trust Artificial Intelligence Model Security Based on Moving Target Defense and Content Disarm and Reconstruction](https://arxiv.org/abs/2503.01758): Combines moving-target defense with content disarm-and-reconstruction for zero-trust model security, a deployment-defense item that may need parent routing.
- [Output Length Effect on DeepSeek-R1's Safety in Forced Thinking](https://arxiv.org/abs/2503.01923): A model, training, or algorithmic contribution for agent safety and security evaluation. Core idea: focus on output length effect on deepseek-r1's safety in forced thinking as a capability-relevant model reference.
- [Linear Representations of Political Perspective](https://arxiv.org/abs/2503.02080): Finds political-perspective directions in LLM representations, informing bias, viewpoint, and safety steering work.
- [Go Beyond Your Means: Unlearning with Per-Sample Gradient Orthogonalization](https://arxiv.org/abs/2503.02312): Orthogonalizes per-sample gradients during unlearning so forget updates conflict less with retained-data performance.
- [Reduced Spatial Dependency for More General Video-level Deepfake Detection](https://arxiv.org/abs/2503.03270): Reduces spatial bias in video deepfake detection with spatially perturbed feature clusters, task-relevant feature integration, and a temporal transformer.
- [Rethinking Video Super-Resolution: Towards Diffusion-Based Methods without Motion Alignment](https://arxiv.org/abs/2503.03355): Contributes a agent safety model-side method, training recipe, architecture, verifier, reward, world-model, or synthetic-data technique.
- [CLIP is Strong Enough to Fight Back: Test-time Counterattacks towards Zero-shot Adversarial Robustness of CLIP](https://arxiv.org/abs/2503.03613): Uses CLIP itself at test time to counter adversarial perturbations, improving zero-shot robustness without retraining.
- [Improving LLM Safety Alignment with Dual-Objective Optimization](https://arxiv.org/abs/2503.03710): Splits safety alignment into robust refusal learning and targeted harmful-knowledge unlearning, improving resistance to prefill, suffix, and multi-turn jailbreaks.
- [Robust Data Watermarking in Language Models by Injecting Fictitious Knowledge](https://arxiv.org/abs/2503.04036): Injects fictitious knowledge into training data as a watermark so later model responses can reveal dataset provenance robustly.
- [Mark Your LLM: Detecting the Misuse of Open-Source Large Language Models via Watermarking](https://arxiv.org/abs/2503.04636): Adds a model-side method, training recipe, architecture, inference strategy, or algorithmic capability for agent safety.
- [Cross-linguistic disagreement as a conflict of semantic alignment norms in multilingual AI~Linguistic Diversity as a Problem for Philosophy, Cognitive Science, and AI~](https://arxiv.org/abs/2503.04792): Contributes a agent safety model-side method, training recipe, architecture, verifier, reward, world-model, or synthetic-data technique.
- [Cyber for AI Selective Unlearning](https://arxiv.org/abs/2503.04795): studies the forgetting-retention balance for large-language-model selective unlearning in SemEval-2025 Task 4.
- [One-Shot is Enough: Consolidating Multi-Turn Attacks into Efficient Single-Turn Prompts for LLMs](https://arxiv.org/abs/2503.04856): Consolidates multi-turn jailbreak attacks into efficient single-turn prompts, adding an attack-transformation method for LLM safety evaluation.
- [Learning LLM Preference over Intra-Dialogue Pairs: A Framework for Utterance-level Understandings](https://arxiv.org/abs/2503.05620): Uses LLM-generated labels plus human annotations to train smaller per-utterance dialogue classifiers for low-latency intent and safety-relevant understanding tasks.
- [AIM-Fair: Advancing Algorithmic Fairness via Selectively Fine-Tuning Biased Models with Contextual Synthetic Data](https://arxiv.org/abs/2503.05665): Fine-tunes biased models on diffusion-generated unbiased synthetic data and uses contextual filtering to improve fairness without relying on demographic group labels.
- [CBW Speaker-Verification Watermarking](https://arxiv.org/abs/2503.05794): uses clustering-based backdoor watermarking for dataset ownership verification in speaker-verification systems.
- [Reinforced Diffuser for Red Teaming Large Vision-Language Models](https://arxiv.org/abs/2503.06223): Trains a reinforced diffusion generator to create adversarial visual inputs for VLM red teaming, expanding multimodal jailbreak coverage.
- [Learning to Unlearn While Retaining: Combating Gradient Conflicts in Machine Unlearning](https://arxiv.org/abs/2503.06339): Addresses gradient conflicts between forgetting and retention objectives, improving unlearning while preserving retained capabilities.
- [Gender Encoding Patterns in Pretrained Language Model Representations](https://arxiv.org/abs/2503.06734): Uses information-theoretic analysis to trace how encoder PLMs store gender information, showing debiasing can reduce outputs while increasing internal encoded bias.
- [Privacy Auditing of Large Language Models](https://arxiv.org/abs/2503.06808): Develops privacy-auditing procedures for LLMs that probe memorization and training-data leakage rather than only reporting aggregate utility.
- [Jailbreak Probability for MLLMs](https://arxiv.org/abs/2503.06989): Uses jailbreak-probability signals both to attack and safeguard multimodal LLMs.
- [Dialogue Injection Attack: Jailbreaking LLMs Through Context Manipulation](https://arxiv.org/abs/2503.08195): Jailbreaks LLMs through context manipulation in dialogue, adding a multi-turn safety attack reference.
- [Prompt Inversion Attack Against Collaborative Inference](https://arxiv.org/abs/2503.09022): Attacks collaborative LLM inference by reconstructing prompts, adding a privacy-risk line for split or assisted inference settings.
- [Probing Latent Subspaces in LLM for AI Security: Identifying and Manipulating Adversarial States](https://arxiv.org/abs/2503.09066): Identifies and manipulates adversarial latent states for LLM security.
- [GRU for LLM Unlearning](https://arxiv.org/abs/2503.09117): Targets the unlearning-retention trade-off in LLMs, adding a model-side forgetting method for safety and privacy control.
- [Prompt Inference Attack on Distributed LLM Inference](https://arxiv.org/abs/2503.09291): studies prompt leakage risks in distributed large-language-model inference frameworks.
- [BiasConnect: Investigating Bias Interactions in Text-to-Image Models](https://arxiv.org/abs/2503.09763): Studies how multiple bias dimensions interact in text-to-image generation, exposing compound fairness risks beyond single-attribute audits.
- [PluralLLM: Pluralistic Alignment in LLMs via Federated Learning](https://arxiv.org/abs/2503.09925): Trains a federated transformer preference predictor with FedAvg so user groups can contribute pluralistic alignment signals without centralizing sensitive preference data.
- [Policy Teaching via Data Poisoning in Learning from Human Preferences](https://arxiv.org/abs/2503.10228): Uses data poisoning in preference-learning pipelines to teach hidden policies, exposing how compromised human-feedback data can redirect aligned models.
- [Semantic LVLM Attack Baseline](https://arxiv.org/abs/2503.10635): Builds a simple high-success black-box attack baseline against frontier LVLMs by making perturbations semantically clearer.
- [CeTAD: Towards Certified Toxicity-Aware Distance in Vision Language Models](https://arxiv.org/abs/2503.10661): Defines a certified toxicity-aware distance for VLMs, linking representation perturbations to toxicity risk rather than relying only on output moderation labels.
- [TAIJI: Textual Anchoring for Immunizing Jailbreak Images in Vision Language Models](https://arxiv.org/abs/2503.10872): Defends against jailbreak or prompt-injection attacks with TAIJI, focusing on the bypass channel and refusal or filtering behavior.
- [PrivacyScalpel](https://arxiv.org/abs/2503.11232): Uses sparse-autoencoder feature intervention to improve LLM privacy, adding an interpretable model-side privacy guardrail.
- [Towards Better Alignment: Training Diffusion Models with Reinforcement Learning Against Sparse Rewards](https://arxiv.org/abs/2503.11240): Adds a model-side method, training recipe, architecture, inference strategy, or algorithmic capability for agent safety.
- [Tit-for-Tat: Safeguarding Large Vision-Language Models Against Jailbreak Attacks via Adversarial Defense](https://arxiv.org/abs/2503.11619): Proposes an adversarial-defense strategy for safeguarding large vision-language models against jailbreak attacks.
- [Safety Mirage](https://arxiv.org/abs/2503.11832): Shows how spurious correlations can undermine VLM safety fine-tuning, exposing a model-side safety generalization risk.
- [DecAlign: Hierarchical Cross-Modal Alignment for Decoupled Multimodal Representation Learning](https://arxiv.org/abs/2503.11892): Adds a model-side method, training recipe, architecture, inference strategy, or algorithmic capability for agent safety.
- [Monitoring Reasoning Models for Misbehavior](https://arxiv.org/abs/2503.11926): Shows that chain-of-thought monitors can detect reward hacking in agentic coding environments, while over-optimizing directly against such monitors can produce obfuscated misbehavior.
- [Empirical Privacy Variance](https://arxiv.org/abs/2503.12314): Measures empirical privacy variance across training examples, showing that average privacy guarantees can hide uneven leakage risk.
- [Synthesizing Privacy-Preserving Text Data via Finetuning without Finetuning Billion-Scale LLMs](https://arxiv.org/abs/2503.12347): Generates private synthetic text by training a lightweight controllable generator instead of fine-tuning billion-scale LLMs.
- [Investigating Human-Aligned Large Language Model Uncertainty](https://arxiv.org/abs/2503.12528): Compares LLM uncertainty metrics against human group-level uncertainty, finding Bayesian measures and top-k entropy align better than some scale-sensitive alternatives.
- [Dynamic Maximin UAP](https://arxiv.org/abs/2503.12793): improves universal adversarial perturbation generalization through dynamic maximin optimization.
- [From Demonstrations to Rewards: Alignment Without Explicit Human Preferences](https://arxiv.org/abs/2503.13538): Learns reward signals from demonstrations instead of explicit preference labels, offering an alignment route when pairwise human feedback is unavailable.
- [Privacy-Aware RAG: Secure and Isolated Knowledge Retrieval](https://arxiv.org/abs/2503.15548): Designs RAG with isolated knowledge retrieval so private documents can be queried without cross-user leakage.
- [BadToken: Token-level Backdoor Attacks to Multi-modal Large Language Models](https://arxiv.org/abs/2503.16023): Introduces token-level backdoor attacks for MLLMs, using token substitution or addition to alter outputs on triggered multimodal inputs while preserving normal utility.
- [Human Preferences for Constructive Interactions in Language Model Alignment](https://arxiv.org/abs/2503.16480): Collects preference signals for constructive interactions, targeting alignment behavior beyond simple helpfulness or refusal labels.
- [Capturing Individual Human Preferences with Reward Features](https://arxiv.org/abs/2503.17338): Represents individual preferences as reward features, enabling personalization and pluralistic alignment without collapsing all feedback into one scalar reward.
- [STShield: Single-Token Sentinel for Real-Time Jailbreak Detection in Large Language Models](https://arxiv.org/abs/2503.17932): Uses a single-token sentinel for real-time jailbreak detection in large language models.
- [MIRAGE: Multimodal Immersive Reasoning and Guided Exploration for Red-Team Jailbreak Attacks](https://arxiv.org/abs/2503.19134): Decomposes toxic requests into environment, role, and action triplets, then uses generated image-text detective narratives to lower MLLM defenses over multiple turns.
- [Safety-Capability Trade-offs in Fine-Tuning](https://arxiv.org/abs/2503.20807): Studies safety-capability trade-offs during LLM fine-tuning, clarifying how adaptation can shift alignment behavior.
- [ENCORE: Entropy-guided Reward Composition for Multi-head Safety Reward Models](https://arxiv.org/abs/2503.20995): Composes multi-head safety reward models with entropy guidance so uncertain risk categories receive calibrated reward signals.
- [Tricking Retrievers with Influential Tokens: An Efficient Black-Box Corpus Poisoning Attack](https://arxiv.org/abs/2503.21315): Shows how influential tokens can poison black-box retrievers, adding a retrieval-side attack model for RAG and agent safety.
- [Optimizing Safe and Aligned Language Generation: A Multi-Objective GRPO Approach](https://arxiv.org/abs/2503.21819): Uses a multi-objective GRPO formulation to optimize safe and aligned language generation, adding an RL-style post-training route for safety alignment.
- [Protecting Your Video Content](https://arxiv.org/abs/2503.21824): disrupts automated video-based LLM annotations, exposing privacy and misuse risks around video-language systems.
- [AI-based Traffic Modeling for Network Security and Privacy: Challenges Ahead](https://arxiv.org/abs/2503.22161): Reviews AI network-traffic analysis for anomaly detection, censorship resistance, fingerprinting, and encrypted-traffic privacy leakage.
- [DELETE](https://arxiv.org/abs/2503.23751): Uses decoupled distillation to erase class-centric knowledge while preserving retained classes, adding a general unlearning method.
- [DiffScale: Continuous Downscaling and Bias Correction of Subseasonal Wind Speed Forecasts Using Diffusion Models](https://arxiv.org/abs/2503.23893): Uses a diffusion model with classifier-free guidance to downscale subseasonal wind-speed forecasts, making it an energy-forecasting model outside core agent safety.
- [Do Large Language Models Exhibit Spontaneous Rational Deception?](https://arxiv.org/abs/2504.00285): Tests whether LLMs strategically deceive under incentive-like settings, separating rational deception behavior from ordinary hallucination or factual error.
- [FA3-CLIP: Frequency-Aware Cues Fusion and Attack-Agnostic Prompt Learning for Unified Face Attack Detection](https://arxiv.org/abs/2504.00454): Fuses frequency-aware cues with attack-agnostic prompt learning in CLIP to detect both physical and digital face attacks.
- [Mixture-of-Attack-Experts with Class Regularization for Unified Physical-Digital Face Attack Detection](https://arxiv.org/abs/2504.00458): Uses a mixture of attack experts plus class regularization to unify physical and digital face-attack detection.
- [STAR-1: Safer Alignment of Reasoning LLMs with 1K Data](https://arxiv.org/abs/2504.01903): Aligns reasoning LLMs for safer behavior using a compact training set.
- [MultiNeRF: Multiple Watermark Embedding for Neural Radiance Fields](https://arxiv.org/abs/2504.02517): Embeds multiple watermarks into neural radiance fields, supporting ownership tracing for 3D scene representations.
- [The Hidden Space of Safety: Understanding Preference-Tuned LLMs in Multilingual context](https://arxiv.org/abs/2504.02708): Studies preference-tuned LLM safety in multilingual contexts.
- [Learning Natural Language Constraints for Safe Reinforcement Learning of Language Agents](https://arxiv.org/abs/2504.03185): Learns task rewards and latent safety constraints from positive and negative demonstrations in a CMDP, improving language-agent adaptation under new danger zones and adversarial inputs.
- [Sample, Don't Search: Rethinking Test-Time Alignment for Language Models](https://arxiv.org/abs/2504.03790): Argues sampling-based test-time alignment can avoid reward-model search over-optimization.
- [PolyGuard: A Multilingual Safety Moderation Tool for 17 Languages](https://arxiv.org/abs/2504.04377): Releases a 17-language safety model plus POLYGUARDMIX and POLYGUARDPROMPTS, labeling prompt harmfulness, response harmfulness, and refusal for multilingual guardrail training and evaluation.
- [Exact Unlearning of Finetuning Data via Model Merging at Scale](https://arxiv.org/abs/2504.04626): Uses model merging for exact finetuning-data unlearning at scale, relevant to safety and privacy-oriented model editing.
- [COIG-P: A High-Quality and Large-Scale Chinese Preference Dataset for Alignment with Human Values](https://arxiv.org/abs/2504.05535): Builds a million-pair Chinese preference dataset from filtered queries, fifteen LLM generators, and automated scoring for value-alignment training.
- [Sugar-Coated Poison: Benign Generation Unlocks LLM Jailbreaking](https://arxiv.org/abs/2504.05652): Uses benign-looking generation steps to unlock jailbreak behavior, showing harmful compliance can be staged through apparently safe intermediate outputs.
- [Mind the Trojan Horse: Image Prompt Adapter Enabling Scalable and Deceptive Jailbreaking](https://arxiv.org/abs/2504.05838): Trains an image prompt adapter that scales deceptive VLM jailbreaks by hiding harmful intent in visual prompts.
- [Agent Guide: A Simple Agent Behavioral Watermarking Framework](https://arxiv.org/abs/2504.05871): Adds behavioral watermarks to agent action patterns so downstream observers can identify guided or authorized agent behavior.
- [Adversarial Training of Reward Models](https://arxiv.org/abs/2504.06141): Trains reward models against adversarial examples so preference scores are less vulnerable to reward hacking or optimized misleading responses.
- [Sharpness-Aware Parameter Selection for Machine Unlearning](https://arxiv.org/abs/2504.06398): Selects parameters for unlearning according to sharpness so forget updates target sensitive regions while reducing retained-task damage.
- [Understanding Machine Unlearning Through the Lens of Mode Connectivity](https://arxiv.org/abs/2504.06407): Analyzes machine unlearning with mode connectivity, testing whether forget and retain solutions remain connected in parameter space.
- [Defending LLM Watermarking Against Spoofing Attacks with Contrastive Representation Learning](https://arxiv.org/abs/2504.06575): Uses contrastive representation learning to distinguish genuine watermarked text from spoofed watermark patterns, hardening provenance detectors.
- [Bridging the Gap Between Preference Alignment and Machine Unlearning](https://arxiv.org/abs/2504.06659): Connects preference alignment objectives with machine unlearning so forgetting updates can preserve aligned behavior while removing targeted knowledge.
- [Dynamic Residual Safe Reinforcement Learning for Multi-Agent Safety-Critical Scenarios Decision-Making](https://arxiv.org/abs/2504.06670): Uses dynamic residual safe RL for multi-agent safety-critical decisions, a control-policy item that likely needs parent routing outside LLM model safety.
- [TASTE: Text-Aligned Speech Tokenization and Embedding for Spoken Language Modeling](https://arxiv.org/abs/2504.07053): Adds a model-side method, training recipe, architecture, inference strategy, or algorithmic capability for agent safety.
- [Boosting Universal LLM Reward Design through Heuristic Reward Observation Space Evolution](https://arxiv.org/abs/2504.07596): Evolves heuristic reward observation spaces to improve universal LLM reward design, targeting reward-model coverage rather than prompt-only guardrails.
- [SAEs Can Improve Unlearning: Dynamic Sparse Autoencoder Guardrails for Precision Unlearning in LLMs](https://arxiv.org/abs/2504.08192): Uses sparse-autoencoder guardrails to improve unlearning.
- [EO-VLM: VLM-Guided Energy Overload Attacks on Vision Models](https://arxiv.org/abs/2504.08205): Uses VLM prompts and generated adversarial images to overload GPU energy consumption in vision models, exposing availability risks from imperceptible inputs.
- [Understanding the Impact of Data Domain Extraction on Synthetic Data Privacy](https://arxiv.org/abs/2504.08254): Shows extracting tabular data domains from private training data can break end-to-end DP guarantees, while public or DP-extracted domains reduce membership-inference risk.
- [Detecting Instruction Fine-tuning Attacks using Influence Function](https://arxiv.org/abs/2504.09026): Detects instruction fine-tuning attacks by comparing influence-function distributions under semantic transformations and flagging stable high-influence poisons.
- [The Structural Safety Generalization Problem](https://arxiv.org/abs/2504.09712): Frames jailbreak robustness as a structural generalization problem, where safety training over seen prompt forms fails to transfer to novel attack structures.
- [Do We Really Need Curated Malicious Data for Safety Alignment in Multi-modal Large Language Models?](https://arxiv.org/abs/2504.10000): Shows MLLM safety gains can come from correcting data-distribution bias, then fine-tunes on small benign multimodal datasets rather than curated malicious data.
- [Large Language Model-Informed Feature Discovery Improves Prediction and Interpretation of Credibility Perceptions of Visual Content](https://arxiv.org/abs/2504.10878): Uses GPT-4o-style feature discovery to extract interpretable visual credibility factors from social-media posts and improve misinformation-risk prediction.
- [QAVA](https://arxiv.org/abs/2504.11038): introduces query-agnostic visual attacks against large vision-language models.
- [Backdoor Attack and Defense for LLM-Empowered Recommendations](https://arxiv.org/abs/2504.11182): studies backdoor attacks and defenses in LLM-empowered recommendation systems, adding an application-side model-safety risk.
- [R-TPT: Improving Adversarial Robustness of Vision-Language Models through Test-Time Prompt Tuning](https://arxiv.org/abs/2504.11195): Performs robust test-time prompt tuning for VLMs by modifying the entropy objective so prompts can adapt against adversarial images at inference time.
- [PATFinger: Prompt-Adapted Transferable Fingerprinting against Unauthorized Multimodal Dataset Usage](https://arxiv.org/abs/2504.11509): Embeds prompt-adapted transferable fingerprints into multimodal datasets so unauthorized VLM pretraining can later be detected.
- [VLMGuard-R1: Proactive Safety Alignment for VLMs via Reasoning-Driven Prompt Optimization](https://arxiv.org/abs/2504.12661): Uses reasoning-driven prompt optimization for proactive safety alignment of VLMs.
- [UncAD: Towards Safe End-to-end Autonomous Driving via Online Map Uncertainty](https://arxiv.org/abs/2504.12826): Adds a model-side method, training recipe, architecture, inference strategy, or algorithmic capability for agent safety.
- [In Which Areas of Technical AI Safety Could Geopolitical Rivals Cooperate?](https://arxiv.org/abs/2504.12914): Analyzes technical AI safety cooperation areas by capability-advancement risk, sensitive-information sharing, and misuse opportunity.
- [GraphAttack: Exploiting Representational Blindspots in LLM Safety Mechanisms](https://arxiv.org/abs/2504.13052): Exploits representational blind spots in LLM safety mechanisms with graph-structured jailbreak prompts that preserve harmful intent while evading filters.
- [A mean teacher algorithm for unlearning of language models](https://arxiv.org/abs/2504.13388): Applies a mean-teacher consistency setup to language-model unlearning, reducing memorization of selected text while retaining general model ability.
- [Q-FAKER: Query-free Hard Black-box Attack via Controlled Generation](https://arxiv.org/abs/2504.13551): Introduces model-side work on q-FAKER: Query-free Hard Black-box Attack via Controlled Generation, fitting training, architecture, post-training, synthetic-data, verifier, reward, or world-model coverage.
- [DETAM](https://arxiv.org/abs/2504.13562): Defends LLMs against jailbreaks through targeted attention modification, adding an internal-intervention route beyond prompt rewriting or finetuning.
- [Hydra: An Agentic Reasoning Approach for Enhancing Adversarial Robustness and Mitigating Hallucinations in Vision-Language Models](https://arxiv.org/abs/2504.14395): Wraps VLMs in an action-critique loop with iterative reasoning, visual retrieval, and cross-model verification to reduce adversarial errors and hallucinations.
- [MR. Guard: Multilingual Reasoning Guardrail using Curriculum Learning](https://arxiv.org/abs/2504.15241): Trains a multilingual reasoning guardrail with culturally nuanced synthetic data, SFT, and curriculum GRPO, improving prompt classification across code-switching and low-resource language distractors.
- [Pre-DPO: Improving Data Utilization in Direct Preference Optimization Using a Guiding Reference Model](https://arxiv.org/abs/2504.15843): Adds a guiding reference model before DPO so preference data can be weighted or selected more effectively during alignment.
- [Certified Mitigation of Worst-Case LLM Copyright Infringement](https://arxiv.org/abs/2504.16046): Introduces BloomScrub, an inference-time copyright takedown method that alternates quote detection, rewriting, Bloom-filter screening, and abstention for certified worst-case risk reduction.
- [Beauty and the Bias: Exploring the Impact of Attractiveness on Multimodal Large Language Models](https://arxiv.org/abs/2504.16104): Tests seven MLLMs on 91 social decision scenarios with paired face images, showing attractiveness filters systematically bias model judgments.
- [Agree to Disagree? A Meta-Evaluation of LLM Misgendering](https://arxiv.org/abs/2504.17075): Meta-evaluates probability-based and generation-based misgendering tests across datasets and models, showing automatic metrics disagree and miss harms beyond pronouns.
- [Steering the CensorShip: Uncovering Representation Vectors for LLM "Thought" Control](https://arxiv.org/abs/2504.17130): Uses representation engineering to find refusal-compliance and thought-suppression vectors, exposing how safety-tuned and reasoning LLMs can be steered around refusal behavior.
- [Towards Generalizable Deepfake Detection with Spatial-Frequency Collaborative Learning and Hierarchical Cross-Modal Fusion](https://arxiv.org/abs/2504.17223): Combines local spectral extraction, global spectral cues, and hierarchical cross-modal fusion to improve deepfake detection on unseen forgeries.
- [CoheMark: A Novel Sentence-Level Watermark for Enhanced Text Quality](https://arxiv.org/abs/2504.17309): Uses cohesive sentence relationships, clustering, and next-sentence prediction to embed sentence-level LLM watermarks while preserving text fluency.
- [Backdoor Defense in Diffusion Models via Spatial Attention Unlearning](https://arxiv.org/abs/2504.18563): Contributes a agent safety model-side method, training recipe, architecture, verifier, reward, world-model, or synthetic-data technique.
- [BadMoE: Backdooring Mixture-of-Experts LLMs via Optimizing Routing Triggers and Infecting Dormant Experts](https://arxiv.org/abs/2504.18598): Backdoors MoE LLMs through routing triggers and dormant experts.
- [Contextual Online Uncertainty-Aware Preference Learning for Human Feedback](https://arxiv.org/abs/2504.19342): Gives RLHF a contextual online preference-learning algorithm with uncertainty-aware exploration and statistical inference guarantees for optimal model selection.
- [JailbreaksOverTime: Detecting Jailbreak Attacks Under Distribution Shift](https://arxiv.org/abs/2504.19440): Studies jailbreak-attack detection under distribution shift, adding a temporal robustness angle for safety detectors.
- [Task-specific Prompts and SAEs for Activation Monitoring](https://arxiv.org/abs/2504.20271): Combines prompts with sparse autoencoders for activation monitoring, adding a model-side monitoring technique for safety-relevant behavior.
- [Token-Efficient Prompt Injection Attack](https://arxiv.org/abs/2504.20493): Uses adaptive token compression to provoke cessation in LLM reasoning, adding a compact prompt-injection attack route for reasoning models.
- [ReCIT: Reconstructing Full Private Data from Gradient in Parameter-Efficient Fine-Tuning of Large Language Models](https://arxiv.org/abs/2504.20570): Reconstructs both contextual prefixes and PII from PEFT gradients, showing federated or collaborative fine-tuning can leak full private training instances.
- [GaussTrap: Stealthy Poisoning Attacks on 3D Gaussian Splatting for Targeted Scene Confusion](https://arxiv.org/abs/2504.20829): Injects malicious training views into 3D Gaussian Splatting so targeted viewpoints render confusing scenes while normal views remain high quality.
- [Kill two birds with one stone: generalized and robust AI-generated text detection via dynamic perturbations](https://arxiv.org/abs/2504.21019): Proposes DP-Net, using reinforcement-learned dynamic perturbations so AI-generated text detection generalizes across generators while staying robust to attacks.
- [Prefill-level Jailbreak: A Black-Box Risk Analysis of Large Language Models](https://arxiv.org/abs/2504.21038): Analyzes black-box jailbreaks that seed the assistant prefill with unsafe continuations, showing refusal behavior can be bypassed before normal response generation begins.
- [NeuRel-Attack: Neuron Relearning for Safety Disalignment in Large Language Models](https://arxiv.org/abs/2504.21053): Identifies safety-critical neurons from harmful-versus-harmless activation patterns and relearns them to remove refusal constraints.
- [SafeQuant: LLM Safety Analysis via Quantized Gradient Inspection](https://doi.org/10.18653/v1/2025.naacl-long.127): Inspects quantized gradients to analyze LLM safety behavior, using gradient signals as a lightweight diagnostic for unsafe-output tendencies.
- [Goal-Conditioned DPO: Prioritizing Safety in Misaligned Instructions](https://doi.org/10.18653/v1/2025.naacl-long.369): Modifies DPO with goal conditioning so preference updates prioritize safety when instructions are misaligned, separating benign helpfulness from unsafe compliance.
- [Learning from Literature: A Retraining-Free Framework for LLM Jailbreak Defense via NLP-Based Adversarial Literature Analysis](https://arxiv.org/abs/2505.01315): Filters and summarizes encoded or manipulative inputs before model execution, a retraining-free runtime defense that is an Agent Harness candidate.
- [The DCR Delusion: Measuring the Privacy Risk of Synthetic Data](https://arxiv.org/abs/2505.01524): Shows distance-to-closest-record can understate synthetic-data privacy risk, arguing for attack-based membership and reconstruction evaluation instead.
- [Soft Best-of-$n$ Sampling for Model Alignment](https://arxiv.org/abs/2505.03156): Generalizes best-of-N alignment sampling with a temperature-controlled soft selection rule, giving model-side control over the reward and distribution-distortion trade-off during inference-time alignment.
- [Automatic Calibration for Membership Inference Attack on Large Language Models](https://arxiv.org/abs/2505.03392): Calibrates membership inference with a tunable temperature derived from likelihood-estimation analysis, reducing false positives without extra reference models.
- [Revisiting Model Inversion Evaluation: From Misleading Standards to Reliable Privacy Assessment](https://arxiv.org/abs/2505.03519): Shows standard model-inversion evaluation can count adversarial false positives as successes and proposes reliability checks for privacy assessment.
- [Large Means Left: Political Bias in Large Language Models Increases with Their Number of Parameters](https://arxiv.org/abs/2505.04393): Measures political bias across LLM sizes and reports that larger parameter counts correlate with stronger left-leaning output bias.
- [Perpetuating Misogyny with Generative AI: How Model Personalization Normalizes Gendered Harm](https://arxiv.org/abs/2505.04600): Studies how personalized text-to-image adapters normalize gendered harm, making model personalization itself a safety and bias surface.
- [X-Transfer Attacks on CLIP](https://arxiv.org/abs/2505.05528): studies highly transferable adversarial attacks on CLIP, extending multimodal model-safety and robustness coverage.
- [LLM-Text Watermarking based on Lagrange Interpolation](https://arxiv.org/abs/2505.05712): Embeds multi-bit author identity into LLM text with Lagrange-interpolation structure, aiming to recover provenance even after heavy redaction.
- [SRAF](https://arxiv.org/abs/2505.06304): Optimizes stealthy adversarial fingerprints for black-box LLM copyright and ownership verification under prompts and model modifications.
- [Understanding and Mitigating Toxicity in Image-Text Pretraining Datasets: A Case Study on LLaVA](https://arxiv.org/abs/2505.06356): Audits LLaVA image-text pretraining toxicity and removes 7,531 toxic pairs, giving data-filtering guidance for safer multimodal pretraining.
- [Unmasking Deep Fakes: Leveraging Deep Learning for Video Authenticity Detection](https://arxiv.org/abs/2505.06528): Combines MTCNN face detection with an EfficientNet-B5 encoder to classify DFDC videos, adding a concrete deepfake-video detector.
- [Think in Safety: Unveiling and Mitigating Safety Alignment Collapse in Multimodal Large Reasoning Model](https://arxiv.org/abs/2505.06538): Diagnoses and mitigates safety-alignment collapse in multimodal large reasoning models.
- [One Trigger Token Is Enough](https://arxiv.org/abs/2505.07167): Proposes a trigger-token defense balancing safety and usability in LLMs. Core idea: activate safety behavior through a compact control signal rather than globally degrading helpfulness.
- [GRADA: Graph-based Reranking against Adversarial Documents Attack](https://arxiv.org/abs/2505.07546): Uses graph-based reranking to defend retrieval-augmented systems against adversarial documents.
- [Adversarial Suffix Filtering: a Defense Pipeline for LLMs](https://arxiv.org/abs/2505.09602): Detects and filters adversarial suffixes before LLM execution, providing a defense pipeline for suffix-based jailbreaks in deployed systems.
- [SpecWav-Attack: Leveraging Spectrogram Resizing and Wav2Vec 2.0 for Attacking Anonymized Speech](https://arxiv.org/abs/2505.09616): Uses spectrogram resizing and Wav2Vec 2.0 features to attack anonymized speech, exposing privacy leakage through speaker re-identification.
- [Adversarial Attack on Large Language Models using Exponentiated Gradient Descent](https://arxiv.org/abs/2505.09820): Adapts exponentiated gradient descent to search adversarial LLM prompts, adding an optimization-based attack route for safety evaluation.
- [Dark LLMs: The Growing Threat of Unaligned AI Models](https://arxiv.org/abs/2505.10066): Characterizes unaligned or maliciously adapted LLMs as a misuse threat, making it a threat-taxonomy or governance candidate rather than a training method.
- [LARGO: Latent Adversarial Reflection through Gradient Optimization for Jailbreaking LLMs](https://arxiv.org/abs/2505.10838): Optimizes adversarial latent vectors and has the same LLM decode them into fluent jailbreak prompts, producing fast transferable attacks.
- [MOPO](https://arxiv.org/abs/2505.10892): Optimizes multiple preference objectives through constrained KL-regularized updates, making helpfulness and harmlessness trade-offs explicit during post-training.
- [One Shot Dominance: Knowledge Poisoning Attack on Retrieval-Augmented Generation Systems](https://arxiv.org/abs/2505.11548): Introduces AuthChain, a single-document knowledge-poisoning attack for RAG systems that keeps poisoned evidence retrievable and trusted even on multi-hop queries.
- [The Ripple Effect: On Unforeseen Complications of Backdoor Attacks](https://arxiv.org/abs/2505.11586): Shows backdoors in third-party pretrained language models can create unintended downstream complications beyond the intended trigger behavior.
- [JULI: Jailbreak Large Language Models by Self-Introspection](https://arxiv.org/abs/2505.11790): Contributes a agent safety model-side method, training recipe, architecture, verifier, reward, world-model, or synthetic-data technique.
- [Multilingual Collaborative Defense for Large Language Models](https://arxiv.org/abs/2505.11835): Defends LLMs through multilingual collaborative signals, extending safety mitigation beyond English-only guardrails and making cross-language attack transfer part of the model-side defense problem.
- [SafeVid](https://arxiv.org/abs/2505.11926): Builds video-specific safety alignment for large video multimodal models, combining safety data and training so dynamic visual contexts are covered rather than only static images.
- [Loss Reweighting for LLM Unlearning](https://arxiv.org/abs/2505.11953): Studies loss-reweighting criteria for stronger LLM unlearning, adding a model-side forgetting and safety-control recipe.
- [ACU: Analytic Continual Unlearning for Efficient and Exact Forgetting with Privacy Preservation](https://arxiv.org/abs/2505.12239): Provides analytic continual unlearning that forgets sequential requests without revisiting retained historical data, targeting efficient exact forgetting under privacy constraints.
- [CAPTURE / CaptureGuard](https://arxiv.org/abs/2505.12368): Trains and evaluates a context-aware prompt-injection guardrail model that balances attack detection against over-defense on benign in-context content.
- [Web IP at Risk: Prevent Unauthorized Real-Time Retrieval by Large Language Models](https://arxiv.org/abs/2505.12655): Studies defenses that prevent LLMs from retrieving protected web content in real time, making it a retrieval and content-protection item for Agent Harness review.
- [Language Models That Walk the Talk: A Framework for Formal Fairness Certificates](https://arxiv.org/abs/2505.12767): Formalizes fairness certificates for language models, adding a verifiable safety and alignment method.
- [Detecting Prefix Bias in LLM-based Reward Models](https://arxiv.org/abs/2505.13487): Introduces metrics and augmentation for detecting and reducing prefix-triggered demographic bias in reward models.
- [Metacognitive Activation Control](https://arxiv.org/abs/2505.13763): Uses a neurofeedback-style paradigm to test whether LLMs can report and steer their own activation patterns, connecting metacognition with both capability and monitor-evasion risk.
- [ShieldVLM: Safeguarding the Multimodal Implicit Toxicity via Deliberative Reasoning with LVLMs: ShieldVLM](https://arxiv.org/abs/2505.14035): Builds a taxonomy and 2,100-item MMIT dataset for multimodal implicit toxicity, then trains ShieldVLM with deliberative reasoning to detect cross-modal harms.
- [MoMoE: Mixture of Moderation Experts Framework for AI-Assisted Online Governance](https://arxiv.org/abs/2505.14483): Routes online-governance cases through a mixture of moderation experts, adding a moderation model architecture for harmful-content workflows.
- [Breaking Bad Tokens](https://arxiv.org/abs/2505.14536): Uses sparse autoencoders to detoxify LLM behavior, linking interpretable feature intervention with model-side safety control.
- [SEPS: A Separability Measure for Robust Unlearning in LLMs](https://arxiv.org/abs/2505.14832): Defines a separability measure for LLM unlearning, diagnosing whether forget targets can be removed without damaging retained knowledge.
- [DECASTE: Unveiling Caste Stereotypes in Large Language Models through Multi-Dimensional Bias Analysis](https://arxiv.org/abs/2505.14971): Measures caste stereotypes in LLMs across multiple bias dimensions, adding a fairness audit for culturally specific safety harms.
- [How Should We Enhance the Safety of Large Reasoning Models: An Empirical Study](https://arxiv.org/abs/2505.15404): Empirically studies supervised fine-tuning recipes for safer large reasoning models, finding that targeted safe-rationale distillation and simpler reasoning traces can improve safety without relying on long chain-of-thought.
- [Comprehensive Evaluation and Analysis for NSFW Concept Erasure in Text-to-Image Diffusion Models](https://arxiv.org/abs/2505.15450): Evaluates NSFW concept-erasure methods in text-to-image diffusion models, exposing residual unsafe generation and utility trade-offs after erasure.
- [Do RAG Systems Really Suffer From Positional Bias?](https://arxiv.org/abs/2505.15561): Tests real retrieval pipelines and finds top-ranked distracting passages often blunt apparent positional bias, tying RAG robustness to retrieval quality as well as prompt order.
- [FedSEA-LLaMA: A Secure, Efficient and Adaptive Federated Splitting Framework for Large Language Models](https://arxiv.org/abs/2505.15683): Splits LLaMA-style models in a secure, efficient, and adaptive federated framework.
- [Investigating Prosocial Behavior Theory in LLM Agents Under Policy-Induced Inequities](https://arxiv.org/abs/2505.15857): Introduces ProSim to simulate LLM agents' prosocial behavior under policy-induced inequities, measuring punishment, fairness responses, and suppressed prosociality.
- [SAE-SSV: Supervised Steering in Sparse Representation Spaces for Reliable Control of Language Models](https://arxiv.org/abs/2505.16188): Adds a model-side method, training recipe, architecture, inference strategy, or algorithmic capability for agent safety.
- [Three Minds, One Legend: Jailbreak Large Reasoning Model with Adaptive Stacked Ciphers](https://arxiv.org/abs/2505.16241): Attacks large reasoning models with adaptive stacked ciphers that exploit reasoning processes.
- [Training on Plausible Counterfactuals Removes Spurious Correlations](https://arxiv.org/abs/2505.16583): Trains on plausible counterfactual explanations to remove spurious correlations while keeping inputs on the data manifold, an adjacent robustness and bias-mitigation method.
- [BitHydra](https://arxiv.org/abs/2505.16670): Studies bit-flip inference-cost attacks against large language models, extending safety coverage to efficiency and resource-exhaustion risks.
- [Secure LLM Fine-Tuning via Safety-Aware Probing](https://arxiv.org/abs/2505.16737): Uses safety-aware probes during fine-tuning. Core idea: monitor safety-sensitive representations so adaptation can avoid silently degrading alignment.
- [When Safety Detectors Aren't Enough: A Stealthy and Effective Jailbreak Attack on LLMs via Steganographic Techniques](https://arxiv.org/abs/2505.16765): Embeds harmful queries inside fluent benign paragraphs with steganographic techniques, achieving both semantic and linguistic stealth against safety detectors.
- [Backdoor Cleaning without External Guidance in MLLM Fine-tuning](https://arxiv.org/abs/2505.16916): Adds a model-side method, training recipe, architecture, inference strategy, or algorithmic capability for agent safety.
- [Improving LLM Outputs Against Jailbreak Attacks With Expert Model Integration](https://arxiv.org/abs/2505.17066): Introduces Archias, a domain expert classifier for in-domain, out-of-domain, malicious, price-injection, and prompt-injection queries, used to harden production LLM outputs against evolving jailbreaks.
- [MTSA](https://arxiv.org/abs/2505.17147): Introduces Multi-turn Safety Alignment for LLMs through Multi-round Red-teaming, a model-side contribution for Agent Safety.
- [Harry Potter is Still Here! Probing Knowledge Leakage in Targeted Unlearned Large Language Models via Automated Adversarial Prompting](https://arxiv.org/abs/2505.17160): Introduces LURK, which automatically generates adversarial suffixes to elicit residual Harry Potter knowledge from supposedly unlearned LLMs.
- [Refusal Direction is Universal Across Safety-Aligned Languages](https://arxiv.org/abs/2505.17306): Shows a refusal vector extracted in one safety-aligned language transfers across 14 languages in PolyRefuse, explaining cross-lingual jailbreaks through parallel refusal directions in activation space.
- [VEAttack](https://arxiv.org/abs/2505.17440): Attacks vision encoders used by large vision-language models in a downstream-agnostic way, exposing a reusable multimodal safety risk.
- [Chain-of-Lure: A Synthetic Narrative-Driven Approach to Compromise Large Language Models](https://arxiv.org/abs/2505.17519): Uses unconstrained synthetic narratives to lure LLMs into harmful compliance, creating a universal jailbreak framework that avoids hand-written attack templates.
- [One Model Transfer to All: On Robust Jailbreak Prompts Generation against LLMs](https://arxiv.org/abs/2505.17598): Generates robust jailbreak prompts transferable across LLMs with a robustness-judgment model.
- [Chain-of-Thought and Jailbreak Harmfulness](https://arxiv.org/abs/2505.17650): Tests whether chain-of-thought reasoning reduces harmfulness under jailbreaking, clarifying a safety boundary of reasoning-style models.
- [Understanding How Value Neurons Shape the Generation of Specified Values in LLMs](https://arxiv.org/abs/2505.17712): Locates value-related neurons to interpret how LLMs generate specified values.
- [Redirection for Erasing Memory (REM): Towards a universal unlearning method for corrupted data](https://arxiv.org/abs/2505.17730): Redirects corrupted-data influence to newly introduced neurons that can be discarded or deactivated, giving a broader vision-classifier unlearning method.
- [Understanding and Mitigating Overrefusal in LLMs from an Unveiling Perspective of Safety Decision Boundary](https://arxiv.org/abs/2505.18325): Analyzes overrefusal through safety decision boundaries and proposes mitigation directions.
- [An Attack to Break Permutation-Based Private Third-Party Inference Schemes for LLMs](https://arxiv.org/abs/2505.18332): Recovers original prompts from permuted hidden states in private third-party LLM inference schemes, breaking obfuscation claims with near-perfect reconstruction.
- [GainRAG: Preference Alignment in Retrieval-Augmented Generation through Gain Signal Synthesis](https://arxiv.org/abs/2505.18710): Defines a gain signal measuring whether retrieved passages improve answer correctness, aligning retriever preferences with the generator in RAG.
- [Soft Weighted Machine Unlearning](https://arxiv.org/abs/2505.18783): Uses analytically solved weighted influence functions to soften unlearning updates, reducing over-unlearning in fairness and robustness settings.
- [Leveraging Per-Instance Privacy for Machine Unlearning](https://arxiv.org/abs/2505.18786): Replaces worst-case unlearning privacy bounds with per-instance privacy losses, predicting which samples are harder to forget during fine-tuning.
- [An Embarrassingly Simple Defense Against LLM Abliteration Attacks](https://arxiv.org/abs/2505.19056): Defends against abliteration attacks that erase refusal behavior, restoring safety responses with a simple model-side intervention.
- [Delving into Multilingual Ethical Bias: The MSQAD with Statistical Hypothesis Tests for Large Language Models](https://arxiv.org/abs/2505.19121): Builds multilingual sensitive question-answer data from Human Rights Watch topics and applies statistical tests to measure cross-language ethical bias.
- [Preference Optimization by Estimating the Ratio of the Data Distribution](https://arxiv.org/abs/2505.19601): Reinterprets DPO through data-distribution ratios and estimates those ratios to improve preference optimization under distribution mismatch.
- [JailBound: Jailbreaking Internal Safety Boundaries of Vision-Language Models](https://arxiv.org/abs/2505.19610): Targets internal VLM safety boundaries to craft jailbreaks that exploit the enlarged attack surface introduced by vision encoders.
- [Diagnosing and Mitigating Modality Interference in Multimodal Large Language Models](https://arxiv.org/abs/2505.19616): Diagnoses spurious cross-modal signals and mitigates them with perturbation-based augmentation plus consistency regularization.
- [Audio-Language Safety Representation Reshaping](https://arxiv.org/abs/2505.19670): Reshapes representation space to balance safety and over-rejection in large audio language models.
- [Editing as Unlearning](https://arxiv.org/abs/2505.19855): Recasts LLM unlearning as knowledge editing toward refusal or empty responses and compares editing methods as practical unlearning baselines.
- [Safety Through Reasoning: An Empirical Study of Reasoning Guardrail Models](https://arxiv.org/abs/2505.20087): Empirically studies reasoning guardrail models, clarifying when explicit reasoning helps safety filtering and when it creates new failure modes.
- [TrojanStego: Your Language Model Can Secretly Be A Steganographic Privacy Leaking Agent](https://arxiv.org/abs/2505.20118): Fine-tunes LLMs to encode sensitive context into natural-looking outputs through vocabulary-partition steganography, creating a passive covert exfiltration channel without attacker control at inference time.
- [Shutdownable Agents through POST-Agency](https://arxiv.org/abs/2505.20203): Studies shutdownable agent design through POST-agency, adding a model-side control objective for ensuring agents can be interrupted or stopped safely.
- [Ra-DPO](https://arxiv.org/abs/2505.20359): adds risk-aware constraints to direct preference optimization, making alignment training sensitive to tail-risk and reference-model deviation.
- [Divide-Then-Align: Honest Alignment based on the Knowledge Boundary of RAG](https://arxiv.org/abs/2505.20871): Separates RAG cases by knowledge-boundary status and aligns responses to answer grounded queries while avoiding unsupported or misleading claims.
- [Towards Safety Reasoning in LLMs: AI-agentic Deliberation for Policy-embedded CoT Data Creation](https://arxiv.org/abs/2505.21784): Generates policy-embedded chain-of-thought data with multi-agent deliberation and a refiner, supporting safety-reasoning SFT and preference alignment.
- [Precise In-Parameter Concept Erasure](https://arxiv.org/abs/2505.22586): Erases concepts inside LLM parameters, adding a model-side unlearning and safety-control method.
- [Understanding (Un)Reliability of Steering Vectors](https://arxiv.org/abs/2505.22637): Audits when activation steering helps or backfires, making steering reliability a model-side safety-control issue rather than a fixed assumption.
- [Does Machine Unlearning Truly Remove Knowledge?](https://arxiv.org/abs/2505.23270): Audits whether unlearning actually removes target knowledge, adding a safety-relevant reliability check for model-side forgetting methods.
- [Understanding Refusal with Sparse Autoencoders](https://arxiv.org/abs/2505.23556): Uses sparse autoencoders to analyze refusal circuits in language models, adding a model-side interpretability route for safety alignment.
- [Distortion of AI Alignment: Does Preference Optimization Optimize for Preferences?](https://arxiv.org/abs/2505.23749): Defines preference-optimization distortion from social choice theory, showing how RLHF and DPO can miss pluralistic average-utility objectives.
- [OMNIGUARD](https://arxiv.org/abs/2505.23856): Introduces an efficient cross-modal AI safety moderation approach, adding a unified multimodal moderation model.
- [Light as Deception: GPT-driven Natural Relighting Against Vision-Language Pre-training Models](https://arxiv.org/abs/2505.24227): Uses ChatGPT-proposed lighting parameters, IC-light relighting, and gradient optimization to craft natural adversarial images for vision-language pretraining models.
- [AMIA](https://arxiv.org/abs/2505.24519): Combines automatic masking with intention analysis to make LVLMs more robust against jailbreaks.
- [K-Steering](https://arxiv.org/abs/2505.24535): Uses a nonlinear multi-label activation classifier to control multiple LLM behavioral attributes without storing separate linear steering vectors.
- [TRIDENT: Enhancing Large Language Model Safety with Tri-Dimensional Diversified Red-Teaming Data Synthesis](https://arxiv.org/abs/2505.24672): Adds a model-side method, training recipe, architecture, or data method for agent safety capability coverage.
- [On Symmetric Losses for Robust Policy Optimization with Noisy Preferences](https://arxiv.org/abs/2505.24709): Treats reward modeling and offline preference optimization as noisy classification problems, using symmetric losses to make RLHF-style alignment more robust to erroneous or biased preference labels.
- [Disentangled Safety Adapters Enable Efficient Guardrails and Flexible Inference-Time Alignment](https://arxiv.org/abs/2506.00166): Builds disentangled safety adapters for flexible inference-time alignment.
- [Keeping an Eye on LLM Unlearning](https://arxiv.org/abs/2506.00359): Analyzes hidden risk and remedies in LLM unlearning, adding a safety-focused model-side unlearning diagnostic.
- [Goal-Aware Identification and Rectification of Misinformation in Multi-Agent Systems](https://arxiv.org/abs/2506.00509): Introduces MisinfoTask and ARGUS, a two-stage training-free defense that uses goal-aware reasoning to locate and rectify misinformation as it propagates through multi-agent information flows.
- [Jailbreak-R1](https://arxiv.org/abs/2506.00782): Explores jailbreak capabilities of LLMs through reinforcement learning.
- [Speech Unlearning](https://arxiv.org/abs/2506.00848): Introduces or analyzes a model-side method for agent safety, alignment, privacy, or provenance, adding training, architecture, alignment, or generation coverage.
- [Doubly Robust Alignment for Large Language Models](https://arxiv.org/abs/2506.01183): Introduces doubly robust preference optimization for RLHF, keeping alignment updates consistent when either the preference model or reference policy is correctly specified under model misspecification.
- [Detoxification of Large Language Models through Output-layer Fusion with a Calibration Model](https://arxiv.org/abs/2506.01266): Fuses a compact calibration model into the LLM output layer, steering generation through a detoxified embedding space trained once on non-toxic data and reusable across target LLMs.
- [Unlearning Blind Spots](https://arxiv.org/abs/2506.01318): Studies over-unlearning and prototypical relearning attacks, clarifying failure modes in LLM unlearning.
- [Invariance Makes LLM Unlearning Resilient](https://arxiv.org/abs/2506.01339): Studies unlearning that remains robust after downstream fine-tuning. Core idea: enforce invariance so forgotten content is less likely to reappear when models are later adapted.
- [Enhancing Diffusion-based Unrestricted Adversarial Attacks via Adversary Preferences Alignment](https://arxiv.org/abs/2506.01511): Frames unrestricted adversarial example generation as preference alignment for diffusion models, decoupling visual consistency and attack effectiveness with differentiable rewards.
- [DRAUN: An Algorithm-Agnostic Data Reconstruction Attack on Federated Unlearning Systems](https://arxiv.org/abs/2506.01777): Reconstructs data requested for removal from federated-unlearning updates, exposing a privacy attack surface created by client-driven deletion workflows.
- [Something Just Like TRuST : Toxicity Recognition of Span and Target](https://arxiv.org/abs/2506.02326): Contributes a model-side method relevant to agent and model safety.
- [Beyond Invisibility: Learning Robust Visible Watermarks for Stronger Copyright Protection](https://arxiv.org/abs/2506.02665): Learns hard-to-remove visible image watermarks for copyright protection, aiming for robustness across changing generative-model architectures rather than model-specific invisible perturbations.
- [Privacy Leaks by Adversaries: Adversarial Iterations for Membership Inference Attack](https://arxiv.org/abs/2506.02711): Analyzes an adversarial risk through Adversarial Iterations for Membership Inference Attack, focusing on the attack surface, transfer path, or defense failure mode.
- [Causal Estimation of Tokenisation Bias](https://arxiv.org/abs/2506.03149): Defines tokenisation bias as the change in string probability caused by tokenizer vocabulary choices and estimates that causal effect across alternative subword segmentations.
- [CARE: Enhancing Safety of Visual Navigation through Collision Avoidance via Repulsive Estimation](https://arxiv.org/abs/2506.03834): Applies CARE to safety-critical robot, vehicle, or traffic decisions, focusing on violation prediction and risk-aware control.
- [SafeSteer: Interpretable Safety Steering with Refusal-Evasion in LLMs](https://arxiv.org/abs/2506.04250): Contributes a safety steering or alignment method; core idea: improve refusal or risk control while preserving useful model behavior.
- [UNO: Unlearning via Orthogonalization in Generative models](https://arxiv.org/abs/2506.04712): Orthogonalizes unwanted-data influence in generative models so unlearning can remove target examples while preserving generation quality and retained-data effects.
- [RIVAL: Reinforcement Learning with Iterative and Adversarial Optimization for Machine Translation](https://arxiv.org/abs/2506.05070): Casts reward-model and translation-model updates as an adversarial min-max loop to reduce reward-model drift during RLHF-style subtitle translation.
- [Dissecting Bias in LLMs](https://arxiv.org/abs/2506.05166): Studies LLM bias through mechanistic interpretability. Core idea: locate internal circuits or representations associated with biased behavior so mitigation can target model mechanisms rather than only outputs.
- [Why LLM Safety Guardrails Collapse After Fine-tuning: A Similarity Analysis Between Alignment and Fine-tuning Datasets](https://arxiv.org/abs/2506.05346): Analyzes why LLM safety guardrails can collapse after fine-tuning by comparing alignment and adaptation data, clarifying fine-tuning drift risk.
- [Beyond RLHF and NLHF: Population-Proportional Alignment under an Axiomatic Framework](https://arxiv.org/abs/2506.05619): Introduces or analyzes a model-side method for agent safety, alignment, privacy, or provenance, adding training, architecture, alignment, or generation coverage.
- [FedShield-LLM: A Secure and Scalable Federated Fine-Tuned Large Language Model](https://arxiv.org/abs/2506.05640): Combines federated LLM fine-tuning with privacy-preserving mechanisms so cross-silo task adaptation can reduce sensitive-data exposure without centralizing client data.
- [Optimization-Free Universal Watermark Forgery with Regenerative Diffusion Models](https://arxiv.org/abs/2506.06018): Introduces Plug-and-Plant, an optimization-free diffusion regeneration attack that transfers a target image watermark onto cover images without knowing the watermarking scheme.
- [Token-Only Dataset Inference](https://arxiv.org/abs/2506.06057): Studies dataset-inference attacks against large language models using only token-level access, adding a privacy-risk diagnostic.
- [Simple Yet Effective: Extracting Private Data Across Clients in Federated Fine-Tuning of Large Language Models](https://arxiv.org/abs/2506.06060): Contributes a model-side method relevant to agent and model safety.
- [Distillation Robustifies Unlearning](https://arxiv.org/abs/2506.06278): Uses distillation to make unlearning more robust. Core idea: transfer safe post-unlearning behavior into a student model so erased content is less likely to return under perturbations.
- [Pairwise Calibrated Rewards for Pluralistic Alignment](https://arxiv.org/abs/2506.06298): Calibrates pairwise reward comparisons for pluralistic alignment so preference optimization can represent heterogeneous human values instead of collapsing them into a single majority signal.
- [Prompt-Injection Detection with Pre-trained Models and Heuristics](https://arxiv.org/abs/2506.06384): Combines a pre-trained detector with heuristic features for prompt-injection defense, making it a model-side guardrail candidate for LLM and agent safety.
- [Backdoor Attack on Vision Language Models with Stealthy Semantic Manipulation](https://arxiv.org/abs/2506.07214): Introduces semantic-manipulation backdoors against VLMs through poisoned image-text pairings.
- [When Style Breaks Safety](https://arxiv.org/abs/2506.07452): Studies superficial style alignment as a safety failure mode and proposes defenses against style-driven safety degradation.
- [Chasing Moving Targets with Online Self-Play Reinforcement Learning for Safer Language Models](https://arxiv.org/abs/2506.07468): Trains a single model in alternating attacker and defender roles with online self-play RL, letting red-team prompts and safety responses co-evolve instead of patching a static model.
- [BLUR](https://arxiv.org/abs/2506.08164): Formulates LLM unlearning as bi-level optimization so forgetting and retaining objectives are separated instead of merged into one fragile loss.
- [Reinforcement Learning from Human Feedback with High-Confidence Safety Constraints](https://arxiv.org/abs/2506.08266): Adds high-confidence safety constraints to RLHF, making safety part of preference optimization.
- [SafeCoT: Improving VLM Safety with Minimal Reasoning](https://arxiv.org/abs/2506.08399): A model, training, or algorithmic contribution for agent safety and security evaluation. Core idea: focus on improving vlm safety with minimal reasoning as a capability-relevant model reference.
- [DAVSP](https://arxiv.org/abs/2506.09353): Aligns large vision-language models against visual-modality attacks by learning deep aligned visual safety prompts while preserving benign utility.
- [Apollo: A Posteriori Label-Only Membership Inference Attack Towards Machine Unlearning](https://arxiv.org/abs/2506.09923): Uses post-unlearning label-only outputs for membership inference, testing whether deletion procedures still leak whether a forgotten example was in training.
- [From Threat to Tool: Leveraging Refusal-Aware Injection Attacks for Safety Alignment](https://arxiv.org/abs/2506.10020): Repurposes refusal-aware adaptive injection to elicit harmful completions, then uses those attack-generated samples as synthetic safety-alignment data.
- [Towards Understanding Bias in Synthetic Data for Evaluation](https://arxiv.org/abs/2506.10301): Audits LLM-generated synthetic information-retrieval test collections, measuring how generated queries and relevance judgments can bias evaluation outcomes.
- [SOFT: Selective Data Obfuscation for Protecting LLM Fine-tuning against Membership Inference Attacks](https://arxiv.org/abs/2506.10424): Obfuscates selected fine-tuning examples before LLM adaptation to reduce membership-inference leakage while preserving downstream utility.
- [Surface Fairness, Deep Bias: A Comparative Study of Bias in Language Models](https://arxiv.org/abs/2506.10491): Introduces or analyzes a model-side method for agent safety, security, privacy, or robustness, adding training, architecture, alignment, verifier, synthetic-data, or generation coverage.
- [ME: Trigger Element Combination Backdoor Attack on Copyright Infringement](https://arxiv.org/abs/2506.10776): Contributes a agent safety model-side method, training recipe, architecture, verifier, reward, world-model, or synthetic-data technique.
- [Interpretability-Based Fairness Steering](https://arxiv.org/abs/2506.10922): Identifies and neutralizes sensitive-attribute directions in activations so fairness mitigations survive realistic contextual details rather than only controlled prompts.
- [GUARD: Guided Unlearning and Retention via Data Attribution for Large Language Models](https://arxiv.org/abs/2506.10946): Uses data attribution to guide unlearning while retaining non-target capabilities in LLMs.
- [Who is in the Spotlight: The Hidden Bias Undermining Multimodal Retrieval-Augmented Generation](https://arxiv.org/abs/2506.11063): Examines spotlight bias in multimodal RAG, where retrieval and generation overrepresent salient people or entities and distort grounded answers.
- [Model Organisms for Emergent Misalignment](https://arxiv.org/abs/2506.11613): Introduces model organisms for studying emergent misalignment.
- [Convergent Linear Representations of Emergent Misalignment](https://arxiv.org/abs/2506.11618): Finds transferable misalignment directions across fine-tuned models and uses representation interventions to ablate misaligned behavior.
- [Revealing Political Bias in LLMs through Structured Multi-Agent Debate](https://arxiv.org/abs/2506.11825): Introduces a model-side method, training recipe, architecture, verifier, reward model, or data-generation route for agent safety.
- [Uncovering Bias Paths with LLM-guided Causal Discovery: An Active Learning and Dynamic Scoring Approach](https://arxiv.org/abs/2506.12227): Introduces a model-side method, training signal, architecture, or adaptation strategy for Agent Safety.
- [EgoPrivacy: What Your First-Person Camera Says About You?](https://arxiv.org/abs/2506.12258): Introduces an egocentric-video privacy benchmark covering demographic, individual, and situational inference tasks about the camera wearer.
- [InfoFlood: Jailbreaking Large Language Models with Information Overload](https://arxiv.org/abs/2506.12274): Attacks LLM safeguards by overloading prompts with excessive information, testing whether long noisy contexts can push models past refusal behavior.
- [Robust LLM Unlearning with MUDMAN: Meta-Unlearning with Disruption Masking And Normalization](https://arxiv.org/abs/2506.12484): Uses meta-unlearning with disruption masking and normalization to make LLM forgetting less brittle under perturbations and downstream adaptation.
- [When Forgetting Triggers Backdoors](https://arxiv.org/abs/2506.12522): Shows that clean unlearning can trigger backdoor behavior, adding a model-side safety risk for forgetting and unlearning pipelines.
- [MEraser LLM Fingerprint Erasure](https://arxiv.org/abs/2506.12551): Erases large-language-model fingerprints while preserving utility, extending model-provenance and ownership-attack coverage.
- [Attention Hijackers](https://arxiv.org/abs/2506.12880): Explains universal jailbreak suffixes as attention hijacking, adding a model-internal view of jailbreak transferability.
- [Jailbreak Transferability Emerges from Shared Representations](https://arxiv.org/abs/2506.12913): Explains jailbreak transfer through shared representations, adding a model-internal account of cross-model safety failures.
- [Rectifying Privacy and Efficacy Measurements in Machine Unlearning: A New Inference Attack Perspective](https://arxiv.org/abs/2506.13009): Critiques average-case unlearning audits and proposes inference-attack measurements that better test privacy and efficacy on targeted forgotten samples.
- [CertDW](https://arxiv.org/abs/2506.13160): applies conformal prediction to certified dataset ownership verification, adding a data-provenance safety mechanism.
- [Unlearning Isn't Invisible](https://arxiv.org/abs/2506.14003): Detects traces of LLM unlearning from model outputs. Core idea: test whether unlearning leaves behavioral fingerprints that downstream auditors or attackers can observe.
- [GRAM: A Generative Foundation Reward Model for Reward Generalization](https://arxiv.org/abs/2506.14175): Trains a generative reward model first on unlabeled data and then on preference labels, using label smoothing as a regularized pairwise-ranking loss to generalize across response ranking, RLHF, and task adaptation.
- [RL-Obfuscation](https://arxiv.org/abs/2506.14261): Fine-tunes models with reinforcement learning to evade latent-space monitors while preserving black-box behavior, making monitor evasion itself a model-side safety risk.
- [LingoLoop Attack: Trapping MLLMs via Linguistic Context and State Entrapment into Endless Loops](https://arxiv.org/abs/2506.14493): Induces MLLMs to generate excessive repetitive output by exploiting POS-level EOS behavior and sentence-structure patterns, creating a resource-exhaustion attack.
